Three residuals are disclosed under HAP-001-R19 rather than closed: the created directory is not relative to the outbox handle (no `mkdirat`); the by-handle identity comparison and the link count are not read, because `std::os::windows::fs::MetadataExt`'s by-handle accessors are unstable (`windows_by_handle`) and no Windows API crate was added — so `outbox-linked` cannot be produced on Windows, and "verify by handle" there means only that the declared path opened as a real directory that was not a reparse point; owner-only permissions are not expressed (ACLs are outside `std`). The unix tests that need a symbolic link, a hard link, or a FIFO are gated per test with the reason stated.
- **D6 — the shell** (`src-tauri/src/outbox_state.rs`, `src-tauri/src/ipc/{commands,dto}.rs`). `harness_spawn`'s `kind: "adapter"` mints a run id (`run-<seconds>-<nanoseconds>-<sequence>`, the sequence a process-wide atomic rather than any table's counter, so two ids minted in one process differ even at identical seconds and nanoseconds — Windows's `SystemTime` has 100 ns resolution and no sub-second guarantee, which is how CI once minted two identical ids), and on the blocking thread loads the policy, prepares the run subdirectory, declares it on the plan, and only then spawns — `launch_with_run_subdirectory`, one plain function (prepare, declare, spawn) the tests drive with a fake spawn; a policy that cannot be loaded, an invalid declaration, a pre-planted path, or a failed verification refuses the launch with `outbox-unavailable` before anything is spawned (HAP-001-R10, D14), and if the declaration or the spawn fails the empty subdirectory just created is removed again — only while still empty: `remove_dir` refuses a non-empty directory, so nothing a same-user process dropped in after creation is ever deleted (both failure branches and the non-empty case are tested). The run's record — the prepared subdirectory with its held handle, the policy in force, the proposals — lives in a bounded `RunTable` (64 records, oldest evicted, its handles released; the entries stay in the outbox for the next whole-outbox inventory). The forwarder's `AdapterWireSequencer` carries a `RunEndTracker`: every `ArtifactPublish` event passing through is recorded — up to `MAX_PROPOSED_ENTRIES` (1024) proposed entries per run, a spike default HAP-001 does not name: a proposal crossing the cap keeps its first entries up to the remaining slots, and every entry beyond is counted, never silently dropped — and the raw `State` frame produces the tracker's run-end events *before* the terminal `state` frame — one `diagnostic` for the counted excess when there is one, then one `diagnostic` per recorded proposal naming a digest no entry carries (name and short digest only), then one `candidates` summary — so `state` stays the last frame and `seq` stays contiguous, with the raw frame's `droppedBefore` on the first of them. The inventory runs on a duplicate of the held handle outside the table's lock, and the candidates are stored on the record through `RunTable::store_candidates`, which is where HAP-001 D22's cap is enforced — **per active workspace within a shell session**: at most 32 handles held across every remembered run of the workspace, counted under the table's lock against every other run's held handles, the excess released in inventory order while the entries stay `candidate` (a second run ending after a first that holds 20 keeps 12); a record evicted from the bounded table (64) releases its handles with it, and `workspace_pick` of a *different* workspace clears the whole table (`OutboxState::forget_runs`, through the pure `activate_workspace`), so a previous project's runs never count against the next one's budget, while re-picking the active workspace keeps them. Nothing else holds a candidate handle, so 32 is the whole-shell bound. The inventory's two phases run under two lock scopes (`RunEndTracker::snapshot`, then the hashing outside the lock, then `RunTable::store_candidates`); if the record was evicted or forgotten in between, the table hands the candidates back, their handles are released explicitly, and one `diagnostic` names the run id and how many candidates were not retained — the loss is never silent. A PTY launch has no line parser and therefore no proposals: its inventory is entirely unattributed, as HAP-001 § Run subdirectories and attribution says. `outbox_status` and `candidates_list` are the two new commands; `ShellErrorCode` gains `outbox-invalid` and `outbox-unavailable`.
- **D7 — the fixture** (`crates/omnifrons-supervisor/src/bin/fake-agent.rs`). `--report-output-dir` (`output-dir set=<bool> writable=<bool>` then `env-keys ...`, names only), `--write-outbox <n>` (n heavy files cycling PDF, PNG, ZIP by magic, plus `notes.md` and an ELF-headed `tool.bin`, then one `artifact.publish` `tool_use` naming every even-indexed heavy file by digest), `--propose-missing` (adds `ghost.pdf`, never written), `--plant-link` (`outside.bin` at the outbox root, then `escape-link` and `linked.bin` inside the run subdirectory). The digests come from a minimal hand-rolled SHA-256 inside the fixture — pinned to FIPS 180-4's test vectors in its own unit tests and checked against the real hasher in `tests/outbox_launch.rs` — so the supervisor's normal dependency set stays unchanged, the same reasoning as slice 3's hand-built JSON; `sha2` joins the supervisor's `[dev-dependencies]` only for that check.

### IPC shapes

Two new commands, registered next to the existing ones:

| Command | Params | Returns |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `outbox_status` | *(none)* | `OutboxStatusDto` |
| `candidates_list` | `{ runId?: string }` | `CandidateDto[]` |

```jsonc
// outbox_status, valid and existing. `outbox` is the third explicit exception to
// RCS-001-R14's no-raw-path rule (alongside EvidenceDto.canonicalPath and
// WorkspaceDto.displayPath): identity evidence for display -- where a run's output
// lands -- never a reference the renderer sends back, and null whenever the
// declaration is not valid or the directory does not exist. `declared` and
// `policyPath` are project-relative names, not device paths.
// `assetRootId` joined in slice 5b: the policy's asset root identity token -- the destination
// HAP-001-R22 shows before the decision -- never a path, null when the policy declares none
// or could not be loaded.
{"declared": ".omnifrons/outbox", "outbox": "/home/user/project/.omnifrons/outbox",
 "exists": true, "state": "valid", "reason": null, "policyPath": ".omnifrons/asset-policy.json",
 "assetRootId": "main"}
// a fresh project: valid, not created yet (the first adapter launch creates it); the default
// policy declares no asset root
{"declared": ".omnifrons/outbox", "outbox": null, "exists": false, "state": "valid",
 "reason": null, "policyPath": ".omnifrons/asset-policy.json", "assetRootId": null}
// the declared path is a link: state outbox-invalid, reason one of outside-project | link |
// not-a-directory | unreadable | policy-unreadable | policy-corrupt | policy-invalid
{"declared": "elsewhere/outbox", "outbox": null, "exists": true, "state": "outbox-invalid",
 "reason": "link", "policyPath": ".omnifrons/asset-policy.json", "assetRootId": "main"}
// a corrupt policy: nothing is declared, nothing is shown
{"declared": null, "outbox": null, "exists": false, "state": "outbox-invalid",
 "reason": "policy-corrupt", "policyPath": ".omnifrons/asset-policy.json", "assetRootId": null}

// candidates_list { runId: "run-…" } -- the inventory taken at that run's end; the
// name is relative to the outbox (a producer-supplied name: plain text only), the
// facts come from the entry's handle, and a refused entry carries none of them.
// `sha256` (the full 64-hex digest beside `sha256Short`) joined the row in slice 5b so
// `artifact_approve` can be called from the row itself; identity evidence, not a secret,
// under the same TM-001-R7 precedent as EvidenceDto.sha256 -- and never a device path
{"name": "run-1725782401-000000001-0/report.pdf", "size": 4096,
 "sha256": "abababababababababababababababababababababababababababababababab", "sha256Short": "abababab",
 "detectedType": "pdf", "class": "generated-heavy",
 "attribution": {"kind": "run", "runId": "run-1725782401-000000001-0"}, "state": "candidate"}
{"name": "run-1725782401-000000001-0/stray.png", "size": 8,
 "sha256": "1111111111111111111111111111111111111111111111111111111111111111", "sha256Short": "11111111",
 "detectedType": "png", "class": "generated-heavy",
 "attribution": {"kind": "unattributed"}, "state": "candidate"}
{"name": "run-1725782401-000000001-0/linked.bin", "size": null, "sha256": null, "sha256Short": null,
 "detectedType": null, "class": null, "attribution": {"kind": "unattributed"}, "state": "outbox-linked"}
{"name": "run-1725782401-000000001-0/escape-link", "size": null, "sha256": null, "sha256Short": null,
 "detectedType": null, "class": null, "attribution": {"kind": "unattributed"}, "state": "outbox-escape"}
// candidates_list {} -- the whole outbox, root entries and one level down, all
// unattributed (a proposal only); an outbox that does not exist yet is []
{"name": "dropped.pdf", "size": 15,
 "sha256": "9d3f2a109d3f2a109d3f2a109d3f2a109d3f2a109d3f2a109d3f2a109d3f2a10", "sha256Short": "9d3f2a10",
 "detectedType": "pdf", "class": "generated-heavy", "attribution": {"kind": "unattributed"}, "state": "candidate"}

// the typed proposal a line agent recognized (proposed AEC-001 kind
// artifact-publish): the harness's own claim, entries by full digest, a proposal only
{"stream": "event", "body": {"id": 42, "seq": 6, "droppedBefore": 0,
  "kind": "artifact-publish", "payload": {"entries": [{"name": "report.pdf",
  "sha256": "abababababababababababababababababababababababababababababababab"}]}}}

// emitted once per adapter launch at run end, before the terminal state frame:
// counts only, never a path. Preceded, when there is something to say, by
// diagnostics: one for proposed entries counted beyond the per-run cap of 1024,
// then one per recorded proposal naming a digest no entry carries (the proposed
// name and the short digest only)
{"stream": "event", "body": {"id": 42, "seq": 7, "droppedBefore": 0,
  "kind": "diagnostic", "payload": {"text": "artifact.publish named 3 entries beyond the 1024 recorded for this run (the cap is 1024 per run); they were not recorded and took no part in attribution"}}}
{"stream": "event", "body": {"id": 42, "seq": 8, "droppedBefore": 0,
  "kind": "diagnostic", "payload": {"text": "artifact.publish names ghost.pdf by a digest not found in the run subdirectory (77777777)"}}}
{"stream": "event", "body": {"id": 42, "seq": 9, "droppedBefore": 0,
  "kind": "candidates", "payload": {"runId": "run-1725782401-000000001-0", "total": 5,
  "candidate": 3, "outboxEscape": 1, "outboxLinked": 1, "attributed": 2, "unattributed": 3,
  "unreadable": 0, "unmatchedProposals": 1}}}
// only if the run's record was evicted or forgotten while its inventory ran: the
// loss is reported (the run id and a count), never silent
{"stream": "event", "body": {"id": 42, "seq": 10, "droppedBefore": 0,
  "kind": "diagnostic", "payload": {"text": "the record of run run-1725782401-000000001-0 was no longer remembered when its inventory completed; 3 candidates were not retained and their handles were released"}}}
{"stream": "state", "body": {"id": 42, "seq": 11, "droppedBefore": 0, "state": "exited", "code": 0}}

// ShellError for an adapter launch whose run subdirectory could not be prepared
// (nothing was spawned); the message names the step class, never a path
{"code": "outbox-unavailable", "message": "something already sits at the run subdirectory path; remove it and relaunch"}
// ShellError for candidates_list {} when the policy cannot be loaded or the declared
// outbox resolves outside the project or is a link (ingestion blocked)
{"code": "outbox-invalid", "message": "the classification policy could not be loaded"}
```

`ShellErrorCode` gains exactly two values, `outbox-invalid` and `outbox-unavailable`; `AdapterEventDto` gains exactly two kinds, `artifact-publish` and `candidates`; `candidates_list` with a `runId` that names no remembered run, or a run that has not ended, is `invalid-request`; `outbox_status` and a whole-outbox `candidates_list` with no active workspace are `workspace-unavailable`. Every fixed message is slash-free, and no payload of this slice carries a device path except `outbox_status.outbox` under the exception stated above.

The policy file (`.omnifrons/asset-policy.json`), for the renderer implementer and for anyone writing one by hand:

```json
{"schema": 1, "outbox": ".omnifrons/outbox",
 "rows": [{"types": ["plain-text"], "extensions": ["csv"], "maxSize": 1048576, "class": "generated-heavy"}]}
```

`types` is a list of `DetectedType` tokens (empty matches every type), `extensions` lowercase without the dot (empty matches every name), `maxSize` inclusive, `class` an `ArtifactClass` token; the project's rows precede the shipped defaults and the two fixed rules precede both. The file is read through a 1 MiB bound: a larger file is refused unparsed (`outbox_status` reason `policy-invalid`, `candidates_list` error `outbox-invalid`).

### Evidence procedures

#### Procedure notes for VP-S22

[HAP-001's proposed VP-S22](heavy-asset-publication.md#desktop-stack-verification-planmd) (outbox publication end to end) cannot run against this slice — nothing publishes — but its first half now has a dev-mode analogue worth carrying into the procedure once a packaged build exists: launch an adapter whose executable is `fake-agent --write-outbox 3`, confirm the `artifact-publish` event names `artifact-0.pdf` and `artifact-2.zip` by digest, and confirm the `candidates` event at run end reports two attributed and three unattributed entries (`artifact-1.png`, `notes.md`, `tool.bin`) with `candidates_list { runId }` showing `notes.md` as `portable-text` and `tool.bin` as `executable`, never `generated-heavy`. The digest in every `candidate` row must equal the digest of the file's bytes read independently; the record digest equality VP-S22 asks for is then the same fact one step later.

#### Procedure notes for VP-S26

[VP-S26](heavy-asset-publication.md#desktop-stack-verification-planmd) is this slice's direct analogue. Per platform, a packaged-build run must show: the child's observed environment is the base allowlist plus `OMNIFRONS_OUTPUT_DIR` and nothing else, with the key naming a directory that exists before spawn, is owner-only (`stat -c %a` reads `700` on Linux and macOS), and sits under the project's outbox; a value planted under the same key in the launching process's environment never reaches the child (the dev-mode analogue is `declared_output_dir_reaches_the_child_and_a_planted_parent_value_never_wins`); the working directory is unchanged; a symbolic link, then a regular file, pre-planted at the next run subdirectory's path refuses the launch with `outbox-unavailable` and leaves the planted object in place (`crates/omnifrons-app/src/contract/run_outbox.rs` is the dev-mode analogue, at the preparer rather than through `harness_spawn`, which this slice's tests cannot drive); a declared outbox that is a link shows `outbox-invalid` with reason `link` in `outbox_status` and refuses the launch. The product work area cases of VP-S26 (a misconfigured work area, a mid-session registration over it) have no implementation to test yet — 5b. On Windows the procedure must record the D5 residuals above as residuals, never as a pass: no `outbox-linked` can be observed there, and the identity comparison is not performed.

#### Procedure notes for VP-S28

[VP-S28](heavy-asset-publication.md#desktop-stack-verification-planmd)'s first steps are exercisable now: with the product closed, write a PDF into the outbox root and a second into an existing run subdirectory, plant a symbolic link in the outbox, then open the project and call `candidates_list {}` — both PDFs must appear as `candidate` with `attribution: unattributed`, the second named `<run id>/<file>` (the run subdirectory as a location fact in the name, never provenance), and the link as `outbox-escape` with no digest facts, having never been dereferenced (`whole_outbox_inventory_lists_root_and_run_entries_unattributed` and `inventory_never_dereferences_a_planted_link_and_catches_a_hard_link` are the dev-mode analogues). A synchronized policy naming a raw path (`"outbox": "/var/tmp/outbox"`) must make `outbox_status` report `outbox-invalid` with reason `policy-invalid` and `candidates_list {}` fail `outbox-invalid` (`a_raw_path_declared_as_the_outbox_is_rejected_at_load`). Registration, removal after publication, the guidance note, and the handoff candidate are 5b.

### Deviations and debt

Deviations from the brief, each with its reason:

- **`OMNIFRONS_OUTPUT_DIR` is not in the descriptors' `declared_env`.** The brief asked for the key "through the existing allowlist" and for both adapters to declare it. An allowlisted key is resolved from the parent environment, which HAP-001 D4 forbids for this key, so the plan gained product-set assignments instead, the key joins the allowlist through `EnvPlan::assign`, and the shell declares it on every adapter plan; a descriptor-level declaration would have let the parent's value through on any plan the shell did not assign. Every adapter kind, including `pty-cli`, gets the declaration.
- **A new wire kind, `artifact-publish`, beyond the brief's `candidates`.** D5 says the line agent recognizes the proposal; a recognized proposal is a typed domain event, and the wire must carry it somehow — as its own kind rather than folded back into `tool-call`, so the renderer can show the entries by digest and the shell never parses JSON out of `argumentsText` a second time. A malformed `artifact.publish` still surfaces as the plain `tool-call` it is.
- **`outbox_status` carries more than the brief's three fields** (`declared`, `exists`, `reason` added; `outbox` null unless valid and existing): the brief's `outbox: displayPath` cannot honestly show a device path for a declaration that failed validation, and a fresh project's default outbox does not exist until the first launch, which a renderer must be able to say.
- **A policy that cannot be loaded is `outbox-unavailable` at launch and `outbox-invalid` at `candidates_list`.** HAP-001-R10 says an `outbox-invalid` outbox renders `outbox-unavailable` at launch; R8 says a failed declaration blocks ingestion as `outbox-invalid`. Both mappings are implemented, one per surface.
- **An unreadable entry is excluded and counted, not rendered `outbox-escape`.** HAP-001's escape row names a link, a non-regular file, and an escaping path; an entry that could not be opened at all (`EACCES`, vanished between listing and open) is none of those, so it is excluded from the candidates and counted in the `candidates` summary's `unreadable`.
- **The whole-outbox inventory holds no handles.** HAP-001-R17's held handle exists for the approval that follows; the session-start inventory proposes and nothing follows in 5a, so its handles are released and 5b re-opens each entry under R15 when its turn comes. The run-end inventory does hold handles, capped at 32 (D22), with the excess kept `candidate` without one.
- **The outbox is created by the first launch, not by `outbox_status`.** A status query is read-only; `open_outbox(create_if_missing)` is the one place that creates it, and a fresh project reports `valid` with `exists: false`.
- **`sha2` joins the supervisor's dev-dependencies** so the fixture's hand-rolled SHA-256 is checked against the real hasher; the normal dependency tree (`cargo tree -p omnifrons-supervisor -e normal --depth 1`) is unchanged.
- **`--propose-missing` is a fixture flag the brief did not list**, for the unmatched-proposal diagnostic; the brief's `--write-outbox <n>` cycles three heavy kinds and always adds the note and the executable-shaped file, and its proposal names the even-indexed heavy files.
- **No end-to-end `harness_spawn` test, and what else is not exercised.** As in slices 3 and 4, a Tauri command cannot be driven from a unit test; the chain is proven in pieces — the fixture and the environment through `spawn_approved` (`tests/outbox_launch.rs`), the preparer and prober through the contracts and `tests/outbox_fs.rs`, the launch sequence through `launch_with_run_subdirectory` with a fake spawn, the tracker, status, whole-outbox inventory, and `candidates_for_run` through the shell's unit tests with a fake adapter that emits a real `ArtifactPublish`, and the error mappings directly. Stated in full, what no test exercises: `fake-agent`'s proposal line flowing through `LineAgent::parse_line` into the tracker in one test (the parse is tested on the same JSON shape the fixture emits, and the tracker on the typed event a fake adapter emits); `harness_spawn`, `outbox_status`, `candidates_list`, and `workspace_pick` as Tauri commands (only their pure bodies — `launch_with_run_subdirectory`, `outbox_status_for`, `inventory_whole_outbox` and `candidates_for_run`, `activate_workspace` — are tested); `forward_adapter_output`'s thread body wiring the `RunEndTracker` and sequencer (both are tested directly); a live shell's run-record eviction releasing handles (tested at the table level only); the growth-between-size-check-and-read half of the policy bound (the size check and the exact-cap boundary are tested, a file growing during the read is not); the `mkdirat`→`openat` window and any other real swap race (not reproducible deterministically; disclosed as residuals); every Windows code path (cross-compiled, never executed, including the reparse-point opens) and every macOS execution (CI only, including `EOPNOTSUPP` on a socket — the socket fixture binds at a short path under the temp directory and renames the entry into the run subdirectory, because the macOS temp path plus the run subdirectory exceeds `sun_path`'s 104 bytes; the assertion was shown load-bearing on Linux by removing the prober's socket arm and watching the test fail with `Unreadable`); and the `remove_dir` refusal on a subdirectory made non-empty by a *different* process (tested with a file the test itself writes into it).
- **A per-run cap of 1024 proposed entries** (`MAX_PROPOSED_ENTRIES`) and the enforcement of D22's 32 held handles across the remembered runs of the active workspace within a shell session (the table is cleared when the workspace changes) are spike defaults HAP-001 does not spell out (it names the 32 per project and says nothing about proposal volume or about what "project" means to a shell that can change workspace); both are disclosed here rather than presented as decided.
- **A `Duration` fixture instant is one second off a round hour** in `outbox_state.rs`'s test, so clippy's `duration_suboptimal_units` stays quiet without an allow.

Debt and open owner decisions this slice names without deciding:

- **The product work area (HAP-001-R7, D1)** is not implemented: nothing in 5a writes a recovery entry or a journal, so the check has no use site; 5b must add it with the canonicalize-and-re-check-at-every-use discipline, and the VP-S26 cases that exercise it.
- **Windows residuals** (D5): no by-handle identity comparison, no link count, no owner-only permissions, no `mkdirat`; `outbox-linked` cannot be produced there. Closing them means a Windows API dependency for `GetFileInformationByHandle`, which the owner has not been asked for.
- **The POSIX `mkdirat`→`openat` window** (D4): no atomic create-and-open for directories exists, so a same-user process can swap a different directory in before the first open; the verification proves that the harness is pointed at the directory whose identity that first open captured, not that no such swap happened — TM-001 A6's same-user residual, disclosed here next to the Windows ones rather than claimed closed.
- **Quarantine-flag detection** is not implemented: HAP-001-R4 identifies an executable by extension list, magic bytes, *and* the platform's quarantine flag where one exists (macOS's `com.apple.quarantine` extended attribute, Windows's `Zone.Identifier` alternate data stream); this slice reads extension and magic bytes only, so a downloaded file carrying only the flag classifies by its content and name. Debt for 5b or a later slice.
- **The listing is path-based** (`read_dir` on the directory's path) while every open is handle-relative; a handle-relative listing needs `nix`'s `dir` feature or `fdopendir` and was not added.
- **Display names are not sanitized** (HAP-001-R24 is 5b): `CandidateDto.name` is producer-supplied text and the renderer must treat it as plain text only, as it already does for every other harness-originated string.
- **The `candidates` event delays the terminal `state` frame** by the time the run subdirectory takes to hash; on a run that wrote very large files the renderer sees `state` late. A bound on inventory time, or an asynchronous inventory with `state` first, is a later decision.
- **A run record evicted from the bounded table** (64) releases its handles and forgets its proposals; its entries are then found by the next whole-outbox inventory as unattributed. HAP-001-R28's retention-by-digest of a refused entry is 5b.
- **`RunId` minting is time-plus-sequence**, unique on one device for the product's lifetime; whether the run id should be the Catalog's run identity (AEC-001's `run_id`) is AEC-001's decision.
- **HAP-001 D3, D5, D12, D16, D17, D18, D19, D20, D22** remain the owner's calls; this slice assumes D17's default path, D20's one-level layout, D22's cap of 32, and D14's block-the-launch, and says so here rather than presenting them as decided.

### Renderer

The renderer half of this slice landed after the Rust half above, against exactly the wire shapes in [IPC shapes](#ipc-shapes-2). `renderer/src/ipc/harness.ts` gains the two commands as typed wrappers — `outboxStatus()` invoking `outbox_status` with no arguments, and `candidatesList(runId?)` invoking `candidates_list` with exactly `{ runId }` for a run and exactly `{}` for the whole outbox (the key is absent, not `undefined`, so the wire request is the documented one) — the types `OutboxStatus` (`declared` and `outbox` nullable strings, `exists`, `state: OutboxState`, `reason: OutboxReason | null`, `policyPath`) with `OutboxState = 'valid' | 'outbox-invalid' | 'outbox-unavailable'` and the seven-token `OutboxReason`, `Candidate` (`name`; nullable `size`, `sha256Short`, `detectedType`, `class`; `attribution: Attribution`; `state: CandidateState`) with `CandidateState`, `ArtifactClass` and `Attribution` (`{ kind: 'run', runId } | { kind: 'unattributed' }`) as closed unions and `detectedType` a plain token string, `ProposedEntry` and `CandidatesSummary` (the nine camelCase fields), two members on the `AgentEvent` union mirroring `AdapterEventDto` field-for-field (`{ kind: 'artifact-publish', payload: { entries: ProposedEntry[] } }` and `{ kind: 'candidates', payload: CandidatesSummary }`), and `'outbox-invalid'` and `'outbox-unavailable'` on `ShellErrorCode`. `harness.test.ts` (30 → 38) asserts the wrapper argument shapes (`Object.keys(args)` exactly `['runId']`, and exactly `[]`), returns both status shapes and a refused candidate's null facts verbatim, rejects `candidatesList` with a `ShellError`-typed `outbox-invalid` and `harnessSpawn` with a typed `outbox-unavailable`, delivers both new kinds through a live `Channel` (the `candidates` payload's keys asserted to be exactly the nine camelCase names), and extends the compile-time exhaustiveness guard to ten kinds. The wrapper tests' red was a runtime red (`TypeError: outboxStatus is not a function`, `TypeError: candidatesList is not a function`; 5 failed, 33 passed); the kinds and codes were type-level reds only, as in slice 4 — `tsc -b` reported 19 errors of the form `Type '"artifact-publish"' is not assignable to type '"state" | … | "terminal-drops"'` and `Type '"outbox-invalid"' is not assignable to type 'ShellErrorCode'` while Vitest was green on those tests — recorded here rather than dressed up as a runtime red.

`renderer/src/AgentPanel.tsx` is the only component touched; there is no new component file, no approval control, and nothing that publishes or ingests. Four additions, each behind exact copy. (1) An **outbox status line** (a `<p role="status" aria-label="Outbox">` under the workspace paragraph — a live region like the workspace-pick status beside it, named so the two stay distinguishable; slice 5 review, R3-018), fetched on mount and again after a successful workspace pick through a sequenced fetch (the R3-008 pattern: a mount-time response landing after the pick-time one is dropped). A valid, existing outbox renders `outbox: <path> (declared <declared>)`, the path being `outbox_status.outbox` — the third explicit RCS-001-R14 exception recorded under [IPC shapes](#ipc-shapes-2), shown as identity evidence of where the agent's outputs land and never sent back in any command; a valid declaration whose directory does not exist yet renders `outbox: <declared> (created at the first launch)` with no device path anywhere; `outbox-invalid` renders `outbox invalid: <declared> (<reason>)` when the policy declared a path and `outbox invalid: <reason>` when the policy itself could not be loaded (`declared` null) — HAP-001's visible state for the condition is the declared path shown as invalid (slice 5 review, R3-017) — and, a state `OutboxStateTag` carries beyond the brief's copy, `outbox-unavailable` renders `outbox unavailable: <declared> (<reason>)` the same way, the reason token mapped by an exhaustive switch to fixed English (`outside-project` → "the declared path resolves outside the project", `link` → "the declared path is a link", `not-a-directory` → "the declared path is not a directory", `unreadable` → "the declared path could not be read", `policy-unreadable` → "the classification policy could not be read", `policy-corrupt` → "the classification policy is corrupt", `policy-invalid` → "the classification policy is invalid", a null reason → "reason unreported") and never the raw token; the whole line renders through `PlainTextLine`. Because the shell answers `outbox_status` with `workspace-unavailable` while no workspace is active, that rejection renders no line and no alert — the missing workspace is already visible — while any other `ShellError` reaches the unchanged banner and a non-`ShellError` rejection renders only `unexpected error`. (2) An `artifact-publish` event renders as one transcript entry holding a block (`data-testid="publish-proposal"`) labelled `publish proposal:` and listing each entry (`data-testid="publish-entry"`) as `<name> <first 8 characters of sha256>` through `PlainTextLine`, the digest cut by code point — a proposal the harness made and nothing here executes, held to the same zero-interactive-elements bar as the tool-call block. (3) A `candidates` event renders as one transcript line, exactly `candidates: 5 total, 3 candidate, 1 escape, 1 linked, 2 attributed, 3 unattributed, 0 unreadable, 1 unmatched proposals` for the fixture summary, every count always present in that order and a zero rendered as `0`; the panel then calls `candidatesList(runId)` with the run id the event named (the shell's own minted token, never a path) and renders a **candidates table** beside the transcript, never inside it: a `<section aria-label="Candidates">` (`data-testid="agent-candidates"`) opening with the fixed line `publication not available in this slice` — no approval, ingestion or publication exists in this slice — then the columns `name`, `size`, `sha256`, `type`, `class`, `attribution`, `state`; every cell through `PlainTextLine`, a `null` fact rendered as `—`, the attribution cell `run` or `unattributed` by an exhaustive switch, the state token transcribed verbatim by another, and every `state !== 'candidate'` row marked with a `<mark>refused</mark>` beside its state. The fetch is sequenced and gated on the spawn generation the channel closure captured, so a response landing after the next Start is dropped, and the next Start clears the table along with the transcript — a Start whose spawn is rejected puts it back, since no run replaced it, unless a frame on the new channel already produced a new one (slice 5 review, R3-019; the R1-012 analogue of the echoed prompt's withdrawal); a `ShellError` from `candidates_list` reaches the banner with no table, a non-`ShellError` renders `unexpected error`, and the summary line stands either way. (4) The two new codes render through the unchanged banner as `outbox-unavailable: something already sits at the run subdirectory path; remove it and relaunch` and `outbox-invalid: the classification policy could not be loaded`, with no "untrusted" prefix (neither is a `LaunchGate` denial) and a stray `detail` never rendered. The stale-run guards, terminal-state release, 2000-entry transcript cap, byte-counted prompt cap, frozen selection controls, transport disclosure and inert tool-call block are untouched, and every slice 3 and slice 4 test still passes.

`AgentPanel.test.tsx` grows by thirty-one tests (90 → 121), and `app.test.tsx` keeps its five with its mount mock now answering `outbox_status` the way the shell does with no workspace (a `workspace-unavailable` rejection) — that mock was the red for the mount-time fetch at the `App` level (`renders no alert on mount` failing with `expected <div role="alert" …> to be null`, the mock's fallback `invalid-request` banner), green once it answered; every default and custom mock in `AgentPanel.test.tsx` answers `outbox_status` too, for the same reason. Each behavior was written before its implementation and observed red — the status line's twelve on `Unable to find an element by: [data-testid="agent-outbox-status"]`, `expected +0 to be 1` (no `outbox_status` call), `Unable to find role="alert"` and `expected false to be true`; the proposal's four on `Unable to find an element by: [data-testid="publish-proposal"]`; the candidates line's two on `expected '' to be 'candidates: 5 total, …'`; the table's nine and the content-security pair on `Unable to find role="region" and name "Candidates"`, `Unable to find role="alert"`, `expected +0 to be 1` and `expected false to be true` — except two pins green on arrival and recorded as such: a `candidates` event carrying a foreign run id triggers no `candidates_list` and appends nothing (the existing id guard already drops the frame), and the `outbox-unavailable` spawn banner (the banner is code-agnostic, as slice 4 found for `pty-unsupported`). Asserted, beyond the copy above: the status line's exact text per state and reason with the raw token absent from the document (`link` excepted, being a word of its own line) and `elsewhere/outbox` shown only when the policy declared it, never when the policy could not be loaded; a second `outbox_status` call after a pick and none of its stale predecessor's effect; the post-unmount continuations of both fetches raising no `console.error`; the proposal block's entries as exactly `['report.pdf abababab', 'data.zip 01234567']` with the full digest absent, an empty proposal as the bare label, and the tool-call test's interactive-element and attribute checks plus a click on every element with the IPC count unchanged; `candidates_list` called with exactly `[{ runId }]`, the header row and the four fixture rows cell by cell (`—` in every null fact, `outbox-linked refused` and `outbox-escape refused` in the state cells, a `<mark>` on exactly the refused rows), the table outside the transcript, the same inertness checks on the whole region, and an empty inventory as the fixed line plus the header row alone; and the brief's content-security set — a candidate name `../../<b>etc</b>/pass<ESC>wd<U+202E>` renders as the text `../../<b>etc</b>/passwd` with no `<b>`, no `<a>`, no `[href]`, no ESC and no override (the traversal is data here; the renderer never resolves it), a `sha256Short` of `zz<b>!?` and a `detectedType` of `<b>pdf</b>` render as text, and an outbox path carrying U+202E renders stripped with a `<b>` in the declared name literal. Totals before the review below: `pnpm -r test` **208 → 247** (`harness.test.ts` 30 → 38, `AgentPanel.test.tsx` 90 → 121), three plain runs and three `CI=true` runs all identical at 247/247, `AgentPanel.test.tsx` alone ten consecutive runs at 121/121; after it, **252** (`AgentPanel.test.tsx` 121 → 126), three plain runs and three `CI=true` runs all identical at 252/252, `AgentPanel.test.tsx` alone ten consecutive runs at 126/126 — `pnpm -r lint` and `pnpm -r build` (`tsc -b && vite build`) clean at every step, no `.only`, provenance sweep empty.

A reliability review of this half was applied before any commit (risk lens: nothing; reliability lens: four warnings, one suggestion), adding five tests to `AgentPanel.test.tsx` (121 → 126) and changing three behaviors, each observed red first. R3-017: the declared name on a non-`valid` line (red `expected 'outbox invalid: the declared path res…' to be 'outbox invalid: elsewhere/outbox (the…'`, and the `outbox-unavailable` twin `expected 'outbox unavailable: the declared path…' to be 'outbox unavailable: .omnifrons/outbox…'`), with a `<b>` in the declared name asserted literal and no `<b>` element. R3-018: the status paragraph is a named live region (`role="status"`, `aria-label="Outbox"`) and the status-line tests query it by role and name instead of a test id, which is gone (red `Unable to find role="status" and name "Outbox"` on the eight tests that need the element). R3-019: a rejected Start restores the candidates table it had cleared (red `Unable to find an accessible element with the role "region" and name "Candidates"`), the success path — a spawn that resolves clears it — kept as its own test. Test-only pins, green on arrival and recorded as such: R3-015, the `candidates` count magnitude (`Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` renders as `9007199254740991`; a u32 maximum as `4294967295` and a u64-shaped 2^64 parsed from JSON as `18446744073709552000`, plain digits and no exponent in every case — every count's wire type is `u32`, so only the first two are reachable); R3-016, both fetch continuations rejected after unmount (`outbox_status` with `outbox-unavailable`, `candidates_list` with `invalid-request`) raise no `console.error` and render no alert.

Deviations from the renderer brief, each with its reason: the `outbox-unavailable` state renders `outbox unavailable: <reason>` because `OutboxStateTag` carries it (a non-directory or unreadable declared path) and the brief's copy named only `valid` and `outbox-invalid`; a `sha256` column joins the brief's six because the brief's own content-security test needs a `sha256Short` to render, and the VP-S22 procedure note above reads digests from candidate rows; and the `workspace-unavailable` quiet path is the implementer's reading of "fetched on mount" against a shell that rejects the mount-time call whenever no workspace is picked. Debt this half names without deciding: the status line is not refreshed after a launch, so a fresh project keeps showing `(created at the first launch)` until the next pick or mount even though the first launch created the directory; neither the `candidates` summary's run id nor an attributed row's run id is displayed (the table is one run's inventory); a `candidates_list` rejection leaves only the banner, with no retry; and the candidates table is a proposal surface only — HAP-001-R24's display-name sanitization, approval, and every state past `candidate` are slice 5b. Everything here stays dev-mode-only exactly as the Rust half states: no packaged build, no live executable exercised by any renderer test, no real outbox read by the renderer (every status and every row above came from a mocked `invoke`), and no producer signing — the producer line's "(unknown)" is unchanged.

### Relationship to VP-001

Every result behind this slice — every passing test listed above; `cargo fmt --all --check` clean; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` (Linux) clean; `cargo clippy -p omnifrons-supervisor -p omnifrons-adapters -p omnifrons-app --all-targets --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu -- -D warnings` clean (cross-compiled, never executed; the shell crate stays outside that check for the `windres` reason slice 3 recorded); `pnpm -r test` at **252** (208 before this slice; the renderer half is recorded in [Renderer](#renderer-4)), `cargo test --workspace --all-targets --all-features` all green at **436** tests, summed over every test binary's own `passed` count the way slices 3 and 4 were (domain 53, app 124, adapters 87, supervisor 68, shell 104; +134 over slice 4's 302: domain +26, app +33, adapters +29, supervisor +7, shell +39, of which 20 arrived with the review and CI fixes recorded above: the handle cap at the table and the tracker, the launch sequence's two failure branches, the non-empty refusal and the success case, the proposal cap at the record and on the wire, `candidates_for_run`'s three refusals, the PTY run's unattributed inventory, the `droppedBefore` placement, the oversized policy at the store and in `outbox_status`, the Unix socket, a workspace change forgetting the previous workspace's runs and a re-pick keeping them, the table's `clear`, a record evicted between the inventory's two phases, and the process-wide run sequence); `cargo test -p omnifrons-shell --test feature_gate` green (2 passed); `cargo tree -p omnifrons-adapters -e normal --depth 1` still exactly `nix`, `omnifrons-app`, `omnifrons-domain`, `serde`, `serde_json`, `sha2`, and the supervisor's still exactly `nix`, `omnifrons-app`, `omnifrons-domain`, `tokio`, `tracing`; no new `unsafe` (the two precedents, `demo.rs` and `pty.rs`, are the only sites); the provenance sweep empty — was produced against a `cargo test` development build, on one contributor's Linux machine, not a packaged release build on a pinned Windows/macOS/Linux baseline, and not against macOS or Windows at all: the Windows clippy pass is a cross-compilation type-check, and the unix-gated link, hard-link, and FIFO tests have not run on macOS here (CI's `macos-latest` leg is where they do). Per [VP-001-R2](desktop-stack-verification-plan.md#product-requirements), this is `dev-mode-only` and therefore **not** admissible as VP-S22, VP-S26, or VP-S28 evidence — nor is HAP-001 itself accepted, so no feature may yet claim the guarantee it describes; it is an input to writing those scenarios' procedures well (above), and, for VP-S26 specifically, to stating per platform what "verified by handle" can and cannot mean.

## Slice 5b — approval, local publication, journal, and Catalog record (HAP-001 publication side)

This slice's Rust half (`omnifrons-domain`, `omnifrons-app`, `omnifrons-adapters`, `src-tauri`) implements the publication side of the [heavy-asset publication contract](heavy-asset-publication.md) (HAP-001, Accepted 2026-09-08) over what 5a built: the product work area (HAP-001-R7), the explicit per-artifact approval bound to identity facts (R22, D3), the provider adapter port with one dev-mode local-directory adapter, the publication transaction in HAP-001's fixed order — approval validated, identity facts re-taken from the held handle, the path-still-names-the-handle check with the recovery entry (R18), the copy streamed from the held handle with its digest taken from the same bytes (R16, R17), the published copy re-read and compared (R21), the Catalog record and the portable reference (R24), the entry removed only after both (R28) — the publication identity and its idempotency (R23), the journal and the restart replay (R29), sanitized display names (R24 under RCS-001's file-name rule), provenance (R36), the `artifact.state` transitions (R35), and the held-handle cap's re-open (R17, D22). Real providers, `provider-synced`, the guidance-note write (D18), the wrong-root scan (D16), handoff preflight, MRP-001 tombstones, Windows owner-only ACLs, and standing approvals are 5c or later and are not implemented here. **Dev-mode only**: the one provider adapter has no remote, no credential, and exists so the transaction can be exercised end to end; nothing here is VP-001 evidence ([Relationship to VP-001](#relationship-to-vp-001-5)). The renderer half landed after the Rust half, against the wire shapes in [IPC shapes](#ipc-shapes-3), and is recorded in [Renderer](#renderer-5).

### Scope

In: R5 (no device path in any record, event, or payload — the two new commands' payloads carry logical identities, states, and the reference only), R6 as far as a policy that declares no asset root goes (rendered `destination-invalid`, never a fallback destination; see the deviation below), R7 (the work area: created owner-only, canonicalized and checked at open, re-canonicalized and re-checked against the active workspace root at every use — every journal write, every recovery-entry write — and refused as `work-area-invalid` when it resolves inside or at that root), R13–R18 and R20–R21 as the transaction states them, R22 (approval by explicit user action naming the candidate by run id, name, and digest; the surface facts identity-bound; unattributed entries approved the same way; refused, unclassified, and non-heavy entries not approvable), R23 (publication identity `sha256(project identity ‖ content digest)`, a repeat → `duplicate-publication` with the existing record acknowledged and the display name added as an alias, never a second copy or record), R24 (every Catalog record field HAP-001 lists, names sanitized, the reference issued only once `registered`), R26 (the state tokens are exactly the signal mapping's), R28 (removal only after the published copy and the record are verified, and only while the path still names the handle's file; every deferral journaled), R29 (a step journaled before the next runs; a `published-local` without a registration resumed from the record the journal carries; nothing repeated; no handle survives a restart, so the entry's removal is deferred and said so), R35 (one `artifact-state` frame per transition, in order), R36 (provenance: scope, producer as the run with its adapter id and executable approval or `unattributed` with the run subdirectory as a location fact, and the UTC time of every transition), R14 for the device asset path (re-canonicalized and re-checked against the active workspace at every publication → `destination-invalid`), R17's cap re-open (a candidate remembered without a held handle is re-opened once, no-follow, relative to the outbox handle, and refused when its facts changed), and R34/R37 as "not applicable": the local-directory adapter holds no credential.

Out, named as debt below: provider upload and `provider-synced` (R25, R30), `over-limit` (R31), the secret scan (R41), per-device availability inside the portable record (R27; this device's observation is reported from its own journal instead), the whole-outbox entries' approval (only a remembered run's inventory is approvable in this slice), the re-publication of a recovery entry, the vault as a containment root (no vault exists in this repository; the checks run against the active workspace root), and a device identity.

### Design decisions (spike defaults, each disclosed)

Every default below is the spike's; a feature that assumes it before the owner disposes must say so, and this section does.

- **D1 — domain types** (`crates/omnifrons-domain/src/publication.rs`). `ProjectIdentity(Sha256Digest)` and `PublicationIdentity(Sha256Digest)`, with `publication_identity_preimage(project, digest)` the exact 64 bytes `project ‖ digest` HAP-001-R23's derivation hashes (no domain tag: the brief's formula, followed literally); `AssetRootId` (one token of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}`, the run id's shape, so it names a device directory without a second validation); `CatalogId { asset_root_id, publication_id }` rendered `<asset root id>/<publication hex>`; `ProviderLocator` (opaque); `ProviderState::{Pending, Synced, Failed, Unavailable}`; `ArtifactState` — the ten tokens this slice can put on a surface, spelled as the signal mapping spells them (`candidate`, `published-local`, `registered`, `provider-synced`, `registration-pending`, `refused`, `integrity-mismatch`, `duplicate-publication`, `outbox-escape`, `outbox-linked`) and nothing else (HAP-001-R26); `DisplayName::sanitize` (RCS-001's file-name rule as this slice reads it: split on `/` and `\`, drop empty, `.`, and `..` components and join the rest with `_`; strip every C0/DEL control and the bidirectional override, embedding, and isolate characters U+202A–U+202E and U+2066–U+2069; cap at 255 bytes on a character boundary; trim trailing dots and spaces; prefix a Windows reserved device name — `CON`, `PRN`, `AUX`, `NUL`, `COM1`–`COM9`, `LPT1`–`LPT9`, with or without an extension, any case — with `_`; an empty result becomes `unnamed`); `CatalogRecord` with HAP-001 § Catalog record's fields (`publication_id`, `catalog_id`, `class`, `detected_type`, `size`, `digest` carried with the algorithm id `sha256` — D13 — `names`, `relationships` (empty in this slice), `provenance { scope_id, producer, transitions }`, `provider { adapter_id, locator, confirmation_kind, state, reason }`, `state`, `record_version` = 1); `Producer::{Run { run_id, adapter_id, executable_approval } | Unattributed { found_under }}`; `PortableReference` (AEC-001's `ref`: kind `artifact`, id the publication identity, locator the Catalog identity; `CatalogRecord::reference()` is `Some` only for a `registered` record, HAP-001-R24); `ArtifactApproval` (every identity-bound fact the surface shows, the destination asset root, the launch's adapter id and executable approval when the run record kept them, and `DeviceLocalUser` as the act-as identity) with `ArtifactApprovalId(u64)` derived from `sha256("omnifrons-artifact-approval-v1" ‖ publication identity ‖ nanos-since-epoch as big-endian u128)`'s first 8 bytes and carried on the wire as 16 hex characters (a `u64` does not survive a JSON number's 53-bit mantissa); and the journal's shapes `JournalEntry::{Approved(Box<ArtifactApproval>) | Step(Box<StepEntry>)}`, `StepEntry { publication_id, approval_id, step, outcome, digest, reason, at, record, recovery }`, `JournalStep` (`published-local`, `registered`, `registration-pending`, `cleanup`, `refused`, `outbox-escape`, `outbox-linked`, `integrity-mismatch`, `duplicate-publication`), `StepOutcome::{Ok, Failed, Deferred}`.
- **D2 — application ports and the transaction** (`crates/omnifrons-app/src/{content_hasher,work_area,blob_store,catalog_store,publication_journal,outbox_entry_ops,publication}.rs`). `ContentHasher { sha256, digest_reader }` exists because this crate depends on `omnifrons-domain` and `thiserror` only; the three derivations live beside it, and the **project identity is a spike default**: `sha256("omnifrons-project-v1" ‖ the workspace root's canonical path bytes)` stands in for the registered scope identity context-orb.md and RSP-001-R13 own, which this repository does not have — HAP-001's cross-project separation rests on that identity being non-forgeable, and a path digest is not that. `WorkAreaRoot::open(configured, workspaces)` refuses a configured path whose projected canonical form is or lies under a registered workspace root before creating anything, creates the area and `journal/` and `recovery/` owner-only (unix `0o700`, set explicitly after `DirBuilder::mode` so a permissive umask cannot widen it), canonicalizes, and `check(workspaces)` re-canonicalizes and re-compares at every use (a vanished area is `Unusable`, never a stale pass). `DeviceAssetPath::open` does the same for an asset root's device directory (HAP-001-R14). `BlobStorePort` is HAP-001's `BlobStorePort` reduced to this slice: `capabilities()`, `stage(identity) -> StagedObject` (a sink the transaction streams the held handle into, then `commit() -> locator` or `abort()`), `open_published(locator)` for HAP-001-R21's read-back, `discard(locator)` for the copy that fails it, and `confirm(locator)` for the observation that would yield `provider-synced`. The brief named `publish(handle, identity) -> locator`; the port hands the transaction a sink instead so the copy and its digest are **one pass in the application layer** — the bytes leave the outbox exactly once, from the held handle, through a `Tee` that hashes exactly what it writes — and the adapter never sees a path or a handle. `CatalogStore { list, find, register, add_alias }` and `PublicationJournal { append, replay }` with the pure replay queries (`find_approval`, `resume_point`, `pending_registrations`); `OutboxEntryOps { identity, unlink }` with `EntryIdentity::{SameFile, DifferentFile, Missing, Unverifiable}`. `publication::publish(ports, approval, source, on_state)` is the transaction: work-area check → journal replay → `catalog.find` (an existing record → alias when the name differs → journal `duplicate-publication` → `DuplicatePublication { existing }`) → resume point (a journaled `published-local` whose copy still verifies skips the copy; one that no longer verifies, or a `registered` step whose record is gone from the Catalog, publishes again from the held handle) → `fstat` on the held handle (not regular → `outbox-escape`; link count above one → `outbox-linked`, unix only) → `entry_ops.identity` (`DifferentFile` or `Missing` → the held bytes written to `recovery/<digest hex>` owner-only via a temporary name, journal `outbox-escape` with the recovery digest, `OutboxEscape { recovery }`; `Unverifiable` proceeds, the disclosed Windows residual) → `stage` + the tee copy (a streamed digest or size unequal to the approval's → `abort`, `integrity-mismatch` reason `source-digest-changed`) → `commit` → read-back (`open_published` hashed again; unequal → `discard`, `integrity-mismatch` reason `published-copy-digest-differs`) → the record built with `provider_state: pending` and the adapter's own reason → journal `published-local` with the record → `register` (a `Duplicate` from the store is idempotent; any other failure → journal `registration-pending`, state `registration-pending`, no reference, cleanup deferred) → journal `registered` → cleanup (`identity` again: `SameFile` → `unlink` → journal `cleanup ok`; anything else → journal `cleanup deferred` with the reason `entry-changed-after-publication`, `entry-missing`, `identity-check-unavailable`, or `unlink-failed`). Every failure journals its step and emits its state before returning; `on_state` receives `StateEvent { publication_id, state, provider_state }` per transition. `recover(ports, project, on_state)` replays the journal, and for every publication of `project` whose resume point is `published-local` re-verifies the copy and registers the journaled record (journal `registered` reason `resumed-after-restart`, cleanup `deferred` reason `no-handle-after-restart`) or, when the copy no longer verifies, journals `integrity-mismatch` reason `published-copy-unverifiable-after-restart` and leaves the retained entry for a fresh approval; a completed step is never repeated. The fakes and contracts behind `contract-tests` (`contract/publication.rs`): `FakeHasher` (deterministic, not cryptographic), `InMemoryBlobStore` with `corrupt_next_commit`, `InMemoryCatalogStore` with `fail_next_register`, `InMemoryJournal`, `ScriptedEntryOps`, and `catalog_store_contract`/`publication_journal_contract`/`blob_store_contract`.
- **D3 — the adapters** (`crates/omnifrons-adapters/src/{sha2_hasher,local_dir_blob_store,jsonl_catalog_store,jsonl_publication_journal,fs_outbox_entry_ops,catalog_record_dto}.rs`; dependency tables unchanged, `tests/deps.rs` as is — `fstat`, `fstatat`, and `unlinkat` are gated by `nix`'s `fs` feature this crate already enables, verified against the vendored 0.31.3 source: `sys::stat` is a `feature = "fs"` module, `unistd::unlinkat` sits inside the `fs` block that opens at `unistd.rs:1478`, `fcntl::AtFlags` is gated by `fs`, `process`, or `user`). `Sha2Hasher` is the real SHA-256. `LocalDirBlobStore` — **the first and only provider adapter, dev-mode only**: adapter id `local-dir`, `publish_mode: copy`, `confirmation_kind: "no remote"`, no object limit, quota unknown, no native placeholder; the copy is written to a per-call staging name `<device asset path>/.<publication hex>.<pid>-<sequence>.part` (owner-only, `create_new`, so two stages of one identity never share a file; a staging file a crash leaves between `stage` and `commit` is not removed by the adapter — disclosed, a sweep is 5c debt) and renamed to `<device asset path>/<publication hex>` on commit, a second commit of the same identity replacing the copy (what a retry after an interruption needs); the locator is `local-dir:<asset root id>/<publication hex>`; `confirm` is always `None`, so a publication through it is `registered` with `provider_state: pending` and the adapter's reason `no remote`, never `provider-synced`; a locator this adapter did not issue — another adapter's, another asset root's, anything that is not exactly 64 hex characters after the prefix — is `ForeignLocator`, never resolved to a path; credentials: none, HAP-001-R34 and R37 not applicable. `JsonlCatalogStore` — **D9's default**: `.omnifrons/catalog.jsonl` under the project (HAP-001 names no file; `catalog.jsonl` is the spike's), one line per event (`record` on registration, `alias` when a later identical publication adds a display name), each with `schema: 1`; a line that does not parse exactly, another schema, an alias naming no record, or a record whose ids disagree fails the whole read as `Corrupt`; an absent file is an empty catalog and a read creates nothing; every display name is re-sanitized on read, because a file under `.omnifrons/` is portable, synchronized, untrusted content (HAP-001-R40). `JsonlPublicationJournal` — `<work area>/journal/publications.jsonl`, created `0o600` on unix like the slice-2 store, one line per entry (`approved` or `step`, `schema: 1`), `sync_data` before `append` returns, a corrupt line or another schema fails the whole replay. `FsOutboxEntryOps` — unix: `fstat(handle)` against `fstatat(dir, name, AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW)` relative to the held directory handle, the same file exactly when device and inode agree, a link at the name compared as itself and never followed, `ENOENT`/`ENOTDIR` → `Missing`, any other error → `DifferentFile` (what cannot be confirmed is not the same file); `unlinkat(dir, name)` without `AT_REMOVEDIR`. Windows: `Unverifiable` and `unlink` unsupported — the transaction publishes from the handle and defers the removal with reason `identity-check-unavailable`, disclosed under HAP-001-R19 rather than closed (`std`'s by-handle identity is unstable and no Windows API dependency was added, as 5a found). The policy store reads `assetRootId` (below).
- **D4 — the shell** (`src-tauri/src/{publication_state.rs, ipc/publication.rs, ipc/dto.rs, outbox_state.rs}`). **D1's default location**: the work area is `<app_local_data_dir>/work-area` (`journal/` and `recovery/` under it, and nothing else in this slice); the asset roots — **D8's per-OS default, derived and never written down** — are `<app_local_data_dir>/asset-roots/<asset root id>`; both sit in the same application-data directory as the slice-2 approval store and RCS-001-R10's quarantine, are re-checked against the active workspace at every use, and never cross IPC. **The asset root identity comes from the project policy**: `.omnifrons/asset-policy.json` gains an optional `assetRootId` (one token, never a path; a raw path or a traversal is `policy-corrupt` at load); a project that declares none has no destination and every approval fails `destination-invalid` — HAP-001-R6's `destination-unconfigured` folded into that code in this slice, because the asset binding the scope will carry does not exist yet. **Approval** (`approve_candidate`): the candidate is looked up in the remembered run's run-end inventory by its listed name; a state other than `candidate`, a digest that does not match the request, or a class other than `generated-heavy` is `refused`; the approval carries the run record's adapter id and executable approval when the launch kept them (`RunRecord::with_provenance`, which `harness_spawn`'s `kind: "adapter"` now uses); the derived id is checked against the journal and a collision — the same publication approved at the same nanosecond — is `invalid-request` rather than a second line (the slice-2 store's duplicate discipline); the approval line is the journal's first entry for the publication, so **the approval store is the journal**. **Publication** (`publish_approved`): the approval is found in the journal, must belong to the active project (`invalid-request` otherwise, with nothing of the other project's touched), is acknowledged as `duplicate-publication` from the Catalog alone when its identity is already registered — before any entry is touched, so a repeat of a completed publication whose entry was removed is a duplicate, never `refused` (`publication::acknowledge_registered`) — and otherwise its bytes come from the run record's held handle (`RunRecord::take_candidate_handle`, the transaction becoming the handle's owner) or, when the record holds none — the D22 cap released it, or the record was evicted — from one no-follow open relative to the outbox and run-subdirectory handles (`FsRunOutboxPreparer::open_outbox`, `FsOutboxInventory::open_subdirectory`, `FsCandidateProber::probe`) whose digest, size, and detected type must equal the approval's, else `refused` (`identity-facts-changed-at-re-open`), with `outbox-escape` and `outbox-linked` for a probe that says so, and `refused` for an entry that cannot be re-opened; then `DeviceAssetPath::open` for the approval's asset root (HAP-001-R14), the local-directory provider, the project's Catalog, and the transaction; every `StateEvent` is sent as an `artifact-state` frame over the command's `onState` channel — the `harness_spawn` pattern — and a renderer that went away is not a failure. **Restart replay**: `recover` runs at the start of `publications_list` and the pure body of `artifact_publish` consults the same resume point, so an interrupted publication resumes at the first command that touches publications after a restart rather than at process start (no workspace is active at start, and the Catalog is the project's); `publications_list` then lists the Catalog's records with this device's own availability (`local` when this device's journal shows `published-local` verified here, `unknown` otherwise — HAP-001-R27 observed, never inferred from the record) plus any `registration-pending` recovery could not complete. `ShellErrorCode` gains nine values (`run-active` arrived with the renderer risk review); `ShellErrorDetail` becomes an untagged enum so `duplicate-publication` can acknowledge the existing record's ids without changing the slice-2 detail's shape. **Concurrency** (R1-001): `PublicationState` carries the publication surface lock, held across the whole of `approve_candidate`, `publish_approved`, and `publications_for`, so the Catalog's replay-then-append is never interleaved and two publications of one identity racing each other yield exactly one `registered` and one `duplicate-publication` (proven with two threads against the real adapters; before the lock both threads registered and the Catalog read `Corrupt` afterwards); with one active workspace per shell, one lock is the per-project lock HAP-001-R23 needs, and a second shell process against the same journal is not serialized by it (no single-instance guard exists, as slice 2 records). A `Corrupt` catalog stays fail-closed (`catalog-unavailable`) and is repaired by hand for now — the offending line removed, every other kept, as `jsonl_catalog_store.rs`'s module doc spells out — with a `catalog_repair` command named as 5c debt. **Every workspace registration re-checks the work area** (R1-002, HAP-001-R7): `activate_workspace` and `workspace_current` run `WorkAreaRoot::check_configured` (a projected-canonical containment check that creates nothing) and report the outcome as `WorkspaceDto.workArea` (`valid` | `work-area-invalid`); the registration itself proceeds — the operation R7 refuses is the work area's use, which every publication command re-checks again.

### IPC shapes

Three new commands, registered next to the existing ones:

| Command | Params | Returns |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `artifact_approve` | `{ runId: string, name: string, sha256: string }` | `ArtifactApprovalDto` |
| `artifact_publish` | `{ approvalId: string, onState: Channel<ArtifactStateFrame> }` | `PublicationDto` |
| `publications_list` | *(none)* | `PublicationDto[]` |

`workspace_pick` and `workspace_current` (slice 3) gain one field on `WorkspaceDto`: `workArea`, `"valid"` or `"work-area-invalid"`, the product work area re-checked against the workspace on every registration (HAP-001-R7; R1-002):

```jsonc
// workspace_pick / workspace_current: the slice-3 shape plus workArea
{"displayPath": "/home/user/project", "workArea": "valid"}
// the picked workspace contains the configured work area: reported, the pick proceeds, and every
// publication command against it fails work-area-invalid until the work area is reconfigured
{"displayPath": "/home/user/project", "workArea": "work-area-invalid"}
```

```jsonc
// artifact_approve { runId: "run-1725782401-000000001-0", name: "run-1725782401-000000001-0/report.pdf",
//   sha256: "061eb0a8…" (64 hex) } -- the candidate exactly as candidates_list listed it, by the full
//   digest its row carries as `sha256` (added to CandidateDto in this slice; see § Slice 5 → IPC shapes).
// The identity-bound facts the approval surface shows (HAP-001-R22): every field a logical value,
// approvalId 16 hex, publicationId 64 hex, actAs the device-local user, approvedAt ms since the epoch.
{"approvalId": "23121521465ad9be", "publicationId": "2a91ea59fc5047831d97dbaebd763a3de37a5b382b683be59d10d6bdcdd92d4e",
 "runId": "run-1725782401-000000001-0", "name": "run-1725782401-000000001-0/report.pdf", "displayName": "report.pdf",
 "sha256Short": "061eb0a8", "size": 37, "detectedType": "pdf", "class": "generated-heavy",
 "attribution": {"kind": "run", "runId": "run-1725782401-000000001-0"}, "assetRootId": "main",
 "actAs": "device-local-user", "approvedAt": 1725782401000}
// an unattributed entry is approved the same way; its approval records the unattributed fact
{"approvalId": "5f0c…", "publicationId": "…", "runId": "run-1725782401-000000001-0",
 "name": "run-1725782401-000000001-0/stray.png", "displayName": "stray.png", "sha256Short": "11111111",
 "size": 11, "detectedType": "png", "class": "generated-heavy", "attribution": {"kind": "unattributed"},
 "assetRootId": "main", "actAs": "device-local-user", "approvedAt": 1725782401000}

// artifact_publish { approvalId: "23121521465ad9be" } -- registered: the reference is AEC-001's ref
// { kind, id, locator } with the Catalog identity as locator; providerState is the record's;
// availability is this device's own observation. No field is a device path.
{"publicationId": "2a91ea59fc5047831d97dbaebd763a3de37a5b382b683be59d10d6bdcdd92d4e", "state": "registered",
 "reference": {"kind": "artifact", "id": "2a91ea59fc5047831d97dbaebd763a3de37a5b382b683be59d10d6bdcdd92d4e",
   "locator": "main/2a91ea59fc5047831d97dbaebd763a3de37a5b382b683be59d10d6bdcdd92d4e"},
 "providerState": "pending", "catalogId": "main/2a91ea59fc5047831d97dbaebd763a3de37a5b382b683be59d10d6bdcdd92d4e",
 "names": ["report.pdf"], "availability": "local"}
// a registration that failed after a verified published-local: no reference before registration
// (HAP-001-R24), resumed by the next publications_list or artifact_publish (HAP-001-R29)
{"publicationId": "…", "state": "registration-pending", "reference": null, "providerState": null,
 "catalogId": null, "names": ["report.pdf"], "availability": "local"}

// the frames artifact_publish sends over onState, one per transition, in this order for a success
// (proposed AEC-001 kind artifact.state; HAP-001-R35): never a path, never a control
{"kind": "artifact-state", "payload": {"publicationId": "2a91…2d4e", "state": "published-local", "providerState": null}}
{"kind": "artifact-state", "payload": {"publicationId": "2a91…2d4e", "state": "registered", "providerState": "pending"}}
// a failure emits its own state once, then the command fails with the matching code:
{"kind": "artifact-state", "payload": {"publicationId": "2a91…2d4e", "state": "outbox-escape", "providerState": null}}
{"kind": "artifact-state", "payload": {"publicationId": "2a91…2d4e", "state": "integrity-mismatch", "providerState": null}}
{"kind": "artifact-state", "payload": {"publicationId": "2a91…2d4e", "state": "refused", "providerState": null}}
{"kind": "artifact-state", "payload": {"publicationId": "2a91…2d4e", "state": "duplicate-publication", "providerState": "pending"}}

// publications_list -> every record of the active project after the restart replay, plus any
// registration-pending recovery could not complete; a fresh project is [] and writes nothing
[{"publicationId": "2a91…2d4e", "state": "registered", "reference": {"kind": "artifact", "id": "2a91…2d4e",
  "locator": "main/2a91…2d4e"}, "providerState": "pending", "catalogId": "main/2a91…2d4e",
  "names": ["report.pdf", "report-copy.pdf"], "availability": "local"}]

// ShellErrors (fixed catalogue messages, no slash, no path, no digest; only duplicate-publication carries detail)
{"code": "duplicate-publication", "message": "an artifact with this content is already registered for this project",
 "detail": {"publicationId": "2a91…2d4e", "catalogId": "main/2a91…2d4e"}}
{"code": "integrity-mismatch", "message": "the published copy did not verify against the approved digest; it was discarded and the entry preserved"}
{"code": "outbox-escape", "message": "the entry's path no longer names the approved file; the approved bytes were kept as a recovery entry"}
{"code": "outbox-linked", "message": "the entry's link count is greater than one"}
{"code": "refused", "message": "the candidate's digest does not match the approval request"}
{"code": "refused", "message": "only a generated-heavy candidate can be approved for publication"}
{"code": "refused", "message": "the entry's identity facts changed since it was approved"}
{"code": "work-area-invalid", "message": "the product work area resolves inside a registered workspace root"}
{"code": "destination-invalid", "message": "the project declares no asset root"}
{"code": "destination-invalid", "message": "the device asset path resolves inside a registered workspace root"}
{"code": "catalog-unavailable", "message": "the catalog is corrupt"}
{"code": "invalid-request", "message": "no approval with that id is recorded"}
// artifact_approve or artifact_publish while any supervised process is running (spike default,
// renderer risk review R1-001): the surface is frozen until every run has reached its terminal state
{"code": "run-active", "message": "a run is active; approve or publish once it has ended"}
```

`ShellErrorCode` gains exactly nine values: `integrity-mismatch`, `duplicate-publication`, `work-area-invalid`, `destination-invalid`, `outbox-escape`, `outbox-linked`, `refused`, `catalog-unavailable`, `run-active`. `ShellErrorDetail` is now `{recordedSha256Short, observedSha256Short}` for `changed-since-approval` (unchanged) or `{publicationId, catalogId}` for `duplicate-publication`. `artifact_approve` with a run the table does not remember, a name its inventory did not list, a malformed digest, or a colliding derived id is `invalid-request`; `artifact_publish` with a malformed or unrecorded approval id, or another project's, is `invalid-request`; all three commands with no active workspace are `workspace-unavailable`; a policy that cannot be loaded is `outbox-invalid` as in 5a.

The policy file gains one optional field, and the two files this slice writes look like this:

```jsonc
// .omnifrons/asset-policy.json: assetRootId is one token, never a path
{"schema": 1, "outbox": ".omnifrons/outbox", "assetRootId": "main", "rows": []}

// .omnifrons/catalog.jsonl (D9 default): one record line, then an alias line from a later identical publication
{"schema":1,"event":"record","record":{"publicationId":"2a91…2d4e","catalogId":"main/2a91…2d4e","assetRootId":"main",
 "class":"generated-heavy","type":"pdf","size":37,"digest":{"algorithm":"sha256","hex":"061eb0a8…"},
 "names":["report.pdf"],"relationships":[],
 "provenance":{"scopeId":"<project identity hex>","producer":{"kind":"run","runId":"run-1725782401-000000001-0",
   "adapterId":"claude-code","executableApprovalId":42},
   "transitions":[{"state":"published-local","at":{"secs":1725782401,"nanos":0}},{"state":"registered","at":{"secs":1725782401,"nanos":0}}]},
 "provider":{"adapterId":"local-dir","locator":"local-dir:main/2a91…2d4e","confirmationKind":null,"state":"pending","reason":"no remote"},
 "state":"registered","recordVersion":1}}
{"schema":1,"event":"alias","publicationId":"2a91…2d4e","name":"report-copy.pdf","at":{"secs":1725782402,"nanos":0}}
// an unattributed entry's producer carries the run subdirectory as a location fact only (HAP-001-R36)
// "producer":{"kind":"unattributed","foundUnderRunId":"run-1725782401-000000001-0"}

// <work area>/journal/publications.jsonl: the approval, then one line per step
{"schema":1,"event":"approved","approvalId":"23121521465ad9be","publicationId":"2a91…2d4e","projectId":"<project identity hex>",
 "runId":"run-1725782401-000000001-0","name":"run-1725782401-000000001-0/report.pdf","displayName":"report.pdf",
 "sha256":"061eb0a8…","size":37,"detectedType":"pdf","class":"generated-heavy","attribution":{"kind":"run","runId":"run-1725782401-000000001-0"},
 "assetRootId":"main","adapterId":"claude-code","executableApprovalId":42,"approvedAt":{"secs":1725782401,"nanos":0}}
{"schema":1,"event":"step","publicationId":"2a91…2d4e","approvalId":"23121521465ad9be","step":"published-local","outcome":"ok",
 "digest":"061eb0a8…","reason":null,"at":{"secs":1725782401,"nanos":0},"record":{ /* the record above, state published-local */ },"recovery":null}
{"schema":1,"event":"step","publicationId":"2a91…2d4e","approvalId":"23121521465ad9be","step":"registered","outcome":"ok",
 "digest":"061eb0a8…","reason":null,"at":{"secs":1725782401,"nanos":0},"record":null,"recovery":null}
{"schema":1,"event":"step","publicationId":"2a91…2d4e","approvalId":"23121521465ad9be","step":"cleanup","outcome":"ok",
 "digest":"061eb0a8…","reason":null,"at":{"secs":1725782401,"nanos":0},"record":null,"recovery":null}
// an escape at publish time: the recovery entry named by the held bytes' digest under <work area>/recovery/
{"schema":1,"event":"step","publicationId":"2a91…2d4e","approvalId":"23121521465ad9be","step":"outbox-escape","outcome":"failed",
 "digest":"061eb0a8…","reason":"path-names-a-different-file","at":{"secs":1725782401,"nanos":0},"record":null,"recovery":"061eb0a8…"}
```

Neither file carries a device path: the journal names the project by its identity, the outbox entry by its outbox-relative name, and the copy by its locator.

### Evidence procedures

#### Procedure notes for VP-S22

[VP-S22](heavy-asset-publication.md#desktop-stack-verification-planmd) (outbox publication end to end) has its dev-mode analogue in `publishing_an_approval_registers_the_artifact_and_emits_the_transitions` (`src-tauri/src/ipc/publication.rs`) and `a_publication_over_the_real_adapters_registers_and_removes_the_entry` (`crates/omnifrons-adapters/tests/publication_fs.rs`). Against a packaged build with a policy declaring `assetRootId`: launch `fake-agent --write-outbox 3`, call `artifact_approve` with `artifact-0.pdf`'s row from `candidates_list { runId }` and its digest read independently from the file, then `artifact_publish`; the `onState` frames must be exactly `published-local` then `registered`; the returned `reference` must be `{ kind: "artifact", id, locator }` with `locator` equal to `<assetRootId>/<id>`; the record line in `.omnifrons/catalog.jsonl` must carry the file's independently computed digest under `digest.hex`, `provenance.producer.kind: "run"` with the launch's run id, adapter id, and executable approval id, and `names: ["artifact-0.pdf"]`; the copy at `<app-data>/asset-roots/<assetRootId>/<id>` must hash to the same digest; the entry must be gone from the run subdirectory only after both, with the journal showing `published-local`, `registered`, `cleanup ok` in that order (on Windows the entry stays and the journal shows `cleanup deferred identity-check-unavailable` — record it as the disclosed residual, never as a pass); approving `notes.md` must fail `refused`; a display name carrying `../`, `CON`, U+202E, or 300 characters must appear sanitized in `names` (`DisplayName::sanitize`'s cases in `crates/omnifrons-domain/tests/publication.rs`); and the rendering-class half of VP-S22 has no implementation to test yet.

#### Procedure notes for VP-S24

[VP-S24](heavy-asset-publication.md#desktop-stack-verification-planmd) (interrupted publication and idempotent retry) has two dev-mode analogues: `registration_resumes_from_the_journal_after_a_failure_with_no_second_copy` (`crates/omnifrons-app/tests/publication.rs`, the Catalog failing once) and `publications_list_resumes_a_pending_registration_from_the_journal` (the shell, the journal cut after `published-local` and the record erased). Against a packaged build: kill the product after the `published-local` frame and before `registered` (the window is the Catalog write; widen it with a slow disk or a read-only `.omnifrons/` toggled back), restart, pick the workspace, and call `publications_list`: the publication must be listed `registered` with a reference, `.omnifrons/catalog.jsonl` must hold exactly one record and the asset root exactly one copy, the journal must show `registered` with reason `resumed-after-restart` and `cleanup deferred no-handle-after-restart`, and the outbox entry must still be present (its removal after a restart is debt, below). Then repeat `artifact_approve` and `artifact_publish` for the same bytes under another name: `duplicate-publication` with the existing ids in `detail`, one record, one copy, the new name as an alias. The kill-after-registration-before-upload half has no upload to interrupt in this slice.

#### Procedure notes for VP-S25

[VP-S25](heavy-asset-publication.md#desktop-stack-verification-planmd) (provider unavailable, non-retryable refusal, relocation) cannot run against this slice: the only adapter has no remote, so `provider-unavailable`, `provider-failed`, and `relocated-pending-upload` have no implementation. What the slice fixes for the procedure is the shape the record and the frames will carry when one exists: `provider_state: pending` with the adapter's own reason token (`no remote` here) is what a record looks like before any confirmation, `confirm` returning nothing is what "not synced" means, and `providerState` rides every `registered` frame. Local publication and registration proceeding with the provider unreachable (HAP-001-R30) is, in this slice, the only path there is.

### Deviations and debt

Deviations from the brief, each with its reason:
