- **Eight `ShellErrorCode`s, not four.** The brief named `integrity-mismatch`, `duplicate-publication`, `work-area-invalid`, and `destination-invalid`. `outbox-escape` and `outbox-linked` are needed because a publish-time identity or link-count failure is neither of those and HAP-001-R18 and R20 forbid rendering them as `integrity-mismatch`; `refused` because an entry that cannot be approved, a mismatched digest, and a re-opened entry whose facts changed are HAP-001's `refused`, which the brief itself named as the outcome; `catalog-unavailable` because a corrupt or unwritable `.omnifrons/catalog.jsonl` is none of the others.
- **The approval store is the journal.** The brief asked for the approval "recorded (append-only JSONL under the work area, 0600)" and for the work area to hold "`journal/`, `recovery/`, and nothing else". Both hold: the approval is HAP-001's step 6 and is the publication journal's first entry for the publication. The derived id is checked against the journal before the line is appended.
- **`BlobStorePort` stages instead of copying.** The brief's `publish(handle, publication identity) -> locator` would have put the copy — and its digest — inside the adapter. The port hands the transaction a staged sink so the bytes leave the held handle exactly once, hashed as they go, in the application layer; a real provider adapter (5c) gets the verified local copy to upload from, which is what HAP-001's step 9 needs anyway.
- **`artifact.state` rides the command's channel.** The brief's "event kind on every transition" is a per-publish `Channel<ArtifactStateFrame>` argument, the `harness_spawn` pattern, rather than an application-wide event: testable without a runtime, and the transitions of a publication belong to the command that runs it. Recovery transitions at `publications_list` therefore have no channel and are journaled only.
- **The restart replay runs at the first publication command, not at process start.** No workspace is active at start and the Catalog is the project's, so `recover` runs when `publications_list` or `artifact_publish` is called for the active project — every completed step is still never repeated. Recovery cannot remove the outbox entry (no handle survives a restart) and says so in the journal; the entry is retained.
- **Only a remembered run's inventory is approvable** (`runId` is required). A whole-outbox entry (a root entry, or one under a run the table forgot) has no recorded facts to bind an approval to; approving it would mean probing at approval time and again at publish time. Debt for 5c.
- **`PublicationDto` carries `names` and `availability` beyond the brief's five fields**, and `approvalId` is a hex string rather than the slice-2 numeric shape: the names are the sanitized record names the renderer will show, availability is HAP-001-R27's per-device observation, and a derived `u64` id cannot round-trip through a JSON number.
- **`destination-unconfigured` is folded into `destination-invalid`.** The asset binding HAP-001-R6 refers to does not exist; a policy without `assetRootId` fails with a distinct fixed message under the same code.
- **The Windows residual widens.** Beyond 5a's list, the identity check before publication and before cleanup is `Unverifiable` on Windows: the copy still comes from the held handle and verifies, and the entry is retained with `cleanup deferred identity-check-unavailable`. Recorded under HAP-001-R19, not closed. And `running_ids` inherits the supervisor's Windows placeholder: `stop` there reports `OrphanRiskUncertain` and never evicts the entry, so after a stop the publication surface stays frozen (`run-active`) for the rest of the process on Windows until Job Object containment lands; `tests/running_ids.rs` asserts that documented behavior per platform.
- **The duplicate's own entry is left in the outbox.** HAP-001-R23 says a repeat acknowledges the existing record; nothing in the contract removes the second entry, and this slice does not.
- **`WorkspaceDto` gains `workArea`** (R1-002), an additive field on a slice-3 shape, chosen over a diagnostic frame because `workspace_pick` has no channel and the state belongs to the workspace it was checked against.
- **A registered record's provenance is written only on success** (R3-002): the `registered` transition is pushed onto the record the Catalog writes, and a record returned `registration-pending` ends at `published-local`.
- **The publication surface is frozen while any supervised process runs** (renderer risk review R1-001, a spike default): `artifact_approve` and `artifact_publish` refuse with `run-active` while `TokioProcessSupervisor::running_ids` -- the same source `harness_stop` and `harness_observe` consult, each child refreshed through `observe`'s reap check -- is non-empty, and are open again once every run has reached its terminal state. The renderer freezes Approve and Publish while a run is active; without the backend mirror, direct IPC would bypass that guard, so one coherent frozen surface exists now. TM-001-R1's facts are re-verified by the transaction regardless of this guard, and the owner may relax it: publishing a finished run's candidates while another run streams is a legitimate future option.

A review of this half was applied before any commit (risk lens: one critical, one warning, one suggestion; reliability lens: one critical, six warnings, one suggestion), adding fifteen tests. Behavior changes, each observed red first: the surface lock and the pre-handle duplicate acknowledgement (two concurrency tests, red `assertion left == right failed: got [Ok(… Registered …), Ok(… Registered …)] left: (2, 0) right: (1, 1)` — both threads registered), the work-area re-check on activation (red `error[E0061]: this function takes 3 arguments but 4 arguments were supplied` and `no field work_area on type WorkspaceDto`, plus `no associated function … check_configured` at the app level), the per-call staging name (red `two staged copies, two staging files, got [".eeee….part"] left: 1 right: 2`), and the `registered` transition written only on success (red `no transition to a state that was never reached — left: [PublishedLocal, Registered] right: [PublishedLocal]`). Pins green on arrival and recorded as such: the crash window between `commit` and the `published-local` journal line (an `InMemoryJournal::fail_next_append` knob; the retry re-stages the same identity and the store replaces the copy — one object, one record; `blob_store_contract` now asserts the replacement for both stores), the exact 255-byte display-name boundary, the three catalog corruption branches (two records with one identity, an alias naming no record, a `catalogId` disagreeing with its identity), a directory and — on unix — a FIFO as the held handle (`outbox-escape`, nothing staged, no recovery entry), another project's approval (`invalid-request`, nothing of the other project's touched), and leading whitespace trimmed from a display name (kept, and now documented: it would only hide where the name starts). The publication suite's `#![cfg(feature = "contract-tests")]` gating is now stated in [repository-layout.md](repository-layout.md) § Build and test commands: run it with `--all-features`, as CI does; a bare `cargo test -p omnifrons-app --test publication` runs zero tests. One wire follow-up came from the renderer half: `artifact_approve` names the candidate by its full digest while `candidates_list` carried only `sha256Short`, so `CandidateDto` gains `sha256` — the full 64 lowercase hex characters, `null` for a refused entry, beside the unchanged `sha256Short` (red: both shape tests failed on the missing key, `left: {… "sha256Short": "abababab" …} right: {… "sha256": "abab…ab", "sha256Short": …}`); the digest is identity evidence, not a secret (`EvidenceDto.sha256` already carries the executable's under TM-001-R7), and the row's keys are asserted to be exactly the eight named, none a path. Two more came from the renderer's risk review: the backend mirror of the frozen surface (`run-active`, above; red `error[E0432]: unresolved import super::RunActivity` at the shell and `error[E0599]: no method named running_ids` at the supervisor, then proven with a fake activity source and, on unix, a real `sleep` child spawned and stopped through the supervisor), and `OutboxStatusDto.assetRootId` so the destination is on the approval surface before the decision (HAP-001-R22; red on the shape tests' missing key).

Debt and open owner decisions this slice names without deciding:

- **`catalog_repair`** (R1-001): a `Corrupt` catalog is repaired by hand today; a command applying the same three rules is 5c debt, as is a sweep of staging files a crash leaves under a device asset path.

- **Provider upload, `provider-synced`, `provider-pending` as a state, `over-limit`, the secret scan (R41), and `locator-broken`** are not implemented; the local-directory adapter is dev-mode only and a real adapter (the Proton Drive CLI) is 5c.
- **The project identity is a canonical-path digest**, standing in for the registered scope identity; HAP-001's cross-project separation is only as strong as that identity, which a path digest does not make non-forgeable. The context-orb scope is the owner's.
- **No device identity**, so `availability` is not written into the portable record (HAP-001-R27 asks for per-device observations there); this device reports its own from its journal.
- **Producer identity (`producer_id`, `producer_instance`)** is not recorded: no producer signing exists in the repository (the renderer's producer line is still "(unknown)"); the record carries the run id, adapter id, and executable approval only.
- **Recovery entries are written but never re-published**: HAP-001-R18 requires a fresh explicit approval for one, and no command approves a recovery entry yet.
- **The vault** does not exist in this repository; every containment check runs against the active workspace root only, and the shell has a single active workspace, so "every registered workspace root" is one root.
- **One asset root per project** is assumed by `publications_list`'s recovery (the policy's); records for another asset root would fail their copy verification during recovery and be journaled `integrity-mismatch`.
- **Not exercised by any test**: the three Tauri commands as commands (their pure bodies are); frame delivery over a live `Channel`; every Windows code path (cross-compiled, never executed) and every macOS execution; two shells against one journal (no single-instance guard, as in slice 2); a crash between a step and its journal line other than the `commit` → `published-local` window (that one is pinned above with the `fail_next_append` knob; the others are reasoned about, not driven); the stale `.part` collision after a crash; files larger than a few dozen bytes; and `write_recovery_entry`'s same-digest-already-recovered branch.
- **HAP-001 D1, D3, D8, D9, D13** remain the owner's calls; this slice assumes their default proposals and the asset root in the policy, and says so here rather than presenting them as decided.

### Renderer

The renderer half of this slice landed after the Rust half above, against exactly the wire shapes in [IPC shapes](#ipc-shapes-3), the `workArea` field the review added to `WorkspaceDto` included. `renderer/src/ipc/harness.ts` gains the three commands as typed wrappers — `artifactApprove(runId, name, sha256)` invoking `artifact_approve` with exactly `{ runId, name, sha256 }`, `artifactPublish(approvalId, onState)` constructing a fresh `Channel<ArtifactStateFrame>` and invoking `artifact_publish` with exactly `{ approvalId, onState }` (the `harnessSpawn` pattern), and `publicationsList()` invoking `publications_list` with no arguments — and the types mirroring `dto.rs` field-for-field: `ArtifactState` (the ten tokens), `ProviderState` (four), `Availability` (`local` | `unknown`), `ActAs` (`device-local-user`), `ArtifactApprovalId` and `PublicationId` (opaque strings, 16 and 64 hex), `PortableReference` (`{ kind: 'artifact', id, locator }`), `ArtifactApproval` (thirteen fields), `Publication` (seven, `reference`/`providerState`/`catalogId` nullable), `ArtifactStateFrame` (`{ kind: 'artifact-state', payload: { publicationId, state, providerState } }`), `WorkAreaState` (`valid` | `work-area-invalid`) as the required `Workspace.workArea`, `Candidate.sha256: string | null` — the full 64-hex digest beside `sha256Short`, `null` for a refused entry, which the Rust half added to the row during this half's review so an approval can be made from the row itself: identity evidence, not a secret, under the same TM-001-R7 display precedent as `Evidence.sha256` on the executable approval surface, and the fact `artifact_approve` names a candidate by — `OutboxStatus.assetRootId: string | null` (the policy's asset root identity token, the destination HAP-001-R22 shows before the decision; `null` when the policy declares none or could not be loaded; added by the same review), and the nine new `ShellErrorCode`s, `run-active` among them (the shell's mirror of the renderer's frozen surface: `artifact_approve` and `artifact_publish` refuse while any supervised process runs — a wait, not a verdict). Two typing decisions go beyond the field-for-field mirror, each stated here: `ArtifactStateFrame` is its own one-kind type and not a member of `AgentEvent` — it rides the publish command's channel, `AdapterEventDto` never carries it, and the adapter channel's union should not admit a kind the Rust side never sends there (the brief's "`AgentEvent`/frame kind `artifact-state` as delivered on the publish channel" read with its last clause as the defining one); and `ShellError` becomes a union discriminated on `code` (`changed-since-approval` with `ChangedSinceApprovalDetail`, `duplicate-publication` with `DuplicatePublicationDetail`, every other code with no detail) — the renderer's typing of `dto.rs`'s untagged `ShellErrorDetail` plus the invariant its doc states, so `HarnessPanel`'s and `ApprovalSurface`'s existing `error.code === 'changed-since-approval' && error.detail` narrowing compiles unchanged and a `duplicate-publication` banner cannot read a digest field. `harness.test.ts` (38 → 52) asserts the wrapper argument shapes (`Object.keys(args).sort()` exactly `['name', 'runId', 'sha256']`; exactly `['approvalId', 'onState']` with `onState.onmessage` a function; exactly `{}`), the returned payloads verbatim with their key sets (thirteen approval keys, seven publication keys, `['id', 'kind', 'locator']` on the reference, the bare `['kind']` of an unattributed attribution, the eight candidate keys with `sha256` 64 hex on a validated row and `null` on a refused one), frames delivered through the live publish `Channel` in order with payload keys exactly `['providerState', 'publicationId', 'state']`, the typed rejections (`refused`, `destination-invalid`, `duplicate-publication` with its two-key detail, `integrity-mismatch` with none, `catalog-unavailable`, `work-area-invalid`, and `run-active` on both commands), `workArea` on both workspace wrappers, `assetRootId` on the outbox status with its seven keys, and four compile-time exhaustiveness guards (`ArtifactState` ten; `ProviderState` four and `Availability` two; the whole thirty-two-code `ShellErrorCode` union with `run-active` included, plus the one frame kind — R3-025; the two `workArea` tokens). The wrapper tests' red was a runtime red (`TypeError: artifactApprove is not a function` ×3, `TypeError: artifactPublish is not a function` ×4, `TypeError: publicationsList is not a function` ×2; 9 failed, 41 passed); the types were type-level reds only, as in slices 4 and 5 — `tsc -b` reported 28 errors (nine `TS2305: Module '"./harness"' has no exported member …`, twelve `Type '"<code>"' is not assignable to type 'ShellErrorCode'`, three `is not assignable to type 'never'`, one `'publicationId' does not exist in type 'ShellErrorDetail'`, one implicit `any`) and, for `workArea`, four `Property 'workArea' does not exist on type 'Workspace'`, and, for `Candidate.sha256`, eight (six `'sha256' does not exist in type 'Candidate'` on the fixtures, two `Property 'sha256' does not exist on type 'Candidate'`), and, for the review round's `run-active` and `assetRootId`, ten (five `'assetRootId' does not exist in type 'OutboxStatus'`, one `Property 'assetRootId' does not exist on type 'OutboxStatus'`, three `Type '"run-active"' is not assignable to type 'ShellErrorCode'`, one `Type '"run-active"' is not comparable to type 'ShellErrorCode'` from the exhaustive switch) — while Vitest was green on those tests, recorded here rather than dressed up as a runtime red.

`renderer/src/AgentPanel.tsx` is the only component touched; there is no new component file. **The approval affordance is the first artifact write path in the renderer**: every earlier surface only showed what a harness produced, and this one lets a human authorize a publication — so the constraints below are the point of the slice, not decoration. Five additions, each behind exact copy. (1) The **candidates table** loses the fixed `publication not available in this slice` line and gains an eighth column, `action`: a row in state `candidate` with class `generated-heavy` — attributed or unattributed alike, HAP-001 admitting an unattributed entry to explicit human approval with the unattributed fact in view — carries an **Approve** button; a refused row (`outbox-escape`, `outbox-linked`), a `candidate` row of any other class, and a row whose `sha256` is `null` carry nothing. That is the implementer's reading of the brief's "rows in any non-candidate state and unattributed rows still get Approve … but rows in refused states get no button at all": `CandidateState` is closed at three, the two non-`candidate` states are exactly the refused ones the same sentence excludes, and the shell refuses every other class as `refused`, so the panel offers no button it knows the shell would refuse — the advisory client-side filter the prompt-size cap already sets the precedent for. Every button is disabled while a run is active or an approve is in flight; the inventory arrives before the run's terminal frame, so the buttons are visibly frozen in that window and enabled once the run ends (publication of a finished run's candidates is allowed only when no run is active — a spike default). (2) Approve opens a **publication approval block** (`<div aria-label="Publication approval">`, beside the table inside the Candidates region and outside the transcript, RCS-001-R6) listing the identity-bound facts verbatim from the candidate row, one `<p>` each through `PlainTextLine`: `name: <name>`, `class: <class>`, `type: <detectedType>`, `size: <size>`, `sha256: <sha256>` (the full digest — identity evidence on the surface, the `Evidence.sha256` precedent), `sha256 short: <sha256Short>`, `attribution: run <runId>` or `attribution: unattributed` (HAP-001-R22's "the producing run or the unattributed fact"), then `destination: asset root <assetRootId>` — the policy's asset root identity from the current outbox status, or `destination: unconfigured` when the policy declares none and `destination: unknown` while no status is known, both of which keep the final button disabled, since the shell would refuse with `destination-invalid` (renderer review R1-002/R3-023: R22's destination on the surface before the decision) — then `scope: <scopeMode>` — the scope mode of the adapter the run was started with, from its descriptor (`advisory`, `harness-enforced`, or `sandbox-enforced`, fixed copy per closed token through an exhaustive switch; no publication DTO carries a scope, so this is renderer-side, and it follows the producing launch rather than the selection at approval time) — then the fixed act-as line `act as: device-local-user` (TM-001-R7's act-as identity, stated before the decision; `device-local-user` is the one `ActAs` token the shell can bind, so the line is the renderer's own constant, never wire text), then the label `Type the short digest (<sha256Short>) to approve`, an input that opens empty, and the final button **Approve publication**. Opening the block invokes nothing. **The confirmation is slice 2's:** the user types the row's own eight-character `sha256Short`, and the final button is enabled only when the typed value equals it exactly, case-sensitively — the fixed phrase `approve`, uppercase hex, another row's short digest, a seven- or nine-character prefix, and the full digest all leave it disabled (R1-001: no shortcut that confirms without having read the evidence). The click calls `artifactApprove(runId, name, sha256)` with the inventory's run id (the `candidates` event's — the shell's minted token, never a path), the row's name exactly as `candidates_list` listed it, and **the row's own full `sha256` — never the typed value and never a proposal's digest**: the typed value is a gate, the request's digest is the identity fact the shell itself listed, and the shell re-verifies it against its inventory (`refused` on a mismatch). The full digest travels on the row for that reason — it is what `artifact_approve` names a candidate by — and may, because it is identity evidence and not a secret, under the same TM-001-R7 display precedent as `Evidence.sha256`. The name identifies the entry the request is about; it never pre-fills, enables, or triggers anything. On success the row's action cell reads `approved <approvalId> — <displayName>, asset root <assetRootId>` — the sanitized display name of HAP-001-R24, known only from the approval's response, beside the destination the block already showed, echoed as the shell recorded it — followed by a **Publish** button; the Approve button is gone and the block closes. On a rejection the row keeps Approve, the block stays open with the typed digest kept and the final button enabled again, and the banner shows the code. Clicking another row's Approve switches the block to that row's facts and clears the input. (3) **Publish** calls `artifactPublish(approvalId, onState)`; each `artifact-state` frame renders as the transcript line `publication <first eight characters of the publication identity>: <state>`, the token transcribed verbatim through an exhaustive switch over the ten ("fixed copy per token" in the sense every state cell of this panel already uses — `docs/target-architecture.md` invariant 8 — rather than an English phrase per token), and the resolved `PublicationDto` becomes the row's action cell `<state> <short publication id>` (`registered 2a91ea59`, or `registration-pending 2a91ea59`) with no button, plus a row of the Publications table. (4) The **Publications table**: a `<section aria-label="Publications">` beside the transcript and the candidates table, never inside either, columns `names`, `state`, `provider state`, `reference`, `catalog id`, `availability` — the sanitized display names joined by `, `, the state token, the provider state, the reference's locator in full (display-only: nothing builds a link, a path, or an address from it), the Catalog identity's short form `<asset root id>/<first eight hex>` (`main/2a91ea59`), and this device's availability — every cell through `PlainTextLine`, a `null` fact (no record before registration) as `—`. `publicationsList()` fills it on mount and again after a successful workspace pick through a sequenced fetch (R3-008); the shell's `workspace-unavailable` answer while no workspace is active renders no table and no alert, as for the outbox line; an empty list renders the header row alone; a completed publication is merged in from `artifact_publish`'s own response unless a refresh started since, whose own response then defines the table. (5) The **work-area line** the Rust review added: a `<p role="status" aria-label="Work area">` beside the outbox line reading `work area invalid — publication refused until it is fixed` while `Workspace.workArea` is `work-area-invalid` (fixed copy chosen per closed token by an exhaustive switch; the raw token is never rendered), nothing while `valid`; it follows `workspace_current` on mount and every `workspace_pick`.

**Banners.** The nine codes render through the unchanged banner as `<code>: <message>` — the shell's fixed catalogue messages through `PlainTextLine` (`run-active: a run is active; approve or publish once it has ended` among them) — with no "untrusted" prefix for any of them: `DENIAL_CODES` stays the four `LaunchGate` denials. The decision the brief asked for: `refused`, `outbox-escape`, `outbox-linked`, and `integrity-mismatch` are refusals of an entry (a mismatched digest, a swapped path, a link count, an unverified copy), not verdicts on a producer, and HAP-001-R22's own "untrusted producer" refusal is about feed proposals, which this surface never carries; `duplicate-publication`, `work-area-invalid`, `destination-invalid`, `catalog-unavailable`, and `run-active` are not refusals of anything — the last is a wait. Only `duplicate-publication`'s `detail` renders, as ` (publication <short id>, catalog <short catalog id>)` — HAP-001-R23's acknowledgement of the existing record by its two identities in short form, the full ones absent — and a stray `detail` on any other code renders nothing (at the type level it cannot exist). **Guards.** The run guards are unchanged; the approval block, every Approve button and every Publish button are disabled while a run is active; the row buttons and the final button are disabled while an approve is in flight (one `artifact_approve` per double-click) and Publish while its own publish is (one `artifact_publish`); every new async path has the `mountedRef` unmount guard and a stale-response guard — `publications_list` sequenced by request id like `outbox_status`; `artifact_approve` and `artifact_publish` gated on an applied-inventory counter bumped whenever a fetched inventory replaces the rows, so a response landing after a new run's inventory — even one listing the same names — touches no row, while a rejected Start, which restores the same inventory with its approvals and open block intact (the R3-019 restore, now carrying the per-row state), still lets a response apply; transcript lines and the banner gated on the spawn generation the click happened under, so a new run's transcript never receives an earlier publication's lines; the Publications-table merge gated on the publications request sequence. **Content security.** A candidate name `../../<b>etc</b>/pass<ESC>wd<U+202E>` renders in the block as `name: ../../<b>etc</b>/passwd` with no `<b>`, no `<a>`, no `[href]`, no ESC and no override (the traversal is data; the renderer never resolves it), a `detectedType` of `<b>pdf</b>` as text, and the label's short digest as text; a Publications display name carrying the same payload renders as text, and the locator and catalog id as text with no anchor; a publish proposal naming the very row by the very digest the row carries leaves the input empty and the final button disabled; a programmatic `input.value = <short digest>` with no input event leaves it disabled — React's controlled input never sees it — and only the user's own typing enables it (TM-001-R1: no content path pre-fills, enables, or triggers the approval; a proposal's digest is never read for it, and the request carries the row's digest, not the input's); the candidates region's only interactive elements are the action column's buttons, the header row and every data cell are inert, and clicking anywhere in the region — the disabled Approve buttons included — invokes nothing. **TM-001-R7** is met by displaying identity evidence (name, class, detected type, size, digest, attribution) and the act-as identity before the decision, with no harness-authored summary anywhere on the surface and a confirmation that cannot be completed without the digest; **TM-001-R1** by the absence of any path from a name, a proposal, a note, or a harness string to the input, the button, or the call.

`AgentPanel.test.tsx` grows by fifty-nine tests (126 → 185) and `harness.test.ts` by fourteen (38 → 52); `app.test.tsx` keeps its five with its mount mock answering `publications_list` the way the shell does with no workspace (a `workspace-unavailable` rejection) — that mock was the red for the mount-time fetch at the `App` level (`renders no alert on mount` failing with `expected <div role="alert" …(1)>…(2)</div> to be null`, the mock's fallback `invalid-request` banner), green once it answered; every default and custom mock in `AgentPanel.test.tsx` answers `publications_list` too, and every workspace fixture carries `workArea: 'valid'`. Each behavior was written before its implementation and observed red — the publications table's eleven on `Unable to find role="region" and name "Publications"` ×4, `expected +0 to be 1` ×3 (no `publications_list` call on mount, after a pick, sequenced), `Unable to find role="alert"` ×2 and `expected false to be true` ×2 (the fetch never started, both unmount continuations); the four rewritten slice-5 table tests on `expected [ 'name', 'size', 'sha256', …(4) ] to deeply equal [ …(5) ]`, `expected <p></p> to be null`, `expected 7 to be 8`, and `expected [] to deeply equal [ 'BUTTON', 'BUTTON' ]`; the approval affordance's sixteen on `Unable to find an accessible element with the role "button" and name "Approve"` ×15 (three of them the `it.each` codes) and `expected [] to deeply equal [ true, true ]` (the frozen buttons); the publish's fourteen on `expected [] to have a length of 1 but got +0` ×13 (nothing invoked `artifact_publish`) and `expected false to be true` (Publish not frozen in flight), then, once the handler landed, the `duplicate-publication` banner's own red `expected 'duplicate-publication: an artifact wi…' to be 'duplicate-publication: an artifact wi…'` (the two short ids missing) before its detail rendered; the work-area line's two on `Unable to find role="status" and name "Work area"`; and the re-gate, once the Rust half had added `Candidate.sha256`, on `Unable to find a label with the text of: /Type the short digest/` ×28 (every test that types into the block, the publish tests included), `expected [ …(7) ] to deeply equal [ …(8) ]` (the block's two digest lines), `expected [ …(7) ] to include 'sha256: 1111…'`, and `expected 'Approve' to be ''` (a row whose `sha256` is `null`), with `tsc -b`'s eight `sha256`-on-`Candidate` errors as the type-level red — except two pins green on arrival and recorded as such: a valid `workArea` renders nothing, and a rejected Start restores the table with its approvals and open block. One test needed a test-side correction after its first run, not a component change: the pre-fill test's final "the user types it" step used the very string the programmatic injection had set on the node, which React's value tracker reports as no change, so the typed sequence passes through another value first — a test-harness detail, not a property of the gate, and said so in the test. A review round of this half (risk R1-002, with the Rust mirrors `run-active` and `OutboxStatus.assetRootId`; reliability R3-020 to R3-025) added thirteen tests. Behavior, red first: the block's `destination:` and `scope:` lines (`expected [ …(8) ] to deeply equal [ …(10) ]`, `expected [ …(8) ] to include 'destination: unconfigured'`, `… 'destination: unknown'`, `… 'scope: sandbox-enforced'`). Test-only, green on arrival and recorded as such: the `run-active` banner from both commands (the banner is code-agnostic); the two-row approve-and-publish cycles in both orders, each row's cell and Publications entry surviving the other's cycle (R3-020); every documented rejection code for `artifactApprove` (`outbox-invalid`, `workspace-unavailable`, `run-active` added) and `artifactPublish` (`invalid-request`, `workspace-unavailable`, `run-active` added) with the block-stays-usable and approved-row-intact assertions (R3-021); reject-after-unmount for both commands, no `console.error` and no alert (R3-022); the null-digest test varied over state and class and retitled (R3-024); and the eight-codes guard turned into the exhaustive switch above (R3-025). The default mock keeps its no-workspace answers; the approval helpers pass a valid outbox status whose policy declares the asset root `main`, since the block will not approve toward a destination it cannot show. Totals: `pnpm -r test` **252 → 325**, three plain runs and three `CI=true` runs all identical at 325/325, `AgentPanel.test.tsx` alone ten consecutive runs at 185/185, the whole battery repeated in full after the re-gate and again after the review round; `pnpm -r lint` and `pnpm -r build` (`tsc -b && vite build`) clean; no `.only`; provenance sweep empty.

Deviations from the renderer brief, each with its reason: the approvable set (above), narrowed further to rows whose `sha256` is not `null` once that field existed; `ArtifactStateFrame` kept off `AgentEvent` (above); `ShellError` discriminated on `code` (above); the transcript's publication line transcribing the token verbatim (above); the sanitized display name shown in the row after approval rather than in the block before the decision, because no query exposes it before `artifact_approve` answers (the destination stood there too until `outbox_status` carried `assetRootId`, after which it moved into the block — review R1-002); the scope line reading the descriptor of the adapter the run was started with rather than the current selection, the producing launch being what the approval is about; the row reading `<state> <short id>` after publishing, which the brief left unstated; and the one edit outside this subsection, the section's opening sentence, which said `renderer/` was untouched. Debt this half names without deciding: the destination line follows the outbox status fetched at mount or at the last pick, so a policy edited between that fetch and the approval shows the earlier asset root until the next pick (the shell's own check at approval is the authority); "Publish disabled while a run is active" is structurally unobservable (no approved row is visible during a run, since Start clears the table and a new inventory resets the row state) and therefore holds by construction rather than by a test; a publication's transcript lines vanish with the next Start while the Publications table persists; the Publications-table merge moves a re-published record to the end; the block has no Cancel (another row's Approve or a new inventory replaces it); a failed publish offers only the re-enabled Publish, the shell deciding whether the same approval may be retried; and the banner shows the shell's messages, so their wording is the Rust half's. Everything here stays dev-mode-only exactly as the Rust half states: no packaged build, no live shell exercised by any renderer test — every approval, frame, and record above came from a mocked `invoke`, and the shell's own re-verification of the row's digest has run against no renderer request here — and no producer signing (the producer line's "(unknown)" is unchanged).

### Relationship to VP-001

Every result behind this slice — every passing test listed above; `cargo fmt --all --check` clean; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` (Linux) clean; `cargo clippy -p omnifrons-supervisor -p omnifrons-adapters -p omnifrons-app --all-targets --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu -- -D warnings` clean (cross-compiled, never executed; the shell stays outside that check for the `windres` reason slice 3 recorded — which is exactly where CI's `windows-latest` leg caught what this machine cannot: a `large_enum_variant` on the shell's `RunEndPhase`, whose `Ready` input is wider on Windows, fixed by boxing that variant); `cargo test --workspace --all-targets --all-features` all green at **546** tests, summed over every test binary's own `passed` count the way slices 3 to 5 were (domain 70, app 158, adapters 112, supervisor 69, shell 137; +110 over slice 5a's 436: domain +17, app +34, adapters +25, supervisor +1, shell +33, of which 20 arrived with the review fixes and the renderer-review follow-ups recorded above — 526 before them); `cargo test -p omnifrons-shell --test feature_gate` green (2 passed); `cargo tree -p omnifrons-adapters -e normal --depth 1` still exactly `nix`, `omnifrons-app`, `omnifrons-domain`, `serde`, `serde_json`, `sha2`, and the supervisor's still exactly `nix`, `omnifrons-app`, `omnifrons-domain`, `tokio`, `tracing`; no new `unsafe` (the two precedents, `demo.rs` and `pty.rs`, are the only sites); the provenance sweep empty — was produced against a `cargo test` development build, on one contributor's Linux machine, not a packaged release build on a pinned Windows/macOS/Linux baseline, and not against macOS or Windows at all: the Windows clippy pass is a cross-compilation type-check, and the unix-gated link, hard-link, FIFO, and swap tests have not run on macOS here (CI's `macos-latest` leg is where they do). Per [VP-001-R2](desktop-stack-verification-plan.md#product-requirements), this is `dev-mode-only` and therefore **not** admissible as VP-S22, VP-S24, or VP-S25 evidence; HAP-001 is accepted, but its acceptance evidence is the checklist in its own § Acceptance evidence and follow-up, none of which a development build discharges. This slice is an input to writing those scenarios' procedures well (above), and, for VP-S24 specifically, to stating which step boundary a kill has to land on for the journal's resume point to be the one tested.

## Slice 5c — unmediated producers: unattributed approval and the guidance-note installer (HAP-001 D18)

This slice's Rust half (`omnifrons-domain`, `omnifrons-app`, `omnifrons-adapters`, `src-tauri`) implements the two halves of HAP-001's § Unmediated producers that 5b left as debt: (A) an entry the whole-outbox inventory listed — at the outbox root, or under a run subdirectory no remembered run's inventory covers — is approvable and publishable as unattributed (HAP-001-R11, R12, R17, R22, R36), and (B) the agent guidance note is offered, proposed, and written into the project's guidance file only as an explicit, user-approved workspace write under the installer discipline D18 names (HAP-001-R42, D18), with the outbox ignore rule as a second managed file (D17's proposed hygiene). Real providers, D16's wrong-root scan and `misplaced`, `catalog_repair`, the stale-`.part` sweep, recovery re-approval, git awareness, Windows owner-only ACLs, and nested guidance paths stay out (5d, 5e, or later). **Dev-mode only**: nothing here is VP-001 evidence ([Relationship to VP-001](#relationship-to-vp-001-6)). The renderer half landed after the Rust half, against the wire shapes in [IPC shapes](#ipc-shapes-4), and is recorded in [Renderer](#renderer-6).

### Scope

In, part A: `artifact_approve { runId: null, name, sha256 }` for an entry `candidates_list { runId: null }` listed in state `candidate` and class `generated-heavy`; at approval time the entry is re-opened once under the single-handle discipline — one no-follow open relative to the outbox handle (or the run subdirectory opened without following a link at its name), a regular file, a link count of one on unix, the digest from that handle equal to the request's — and the approval binds the facts so taken (HAP-001-R15, R16, R20, R22); the held handle then follows the run entry's path (held through approval, copied from at publication, the 32-handle cap of D22 counting it); the approval and the Catalog record carry `attribution: unattributed`, `producer: unattributed` with the run subdirectory, when there was one, as `foundUnderRunId` only (HAP-001-R36), no run id, adapter id, or executable-approval reference standing in, and `ArtifactApprovalDto.runId: null`. In, part B: the fixed template verbatim with only the outbox path substituted (HAP-001-R42), the user-named guidance file at the workspace root under RCS-001's file-name rule, sentinels naming the kind, the template version, and the body digest, a modified or malformed block refused rather than replaced, the snapshot before any write (deduplicated, five unpinned kept per file, pinnable, restorable), a removal deleting only the lines Omnifrons owns, an idempotent re-apply, the write bound to the file digest the surface showed, the work area re-checked at every use (HAP-001-R7), and every write refused while a run is active.

### Design decisions (spike defaults, each disclosed)

Every default below is the spike's; a feature that assumes it before the owner disposes must say so, and this section does.

- **A1 — the approval names no run** (`crates/omnifrons-domain/src/publication.rs`, `crates/omnifrons-app/src/publication.rs`, `crates/omnifrons-adapters/src/jsonl_publication_journal.rs`). `ArtifactApproval.run_id` becomes `Option<RunId>`: the run whose run-end inventory listed the candidate, `None` for an entry approved from the whole-outbox inventory, which lists without a run. `ArtifactApproval::found_under()` is the location fact HAP-001-R36 asks for: the listing run, or, when there is none, the run id the outbox-relative name carries as a prefix (`<run id>/<file>`, the shape the inventories themselves build), `None` for a root entry; `build_record` writes it as `producer.foundUnderRunId`. The journal's `approved` line carries `runId: null` for such an approval; a line written before this slice always carries a run and replays unchanged.
- **A2 — the whole-outbox path at approval** (`src-tauri/src/ipc/publication.rs`, `outbox_state.rs`). With `runId` absent or `null` the request's name is parsed as an inventory spells it — at most one `/`, a run id before it, one file-name component that is not empty, `.`, or `..` and carries no separator on any platform (a backslash refused too, so a name means one component everywhere) — else `invalid-request`. An entry under a run the table remembers *with its inventory* is refused as `invalid-request` ("approve it through that run id"): that inventory's attribution and provenance are the ones to approve under, and approving it unattributed would discard a proposal the run made; a remembered run whose inventory failed is treated as forgotten. The policy is loaded (`outbox-invalid` otherwise), the asset root taken from it (`destination-invalid` when it declares none), the outbox opened without creating it (`invalid-request` when it does not exist yet; `outbox-invalid`/`outbox-unavailable` as 5a maps them), the run subdirectory opened relative to the outbox handle without following a link (`invalid-request` when there is none), and the entry probed through `FsCandidateProber` — the same open-once discipline as the run-end inventory: a digest other than the request's is `integrity-mismatch` (the listing's digest is what the approval binds to; the entry changed since), a link or a non-regular file `outbox-escape` (never dereferenced), a link count above one `outbox-linked`, a name that cannot be opened `invalid-request`; the class is computed from the probe with the policy's classifier and anything but `generated-heavy` is `refused`. The approval is journaled first; only then is the re-opened handle held. **The held handles live in the run table**: `RunTable` gains a map of `CandidateSource`s by approval id (the entry's handle *and* the directory handle it was opened relative to, so the publication's identity check and removal run relative to that directory, exactly as for a run entry); `held_handles()` counts them with every run record's candidates, so a run ending afterwards keeps `32 − held` and a `store_candidates` past the cap releases in inventory order as before; `clear()` (a workspace change) releases them with the rest. When the cap has no room at approval time the approval stands — its facts were verified from the re-opened handle — the handle is released explicitly, and the fact rides the payload as `handleHeld: false` (never silent), so the publication re-opens the entry under HAP-001-R15 when its turn comes, exactly as 5b's cap re-open does; `handleHeld` is reported for a run entry too (whether its record still holds the handle). A held handle that is never published stays held until the workspace changes or the process ends, bounded by the cap; it can starve a later run-end inventory of held handles, which degrades to the re-open path, never to a refusal — disclosed. **Publication** (`acquire_source`): the source comes from the run record for a listed entry or from the table for a whole-outbox approval; when neither holds one, the re-open path opens the outbox and, when the approval's name carries a run prefix, the subdirectory, then probes once and requires the approved facts (`refused` with reason `identity-facts-changed-at-re-open` otherwise), as in 5b.
- **B1 — domain types** (`crates/omnifrons-domain/src/guidance.rs`). `GUIDANCE_TEMPLATE_VERSION = "hap-001-guidance-v1"`; `GuidanceNote::render(outbox)` is HAP-001's fenced template verbatim (heading, blank line, seven bullets, one trailing newline; `tests/guidance.rs` embeds the expected text for `.omnifrons/outbox` and mirrors `docs/heavy-asset-publication.md` lines 316–324, the document being the source of truth) with only `<outbox path>` substituted by the declared spelling. `ManagedFileKind::{Guidance, Ignore}`; `ManagedFileName::guidance(raw)` refuses rather than normalizes, sharing `DisplayName::sanitize`'s bidirectional-control and reserved-device-name checks (now crate-private helpers): empty, a separator (`/` or `\`), `.`/`..`, a control or bidi character, more than 255 bytes, leading whitespace or a trailing dot or whitespace, a reserved device-name stem, or no `.md` extension (case-insensitive) are `ManagedFileNameError` variants with fixed messages; the default is `AGENTS.md`; `ManagedTarget::ignore()` is the fixed `.gitignore` at the root. `ManagedBlock { kind, version, body, digest }` — the body canonical (`\n`-separated, no trailing newline), the digest over those bytes so a CRLF file and an LF file carry the same digest for the same note — with the sentinels `<!-- omnifrons:begin guidance hap-001-guidance-v1 sha256:<64 hex> -->` … `<!-- omnifrons:end guidance -->` for Markdown and `# omnifrons:begin ignore hap-001-guidance-v1 sha256:<64 hex>` … `# omnifrons:end ignore` for the ignore file, whose body is the one rule `/<outbox path>/` (a leading `./` and a trailing `/` of the declaration folded away). **This crate has no hasher**, so every function that needs a digest takes a `DigestFn` (`&dyn Fn(&[u8]) -> Sha256Digest`) the caller supplies — `omnifrons-app`'s `ContentHasher` — and the domain supplies the exact bytes hashed, the same division as the identity preimages of 5b. `ManagedBlock::locate(kind, text, digest)` is pure parsing: `Ok(None)` when no sentinel of the kind is present; `Ok(Some(Located { span, version, sentinel_digest, body }))` for exactly one intact block, the span from the begin line's start to the end sentinel's end (the end line's terminator excluded); `Err(Malformed)` for a begin without an end, an end without or before a begin, more than one of either, or a begin line that does not parse; `Err(Modified)` when the body's digest differs from the sentinel's. `ManagedStatus::{Absent, Current, Outdated { version }, Modified, Malformed}` (`Outdated` also covers this version rendered for another outbox path, reported under the version the sentinel carries); `plan_apply(current, block, digest) -> ApplyPlan { action: Insert | Replace | NoOp, result }` inserts after a single blank line at the end (a file with no trailing newline gets one, one already ending in a blank line gets no second), creates the file when absent or empty, replaces exactly the located span, and is a no-op when current; `plan_remove(current, located, created_by_us) -> RemovePlan { result: Option<String> }` removes the block's lines and one adjacent blank line — the one before it when there is one, else the one after — and yields `None` when the file would be left empty and Omnifrons created it. Line endings are kept as found: the dominant terminator decides (CRLF only when it outnumbers bare LF), and the block is rendered in it.
- **B2 — application ports and the transaction** (`crates/omnifrons-app/src/{managed_file,snapshot_store,guidance}.rs`, `content_hasher.rs`, `work_area.rs`). `ProjectTextFile { read, replace, remove }` over a `ManagedTarget` at a `WorkspaceRoot`: one no-follow open, a regular file only, at most 1 MiB (`MAX_MANAGED_FILE_BYTES`), invalid UTF-8 refused, the bytes returned as bytes so the caller digests exactly what is on disk; `replace` writes a sibling `.<name>.<pid>-<seq>.part` created exclusively and renames it over the target; `remove` never follows a link and treats an absent file as nothing to remove; `ProjectTextFileError::{NotAFile, TooLarge, NotUtf8, Unreadable, WriteFailed, Changed}` with fixed reason tokens. `replace` and `remove` also take `expected: Option<&[u8]>` — the content the caller read, `None` for a file it found absent — and refuse with `Changed` when the target is no longer that (R1-002); the transaction maps that refusal to its own `FileChanged`. `SnapshotStore { record, list, read, pin }` over `SnapshotManifest { schema: 1, id, kind, file, existed, sha256, size, taken_at, pinned }`, with the rules every implementation applies living beside the port so the fake and the real store cannot drift: `sort_newest_first` (by instant, then id), `dedup_target` (the most recent snapshot of the same kind *and file* records the same `existed` and `sha256` → its id comes back and nothing is written), `prune_targets` (every unpinned snapshot of that kind and file beyond the `MAX_UNPINNED_SNAPSHOTS = 5` newest; pinned never). `SnapshotId(u64)` rides the wire as 16 hex and is `derive_snapshot_id`: the first 8 bytes of `sha256("omnifrons-guidance-snapshot-v1" ‖ project identity ‖ file digest ‖ nanos since the epoch as big-endian u128)` — the preimage spelled in `snapshot_store::snapshot_id_preimage`, the hasher only supplying the function. `WorkAreaRoot::open` now creates `snapshots/` beside `journal/` and `recovery/` (owner-only), and `snapshots_dir()` names it. The transaction (`guidance.rs`): `status`, `preview`, `apply`, `remove`, `restore`, `snapshots`, `pin` over `GuidancePorts { files, snapshots, hasher, clock, work_area, workspaces }`, every one in a fixed order — the work area re-check (HAP-001-R7) → the current file read → the binding of the request to the digest the surface showed, or to the file's absence (`FileChanged` otherwise) → the block refusals (`BlockModified`, `BlockMalformed`: a modified block is refused rather than replaced or removed; the user resolves by hand or restores) → the pure plan → a no-op returns without snapshotting → the snapshot (deduplicated and pruned by the store) → the atomic write → the read-back whose digest must equal the planned content's (`VerifyFailed`). `remove` takes `created_by_us` from the oldest snapshot of that file (`existed == false`) and removes the file when the plan says so; `restore` snapshots the current state first (deduplicated), checks the snapshot's bytes against its manifest digest (`Corrupt` otherwise), then writes the bytes back or removes the file when the manifest says the file did not exist. `GuidanceError::{WorkAreaInvalid, FileInvalid(ProjectTextFileError), FileChanged, BlockModified, BlockMalformed, Unmanaged, Snapshot(SnapshotStoreError), VerifyFailed}`, each with a fixed reason token. Nothing in the module reads the note as an instruction: the note is content the product proposes and the user disposes of, never authority (HAP-001-R22). The fakes and contracts behind `contract-tests` (`contract/guidance.rs`): `InMemoryProjectTextFile` (applies the bound and the UTF-8 rule to what it holds; `fail_next_write`), `InMemorySnapshotStore` (`fail_next_record`), and `snapshot_store_contract` (round trip, dedup against the most recent only, newest-first order, kinds and projects kept apart, prune with a pin, unknown ids) and `managed_file_contract` (absent, replaced byte for byte with CRLF kept, invalid UTF-8 and the bound refused on read while the exact bound reads, removed, kinds independent).
- **B3 — the adapters** (`crates/omnifrons-adapters/src/{fs_project_text_file,fs_snapshot_store}.rs`; dependency tables unchanged, `tests/deps.rs` as is — the unix `O_NOFOLLOW` open is gated by `nix`'s `fs` feature already enabled). `FsProjectTextFile`: unix `open(O_RDONLY | O_NOFOLLOW | O_CLOEXEC | O_NONBLOCK)` with `ELOOP`, `ENXIO`, `EOPNOTSUPP`, and `ENOTDIR` as not-a-file; Windows `FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` with the handle's own metadata refusing a reparse point and a directory classified from the path's metadata (the same residual 5a discloses for the prober); the regular-file fact and the size from the handle; the read bounded twice (the reported size, then the bytes actually read through `take(MAX + 1)`, so a file growing between the two is still refused); `replace` refuses a link or a directory at the name, writes the sibling `.part` exclusively, syncs it, copies the original's permissions over (`File::set_permissions`, the mode on unix, the read-only bit on Windows), re-reads the target through that same no-follow open as the last thing before the rename and refuses `Changed` when it is not the caller's `expected` any more (R1-002), and renames it into place, removing the `.part` on any failure; `remove` re-checks the same way and never follows a link. `FsSnapshotStore`: `<work area>/snapshots/<project identity hex>/<id>.json` (`camelCase`, `schema: 1`, `deny_unknown_fields`, `takenAt: {secs, nanos}` as the journal's timestamps) beside `<id>.bytes` (empty for a snapshot of an absent file), the directory `0o700` and both files `0o600` on unix, each written to a sibling temporary name and renamed into place, the bytes before the manifest so a manifest never names bytes that are not there; a manifest that does not parse, carries another schema, or sits under a file name disagreeing with its id fails the whole listing closed as `Corrupt` — and with it every `record`, which needs the listing for dedup and prune — never a listing with the bad one skipped; the read of that id fails the same way, another id still reads, a manifest naming missing bytes is `Corrupt`, a missing manifest `Unknown`; pruning deletes both files.
- **B4 — the shell** (`src-tauri/src/ipc/guidance.rs`, `ipc/dto.rs`). Seven commands, each a thin wrapper over a pure body tested without a Tauri runtime, composing the transaction with the real adapters; no new managed state was needed: the work area and the publication surface lock come from `PublicationState`, the outbox path from `OutboxState`'s policy store. `kind` is `"guidance"` or `"ignore"` (`ManagedFileKindDto`, deserialized strictly); `file` defaults to `AGENTS.md` for `guidance` and is ignored for `ignore`, which always manages `.gitignore`; the guidance file name is validated before anything is read and refused as `guidance-file-invalid` with the rule's own message, never the name. `apply`, `remove`, and `restore` hold the publication surface lock and refuse with the existing `run-active` while any supervised process runs (the same `RunActivity` source as `artifact_approve`); `pin` holds the lock too (it rewrites a manifest); `status`, `preview`, and `snapshots` are read-only and take neither. `fileSha256` is the full 64-hex digest the surface showed, or `null` for a file it showed as absent (`invalid-request` when malformed); `id` is 16 hex (`invalid-request` otherwise). Six new closed-set `ShellErrorCode`s with fixed, path-free messages — `guidance-file-invalid`, `guidance-file-changed`, `guidance-block-modified`, `guidance-block-malformed`, `guidance-unmanaged` (a removal with no block; a restore or a pin naming a snapshot this project does not hold), `snapshot-unavailable` — beside the existing `work-area-invalid`, `outbox-invalid` (the policy could not be loaded), `run-active`, `invalid-request`, and `workspace-unavailable`. `GuidancePreviewDto.proposed` is the block as it would be written, in the file's own line ending; `GuidanceAppliedDto` is shared by `apply`, `remove`, and `restore`, so its action set is `insert | replace | no-op | remove | restore` and `resultSha256Short` is `null` when the file was removed. `artifact_approve`'s `runId` is optional on the wire (`Option<String>`: absent or `null`), and `ArtifactApprovalDto` gains `handleHeld` beside the now-nullable `runId`, the slice-5b shape unchanged when a run is present.

### IPC shapes

One changed command and seven new ones, registered next to the existing ones:

| Command | Params | Returns |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `artifact_approve` | `{ runId: string \| null, name: string, sha256: string }` (`runId` may be absent) | `ArtifactApprovalDto` |
| `guidance_status` | `{ kind: "guidance" \| "ignore", file?: string }` | `GuidanceStatusDto` |
| `guidance_preview` | `{ kind, file? }` | `GuidancePreviewDto` |
| `guidance_apply` | `{ kind, file?, fileSha256: string \| null }` | `GuidanceAppliedDto` |
| `guidance_remove` | `{ kind, file?, fileSha256: string \| null }` | `GuidanceAppliedDto` |
| `guidance_snapshots` | `{ kind }` | `SnapshotDto[]` |
| `guidance_pin` | `{ id: string, pinned: boolean }` | `SnapshotDto` |
| `guidance_restore` | `{ id: string, fileSha256: string \| null }` | `GuidanceAppliedDto` |

```jsonc
// artifact_approve { runId: null, name: "dropped.pdf", sha256: "9d3f2a10…" } -- an entry candidates_list {} listed
// at the outbox root: re-opened once under the single-handle discipline, the digest from that handle equal to
// the request's; no run, no launch provenance, the unattributed fact; handleHeld says the re-opened handle is
// held for the publication (false once the D22 cap is full, never silently)
{"approvalId": "5f0c3b2a9e1d7c44", "publicationId": "…", "runId": null, "name": "dropped.pdf",
 "displayName": "dropped.pdf", "sha256Short": "9d3f2a10", "size": 15, "detectedType": "pdf",
 "class": "generated-heavy", "attribution": {"kind": "unattributed"}, "assetRootId": "main",
 "actAs": "device-local-user", "approvedAt": 1725782401000, "handleHeld": true}
// under a run subdirectory the table does not remember: the same shape with name "run-old/stray.png" and
// runId still null -- the subdirectory is a location fact the name carries, never provenance; the record then
// carries "producer":{"kind":"unattributed","foundUnderRunId":"run-old"} (null for a root entry)
// the slice-5b shape is unchanged when a run is named, plus handleHeld:
{"approvalId": "23121521465ad9be", "publicationId": "…", "runId": "run-1725782401-000000001-0",
 "name": "run-1725782401-000000001-0/report.pdf", "displayName": "report.pdf", "sha256Short": "061eb0a8",
 "size": 37, "detectedType": "pdf", "class": "generated-heavy",
 "attribution": {"kind": "run", "runId": "run-1725782401-000000001-0"}, "assetRootId": "main",
 "actAs": "device-local-user", "approvedAt": 1725782401000, "handleHeld": true}

// ShellErrors of the whole-outbox approval (fixed messages, no slash, no path, no digest)
{"code": "integrity-mismatch", "message": "the entry's digest differs from the digest the request names; it changed since it was listed"}
{"code": "outbox-escape", "message": "the entry is not a regular file inside the outbox"}
{"code": "outbox-linked", "message": "the entry's link count is greater than one"}
{"code": "refused", "message": "only a generated-heavy candidate can be approved for publication"}
{"code": "invalid-request", "message": "the entry name is not one the outbox inventory lists"}
{"code": "invalid-request", "message": "the entry belongs to a remembered run; approve it through that run id"}
{"code": "invalid-request", "message": "no entry with that name could be opened under the outbox"}
{"code": "invalid-request", "message": "the outbox does not exist yet, so no entry can be listed in it"}
{"code": "invalid-request", "message": "no run subdirectory with that name exists in the outbox"}

// <work area>/journal/publications.jsonl: the approved line of a whole-outbox approval carries runId null
{"schema":1,"event":"approved","approvalId":"5f0c3b2a9e1d7c44","publicationId":"…","projectId":"<project identity hex>",
 "runId":null,"name":"dropped.pdf","displayName":"dropped.pdf","sha256":"9d3f2a10…","size":15,"detectedType":"pdf",
 "class":"generated-heavy","attribution":{"kind":"unattributed"},"assetRootId":"main","adapterId":null,
 "executableApprovalId":null,"approvedAt":{"secs":1725782401,"nanos":0}}
```

```jsonc
// guidance_status { kind: "guidance" } -- file defaults to AGENTS.md; a fresh project. managed is one of
// absent | current | outdated | modified | malformed; templateVersion is what an apply would write;
// fileSha256 is the full digest an apply or a removal binds to (null while the file does not exist)
{"kind": "guidance", "file": "AGENTS.md", "exists": false, "managed": "absent",
 "templateVersion": "hap-001-guidance-v1", "fileSha256": null, "fileSha256Short": null, "snapshots": 0, "pinned": 0}
{"kind": "guidance", "file": "AGENTS.md", "exists": true, "managed": "current",
 "templateVersion": "hap-001-guidance-v1", "fileSha256": "3c1e9a07…", "fileSha256Short": "3c1e9a07",
 "snapshots": 1, "pinned": 0}

// guidance_preview { kind: "ignore" } -- file is ignored for ignore: always .gitignore. proposed is the block as
// it would be written, in the file's own line ending; action is what the apply would do
{"kind": "ignore", "file": ".gitignore", "action": "insert",
 "proposed": "# omnifrons:begin ignore hap-001-guidance-v1 sha256:<64 hex of the body bytes>\n/.omnifrons/outbox/\n# omnifrons:end ignore",
 "fileSha256": "a71b…", "fileSha256Short": "a71bc0d2", "resultSha256Short": "0e9f4c31"}
// guidance_preview { kind: "guidance", file: "CLAUDE.md" } -- proposed is
// "<!-- omnifrons:begin guidance hap-001-guidance-v1 sha256:<64 hex> -->\n## Generated files\n\n- Write every …\n<!-- omnifrons:end guidance -->"
// (the template of docs/heavy-asset-publication.md § Agent guidance note with only the outbox path substituted)

// guidance_apply { kind: "guidance", fileSha256: null } -- the file was absent: inserted, its absence snapshotted first
{"kind": "guidance", "file": "AGENTS.md", "action": "insert", "snapshotId": "0123456789abcdef", "resultSha256Short": "3c1e9a07"}
// a second apply with the file's digest: a no-op, nothing written, nothing snapshotted
{"kind": "guidance", "file": "AGENTS.md", "action": "no-op", "snapshotId": null, "resultSha256Short": "3c1e9a07"}
// guidance_remove { kind: "guidance", fileSha256: "3c1e9a07…" } -- the file Omnifrons created is removed with the block
{"kind": "guidance", "file": "AGENTS.md", "action": "remove", "snapshotId": "89abcdef01234567", "resultSha256Short": null}
// guidance_restore { id: "0123456789abcdef", fileSha256: "3c1e9a07…" } -- the state before the restore snapshotted first
{"kind": "guidance", "file": "AGENTS.md", "action": "restore", "snapshotId": "fedcba9876543210", "resultSha256Short": null}

// guidance_snapshots { kind: "guidance" } -> newest first; takenAt ms since the epoch; sha256Short of the bytes recorded
[{"id": "89abcdef01234567", "kind": "guidance", "file": "AGENTS.md", "existed": true, "sha256Short": "3c1e9a07",
  "size": 1512, "takenAt": 1725782402000, "pinned": false},
 {"id": "0123456789abcdef", "kind": "guidance", "file": "AGENTS.md", "existed": false, "sha256Short": "e3b0c442",
  "size": 0, "takenAt": 1725782401000, "pinned": true}]
// guidance_pin { id: "0123456789abcdef", pinned: true } -> the snapshot as it now stands (a SnapshotDto)

// ShellErrors (fixed catalogue messages, no slash, no path, no name; none carries detail)
{"code": "guidance-file-invalid", "message": "the file name must be one path component with no separator"}
{"code": "guidance-file-invalid", "message": "the guidance file must end in .md"}
{"code": "guidance-file-invalid", "message": "the managed file is not a regular file"}
{"code": "guidance-file-invalid", "message": "the managed file exceeds the size limit"}
{"code": "guidance-file-invalid", "message": "the managed file is not valid UTF-8"}
{"code": "guidance-file-invalid", "message": "the managed file did not read back as written"}
{"code": "guidance-file-changed", "message": "the managed file changed since it was shown; read its status again and retry"}
{"code": "guidance-block-modified", "message": "the managed block was modified inside its sentinels; resolve it by hand or restore a snapshot"}
{"code": "guidance-block-malformed", "message": "the managed block's sentinels are not one intact pair; resolve it by hand or restore a snapshot"}
{"code": "guidance-unmanaged", "message": "the file carries no managed block"}
{"code": "guidance-unmanaged", "message": "no snapshot with that id is recorded for this project"}
{"code": "snapshot-unavailable", "message": "a snapshot manifest is corrupt"}
{"code": "work-area-invalid", "message": "the product work area resolves inside a registered workspace root or cannot be used"}
{"code": "run-active", "message": "a run is active; approve or publish once it has ended"}
{"code": "invalid-request", "message": "the file digest is not 64 hex characters"}
{"code": "invalid-request", "message": "the snapshot id is not 16 hex characters"}
```

`ShellErrorCode` gains exactly six values: `guidance-file-invalid`, `guidance-file-changed`, `guidance-block-modified`, `guidance-block-malformed`, `guidance-unmanaged`, `snapshot-unavailable`. The files this slice writes look like this:

```markdown
<!-- omnifrons:begin guidance hap-001-guidance-v1 sha256:<64 hex of the body bytes> -->
## Generated files

- Write every generated file that is not a Markdown note — … — into `.omnifrons/outbox`, relative to this project's root. Create the directory if it does not exist.
- … (the seven bullets of docs/heavy-asset-publication.md § Agent guidance note, verbatim) …
<!-- omnifrons:end guidance -->
```

```gitignore
# omnifrons:begin ignore hap-001-guidance-v1 sha256:<64 hex of the body bytes>
/.omnifrons/outbox/
# omnifrons:end ignore
```

```jsonc
// <work area>/snapshots/<project identity hex>/0123456789abcdef.json, beside 0123456789abcdef.bytes
{"schema":1,"id":"0123456789abcdef","kind":"guidance","file":"AGENTS.md","existed":false,
 "sha256":"e3b0c442…","size":0,"takenAt":{"secs":1725782401,"nanos":0},"pinned":false}
```

The body digest in a sentinel is over the canonical body — the lines between the sentinels joined by `\n`, no trailing newline — so a CRLF file carries the same digest as an LF one; a block is inserted after a single blank line at the end of the file, in the file's dominant line ending, and removed with one adjacent blank line. Neither file carries a device path; a manifest names the project by its identity and the file by its name at the workspace root.

### Evidence procedures

#### Procedure notes for VP-S28

[VP-S28](heavy-asset-publication.md#desktop-stack-verification-planmd)'s "the offered note equals the template with only the path substituted" now has its dev-mode analogue in `the_note_is_hap_001s_template_with_only_the_outbox_path_substituted` (`crates/omnifrons-domain/tests/guidance.rs`, the expected text embedded and mirroring the document's lines 316–324) and `preview_offers_the_template_with_only_the_path_substituted_and_writes_nothing` (`src-tauri/src/ipc/guidance.rs`). Against a packaged build with a policy declaring an outbox path: call `guidance_preview { kind: "guidance" }` and compare `proposed` line by line with the document's fenced block — the first and last lines must be the two sentinels exactly as [IPC shapes](#ipc-shapes-4) spells them, every line between must equal the document's with `<outbox path>` replaced by the policy's declared spelling and nothing else changed, and the `sha256` in the begin sentinel must equal the SHA-256 of those lines joined by `\n` computed independently; then confirm no file was written and `<app-data>/work-area/snapshots/` holds nothing for the project. Call `guidance_apply { kind: "guidance", fileSha256: null }` on a project with no `AGENTS.md`: the file must consist of exactly the proposed block plus one trailing newline, `snapshots/<project hex>/` must hold one manifest with `existed: false` and an empty bytes file, and a second `guidance_apply` with the file's digest must answer `no-op` with `snapshotId: null` and add no file. VP-S28's other steps — the outbox-root and forgotten-subdirectory ingestions — have their analogues in `approving_an_unattributed_root_entry_holds_its_handle_and_publishes_unattributed` and `approving_an_entry_under_a_forgotten_run_records_its_subdirectory_as_a_location_fact` (`src-tauri/src/ipc/publication.rs`) and `an_unattributed_approval_over_the_real_adapters_registers_producer_unattributed` (`crates/omnifrons-adapters/tests/publication_fs.rs`): against a packaged build, with the product closed, write a PDF into the outbox root and a second into an existing run subdirectory, open the project, call `candidates_list {}` and then `artifact_approve { runId: null, … }` with each row's `name` and `sha256`; both approvals must return `runId: null` and `attribution: {kind: "unattributed"}`, the record lines in `.omnifrons/catalog.jsonl` must carry `producer: {"kind":"unattributed","foundUnderRunId":null}` and `{"kind":"unattributed","foundUnderRunId":"<run id>"}` respectively with no run id, adapter id, or executable approval anywhere else in the record, and rewriting a listed file between `candidates_list` and `artifact_approve` must answer `integrity-mismatch` with nothing journaled. The planted-link step must answer `outbox-escape` from `artifact_approve` as well as rendering it in the inventory. The `misplaced`, handoff-candidacy, and synchronized-policy steps are 5d and later.

### Deviations and debt

Deviations from the brief, each with its reason:

- **`ArtifactApproval.run_id` is `Option<RunId>` and `found_under()` derives the location fact.** The brief asked for `runId: null` on the approval and `producer: unattributed` with the subdirectory as a location fact on the record; the domain type had a required run id. Making it optional is the honest model (the whole-outbox inventory lists without a run), and the record's `foundUnderRunId` is derived from the listing run or the name's `<run id>/` prefix rather than a second field the two paths would have to keep in step.
- **`handleHeld` on `ArtifactApprovalDto`.** The brief said "eviction never silent" for the cap that counts the re-opened handles; an approval has no channel for a diagnostic frame, so the fact rides the payload as an additive boolean (reported for run entries too). A held handle is never evicted in favour of a newer approval: the newest approval is the one left unheld when the cap is full.
- **An entry of a remembered run is refused with `runId: null`** ("approve it through that run id"): the run's inventory carries the attribution and provenance the record must record; approving it unattributed would discard a proposal the run made. A remembered run whose inventory failed is treated as forgotten.
- **`integrity-mismatch` at approval for a changed digest**, per the brief's mapping, although nothing was published: the listing's digest is the fact the approval binds to, and the code says the entry's content is not that.
- **The domain takes a digest function** (`DigestFn`) instead of hashing: `omnifrons-domain` depends on `std` and `thiserror` only, so sentinel digests are computed through the app's `ContentHasher`, the domain spelling the bytes.
- **`status`, `plan_apply`, and `plan_remove` take the block or the located span rather than a kind**: the kind is the block's, and passing it twice invited disagreement.
- **`ManagedStatus::Outdated` also covers the same version rendered for another outbox path**, reported under the version the sentinel carries: the closed set the brief named has no other token for "intact, this version, a different body", and an apply replaces it either way.
- **`GuidanceAppliedDto.action` has five tokens and `resultSha256Short` is nullable**: `remove` and `restore` share the payload with `apply`, and a removed file has no digest.
- **`guidance_pin` returns the pinned `SnapshotDto`** (the brief named no return), searching both kinds' listings for the id, since the store keys snapshots by id and the request names no kind.
- **`pin` holds the surface lock** although the brief named only `apply`, `remove`, and `restore`: it rewrites a manifest.
- **A corrupt manifest fails the whole listing** (`snapshot-unavailable`) rather than being reported beside the good ones: the JSONL stores' fail-closed discipline, and a record needs the listing for dedup and prune. The id's read fails the same way and another id still reads.
- **Snapshots are bounded per kind and file** (five unpinned each), not per project: the bound the brief names is what a restore of one file needs.
- **A name with a backslash is refused** by the whole-outbox approval and by the guidance file rule, so a name means one component on every platform; a unix file whose name carries a backslash is listed by the inventory but not approvable through `runId: null`.
- **`remove` on an absent file is nothing**, at the port: the transaction's restore of an absent-file snapshot should not fail when the file is already gone.
- **No new managed state**: the brief said "state alongside `PublicationState`"; the guidance commands need only the work area and the lock it already holds, and the policy store `OutboxState` already holds.
- **`OutboxPath::new` refuses a control character** (R1-001, this slice's risk review): a new `OutboxPathError::Control` covers every `char::is_control()` character and U+2028/U+2029, refused before any path shape is looked at. The declaration comes from the synchronized `.omnifrons/asset-policy.json` — untrusted content under HAP-001-R40 — and is the one thing substituted into the managed blocks this slice writes into a project's text files, so `"out\n<!-- omnifrons:end guidance -->\nx"` would have put a second end sentinel inside the block and made every later `ManagedBlock::locate` answer `Malformed`: a permanent, self-inflicted denial of the guidance feature for that project, and one no user edit inside the sentinels could explain. The refusal rides the existing invalid-declaration path (`PolicyError::InvalidOutboxPath` → `outbox-invalid` with reason `policy-invalid`), so nothing downstream ever sees such a path; `a_rendered_block_carries_exactly_one_begin_and_one_end_line_for_every_accepted_path` (`crates/omnifrons-domain/tests/guidance.rs`) stands over the property for every path that is accepted, including ones that spell a sentinel on their single line.
- **The same refusal covers the bidirectional override range** (R1-004, this slice's risk re-review): `char::is_control()` covers only the Cc category, so U+202A–U+202E and U+2066–U+2069 passed the check above and were written verbatim into the user's `AGENTS.md` and `.gitignore`. A right-to-left override there makes the managed block render in an order it is not stored in — Trojan-Source class spoofing in the two files a human and an agent both read as instructions — and the renderer's own strip protects the preview only, never the bytes on disk. `OutboxPath::new` now calls the crate-private `is_bidi_control` helper `ManagedFileName::check_component` and `DisplayName::sanitize` already share, so one data flow has one boundary. **The range rides the existing `Control` variant rather than a new one**: it is the same class of character — the ones that make a managed block read as something other than what it is — no caller can act differently on an override than on a newline (both ride `PolicyError::InvalidOutboxPath` → `outbox-invalid` with reason `policy-invalid`), and `ManagedFileNameError::Control` already folds controls and overrides together for the very files this path writes; a second variant would multiply wire tokens without giving anyone a second remedy. The message stays fixed and path-free, now naming all three classes. `outbox_path_rejects_bidirectional_override_characters` (`crates/omnifrons-domain/tests/outbox.rs`) carries a raw U+202E inside an otherwise valid path and pins U+200F — a plain right-to-left mark, not an override — as still accepted, where `is_bidi_control` draws the line everywhere else.
- **`ProjectTextFile`'s writes carry the bytes they were planned from** (R1-002, the same review): `replace` and `remove` take `expected: Option<&[u8]>` — the content the caller read, `None` for a file it found absent — and the adapter re-reads the target through the same no-follow open immediately before the rename or the removal, refusing with the new `ProjectTextFileError::Changed` when the bytes or the presence differ. The transaction maps that to the `FileChanged` refusal the digest binding already gives, so the surface reads the status again and retries; the wire keeps one code and one message. Before this, the digest binding held only between the surface and the transaction's own read: an external rewrite landing after that read was overwritten by the rename and captured by no snapshot, since the snapshot records the bytes the transaction read, not the bytes it destroyed.

Pins green on arrival, recorded as such rather than as reds: the journal contract's unattributed `approved` line (`runId: null` round-trips) and the real-adapters publication of an unattributed approval (both exercised code the domain change had already forced through the compiler: the behavioral red for the location fact was the app transaction's `left: Unattributed { found_under: None } / right: Unattributed { found_under: Some(RunId("run-1")) }`); the in-memory fakes against their own contracts (written alongside them, as 5b's were); and the domain's twelve guidance tests, which passed on the module's first compile after their `error[E0432]: unresolved import omnifrons_domain::guidance` red — every behavior of the module was written to those tests, not the other way round.

The risk and reliability review-fix pass that followed carried two reds of its own: `error[E0599]: no variant, associated function, or constant named 'Control' found for enum 'OutboxPathError'` for the declaration refusal (R1-001), and `error[E0061]: this method takes 3 arguments but 4 arguments were supplied` in `contract/guidance.rs` for the write's `expected` parameter (R1-002), whose new `Changed` cases in `managed_file_contract` then failed behaviorally against both the fake and the real adapter until each re-checked its target. Everything else that pass added is coverage of branches the code already had, and is recorded as a pin: the missing `.bytes` sibling and the tampered-with bytes refused at restore, the hard-linked outbox entry, the run remembered without an inventory, the block at either edge of a file, the typed `producer` round trip through `JsonlCatalogStore::list()`, the read-only guidance operations against an invalid work area, and the rendered block's one begin and one end line for every accepted path.

The scoped re-review that followed *that* pass carried one red, and one change no red can be quoted for. The red is R1-004's, in `outbox_path_rejects_bidirectional_override_characters` (`crates/omnifrons-domain/tests/outbox.rs`): `called 'Result::unwrap_err()' on an 'Ok' value: OutboxPath("build/\u{202e}gnp.esrever/out")`, green once `OutboxPath::new` called `is_bidi_control` beside `char::is_control`.
