The change without a red is R1-005's, and it is recorded as such rather than dressed up: `approving_with_no_run_cannot_see_a_second_link_off_unix` is this repository's only whole `#[cfg(not(unix))]` test, its body is compiled by no check that runs here — the Windows clippy pass excludes the shell for the `windres` reason slice 3 recorded, and `cargo check -p omnifrons-shell --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` fails in the build script with `NotAttempted("x86_64-w64-mingw32-windres")` — so CI's `windows-latest` leg is its first ever execution, and its `std::fs::hard_link(...).expect(...)` was the only un-gated one on a path that leg actually runs (the other un-gated call, `--plant-link` in `crates/omnifrons-supervisor/src/bin/fake-agent.rs`, is driven by a `#[cfg(unix)]` test alone; every other `hard_link` in the tree sits inside a `#[cfg(unix)]` block). `CreateHardLinkW` needs NTFS or ReFS on a single volume and is refused on FAT, exFAT, or a mapped network temp, and the fixture sits under `std::env::temp_dir()`; since the test asserts the *absence* of link detection, a fixture the filesystem refuses costs it nothing, so the link is now tolerated (`let _ = …`) with the doc comment saying plainly that it may or may not exist there, and the assertions that do run — approval succeeds, `unattributed`, handle held — are unchanged. It was verified by flipping the gate to `#[cfg(unix)]` locally: `cargo clippy -p omnifrons-shell --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` then found and fixed a real `clippy::doc_markdown` the Windows leg would have hit, and the test compiled and ran, failing on `ShellError { code: OutboxLinked, … }` exactly as unix should — proof the surviving assertions still bite — before the gate was put back.

Debt and open owner decisions this slice names without deciding:

- **D18 assumed at its default** (offer, propose, approved write) with the installer discipline; D16's wrong-root scan and `misplaced` with its remedies are 5d; `catalog_repair`, the stale-`.part` sweep, and recovery re-approval are 5e.
- **The snapshot location is the product work area**, not `.omnifrons/`, which is synchronized and therefore untrusted content under HAP-001-R40; five unpinned snapshots per file; the sentinel format and the digest pin; a modified block refused rather than replaced; the guidance file limited to one root-level `.md` component; the `.gitignore` rule shape `/<outbox path>/` — all spike defaults disclosed here.
- **No git awareness**: `.gitignore` is edited as text and never consulted for a verdict (HAP-001-R32); whether the outbox is actually ignored is not checked.
- **Windows residuals widen**: owner-only modes on the snapshot files and directory are not expressed (ACLs are outside `std`), the guidance file's original permissions carry over as the read-only bit only, and the no-follow open classifies a directory from the path's metadata rather than the handle's — disclosed, as 5a and 5b disclose theirs. The whole-outbox approval's link-count check is unix only (`outbox-linked` cannot be produced on Windows, as before).
- **A held unattributed handle that is never published** stays held until the workspace changes or the process ends, counting against the cap; a later run-end inventory then keeps fewer handles and its entries take the re-open path. A time bound or an explicit release is a later decision.
- **The `.part` file a crash leaves beside a managed file** (`.<name>.<pid>-<seq>.part`) or a snapshot is not swept, like the provider's staging files (5e).
- **The `.part` file takes the original's mode only after its bytes are written** (R1-008, examined in the re-review and left alone): it is created at the process umask's default, so a 0600 `AGENTS.md` has a briefly 0644 replacement in the workspace root before `set_permissions` runs. Left as is deliberately — the guidance note and the ignore rule are the product's own fixed template plus one declared path, non-secret by construction — and named here so a later managed file with different content re-decides it rather than inheriting it.
- **The read-only guidance bodies do not take the surface lock** (R1-009, examined and left alone): `guidance_snapshots_for` was raised, and `guidance_status_for` and `guidance_preview_for` are in exactly the same position — all three read and write nothing, and `lock_surface()` is taken by `apply`, `remove`, `restore`, and `pin` alone, as this module's header already says. The worst a lock-free reader can answer is a view one write old, the staleness any listing has by the time it is rendered, and every write is bound to a digest the caller must send back, so a stale read cannot be turned into a wrong write. Named here so the exception is on the record rather than looking like an omission.
- **The write's re-check leaves a residual sub-window** (R1-002), and **two vectors reach into it** (R1-006, this slice's risk re-review): the target is re-read immediately before the rename, but `rename(2)` cannot be made conditional on the target's content through `std`, so (a) a rewrite of the target landing between that read and the rename itself is still overwritten, silently and captured by no snapshot; and (b) the `.part` file sits in the workspace root — a directory the harness can write to — under a name predictable but for its sequence number, so an unlink and a symbolic link created at that name inside the window make the rename move the *link*, leaving a symbolic link at `AGENTS.md` (or `.gitignore`) and discarding the planned bytes. (b) is detected rather than silent: the transaction's read-back goes through the same no-follow open, which refuses the planted link as `ProjectTextFileError::NotAFile` → `GuidanceError::FileInvalid` → `guidance-file-invalid`, so the write is never reported as having succeeded — but the module header's earlier "the target is either the old content or the new one" was stronger than what holds, and now says both vectors and the detection. The window shrinks from "the whole transaction" to "two adjacent syscalls" and no further; closing either needs a lock the product does not take on a file the user owns, or a platform primitive outside `std`. Disclosed, not closed.
- **Not exercised by any test**: the seven guidance commands and `artifact_approve` as Tauri commands (their pure bodies are); every Windows code path (cross-compiled, never executed — including this slice's two `#[cfg(not(unix))]` counterparts, the entry that stays after a whole-outbox publication and the hard link the link count cannot see) and every macOS execution; a file growing between the size check and the read; two shells against one work area; a crash between the bytes file and the manifest; the residual sub-window between the write's re-check and its rename; and the outdated-block replace over the real adapters (covered over the fakes).
- **HAP-001 D3, D16, D18, D21, D22** remain the owner's calls; this slice assumes D18's and D22's defaults and says so here rather than presenting them as decided.

### Renderer

The renderer half of this slice landed after the Rust half above, against exactly the wire shapes in [IPC shapes](#ipc-shapes-4). It was written in two sittings, and the second resumed an interrupted first: `renderer/src/ipc/harness.ts` with its suite and every one of `AgentPanel.test.tsx`'s new behaviors were already on the tree — written first, and red — when the Guidance section itself was implemented. The reds below are therefore quoted as the resuming run reported them, in bulk (`pnpm -r test` at `Tests 39 failed | 352 passed (391)`, `AgentPanel.test.tsx` alone at `39 failed | 197 passed (236)`, every failure in the three guidance describes: `expected null to be 'AGENTS.md: block absent; file absent;…'` and its five siblings for the status line's fixed copy per `managed` token, `expected null not to be null` for every block, table and label the section had yet to render, and, at `src/AgentPanel.test.tsx:6890`, `expect(ignoreStatusLine()).not.toBeNull()` timing out), rather than one behavior at a time the way slices 3 to 5b record them. That departure from the strict-TDD record is stated here rather than dressed up.

**Wrappers and types.** `harness.ts` gains the seven guidance commands as typed wrappers, each with `# Errors` JSDoc listing its rejections: `guidanceStatus(kind, file?)`, `guidancePreview(kind, file?)`, `guidanceApply(kind, file, fileSha256)`, `guidanceRemove(kind, file, fileSha256)`, `guidanceSnapshots(kind)`, `guidancePin(id, pinned)`, `guidanceRestore(id, fileSha256)`. A shared `guidanceTarget` puts the `file` key on the wire only when a file was named, so an `ignore` request crosses IPC as exactly `{ kind: 'ignore' }` — the key absent, not `undefined`, the discipline `candidatesList`'s whole-outbox `{}` already set. The types mirror `dto.rs` field-for-field: `GuidanceKind` (`guidance` | `ignore`), `ManagedStatus` (the five tokens), `GuidanceAction` (five), `SnapshotId` (an opaque 16-hex string, never parsed here), `GuidanceStatus` (nine fields), `GuidancePreview` (seven), `GuidanceApplied` (five), `Snapshot` (eight). `artifactApprove` takes `runId: string | null` and still invokes `artifact_approve` with exactly `{ runId, name, sha256 }` — the key present and null for the whole-outbox path — `ArtifactApproval` gains `handleHeld`, and the closed `ShellErrorCode` union gains the six new codes (`guidance-file-invalid`, `guidance-file-changed`, `guidance-block-modified`, `guidance-block-malformed`, `guidance-unmanaged`, `snapshot-unavailable`) with the compile-time exhaustiveness guards kept in step. `harness.test.ts` grows 52 → 67.

**The whole-outbox listing.** A **List outbox** button beside the outbox status line, disabled while no workspace is active and while a run is active, invokes `candidates_list` with exactly `{}` — no `runId` key — and applies the answer as the candidates table with no run id and no adapter, and no summary line in the transcript, since no run announced it. Every row reads `unattributed`; a generated-heavy `candidate` row carries **Approve** exactly as an attributed row does (HAP-001 admitting an unattributed entry to explicit human approval with the unattributed fact in view), and the block reads `attribution: unattributed`, the destination from the outbox status, and `scope: none` — no run produced the entry, so there is no adapter whose scope mode could apply, and nothing enforces anything over an unmediated producer. The gate stays the row's short digest and the request is `artifact_approve { runId: null, name, sha256 }` carrying the row's own full digest, never the typed value. An entry under a run subdirectory the shell no longer remembers sends its `<run id>/<file>` name exactly as listed, and the block reports that string only as the entry's name: the subdirectory is a location fact the name carries, never a run the surface claims. An approval answering `handleHeld: false` renders `handle: not held (cap reached)` in a `<mark>` beside the approved cell with **Publish** still offered — the cap left no room for the handle, the approval stands on the facts verified from the re-opened one, and the publication re-opens the entry under the single-handle discipline when its turn comes (HAP-001-R17, D22) — and nothing of the sort for a held handle. The four whole-outbox rejection codes (`integrity-mismatch`, `outbox-escape`, `outbox-linked`, `invalid-request`) render as plain catalogue codes with the shell's fixed messages and no "untrusted". A second List outbox replaces the rows and clears the approvals and the open block, like a new run's inventory; a listing response landing after a Start is dropped by the spawn-generation guard.

**The Guidance section.** `AgentPanel.tsx` is again the only component touched; there is no new component file. The section is a `<section aria-label="Guidance">`, a sibling of the transcript, the Candidates region and the Publications region and never inside any of them (RCS-001-R6), rendered whether or not a workspace is active. It opens with the fixed line `advisory: the note and the ignore rule set expectations and enforce nothing — not containment` and holds one block per managed kind: `<div aria-label="Guidance note">` with an editable `Guidance file` input defaulting to `AGENTS.md`, and `<div aria-label="Ignore rule">` with the fixed line `Ignore file: .gitignore`. Each block shows a status line (`<p role="status" aria-label="Guidance note status">` / `"Ignore rule status"`) reading `<file>: <block state>; file sha256 short <short>` — or `file absent` — `; snapshots <n>, pinned <n>`, the block state fixed copy per closed `managed` token through an exhaustive switch (`block absent`, `block current`, `block outdated (template <templateVersion>)`, `block modified — resolve by hand or restore`, `block malformed — resolve by hand`), the whole line through `PlainTextLine` because the file name is project-originated text. On mount, and again after a successful workspace pick, four fetches run: `guidance_status { kind: "guidance", file: "AGENTS.md" }`, `guidance_status { kind: "ignore" }`, `guidance_snapshots { kind: "guidance" }`, `guidance_snapshots { kind: "ignore" }`. Committing the file name — leaving the field, or Enter — refetches `guidance_status { kind: "guidance", file: <name> }` and shows that file's status.

**Preview** invokes `guidance_preview` with exactly `{ kind, file }` (`{ kind }` for `ignore`) and nothing else, and opens the write block (`<div aria-label="Guidance write">`, inside the Guidance region, beside the two kind blocks and outside the transcript): `file: <file>`, `action: <insert|replace|no-op>`, `proposed:` and **the proposed block one line per text line through `PlainTextLine`** — the HTML-comment sentinels, the Markdown heading and every bullet rendered as the text they are, with no comment node, no heading, no anchor, no list and nothing interactive anywhere inside it — then `file sha256 short: <short>` or `file: absent`, the fixed sentence `a snapshot of the current file is taken before any write`, the fixed `act as: device-local-user` (the one `ActAs` token the shell can bind, so the renderer's own constant, never wire text), the gate label, an input that opens empty, and a disabled **Write**. The gate reads `Type the file's short digest (<fileSha256Short>) to write`, or, for a file the preview reports absent, `Type the proposed block's short digest (<resultSha256Short>) to create the file` — there is no file digest to have read. It is enabled only on an exact, case-sensitive match: the fixed phrase `write`, uppercase hex, a seven- or nine-character prefix, the result digest of a file that exists, the full 64-hex digest, the other kind's short digest, and the wanted digest itself carrying a leading or trailing space — the shape a paste produces — all leave it disabled (R1-001, as in slice 2 and the 5b approval); the same boundary set is checked again for a file the preview reports absent, whose gate is the proposed block's own digest and whose negative cases include the file's digest and the gate verb. The click calls `guidance_apply` with exactly `{ kind, file, fileSha256 }` (`{ kind, fileSha256 }` for `ignore`) — the preview's own file and its full 64-hex digest, or `null` for a file it showed as absent — **never the typed value**, once per double-click; on success `<p role="status" aria-label="Guidance result">` reads `<kind label> <file>: written <action>, snapshot <id>` (or `none`, for the no-op that snapshots nothing) `, result <short>` (or `absent`) — the kind and the file are named because the section has one result line shared by both kinds, so a receipt has to say which file it is a receipt for (R1-003/R3-007) — the block closes, and that kind's status and snapshots — only that kind's — are fetched afresh.

**Remove** opens the same block bound to the status the section already shows (`file`, `action: remove`, the fixed line `removes the managed block only; the file itself goes only when nothing else remains in it and Omnifrons created it`, the file's short digest, the snapshot sentence, the act-as line, the label `…to remove`, a disabled **Remove block**) and invokes nothing until the gate confirms; the click calls `guidance_remove` with exactly `{ kind, file, fileSha256 }` from the status and reports the removal receipt. That scope line is the review's own addition (R1-005): unlike an apply, opening a Remove previews nothing, and `guidance_remove` takes out the file as well as the block when the shell's `plan_remove` finds nothing else left in a file Omnifrons created — so the surface states in words what an apply states by showing the exact bytes, and the snapshot sentence beside it says a snapshot precedes the act either way. It is offered only for a block the shell would remove — `current` or `outdated` — and disabled for `absent`, `modified` and `malformed`: there is nothing of Omnifrons' to remove from the first, and the last two are the user's to resolve by hand or to restore, never Omnifrons' to delete. It is disabled for a status carrying no short digest too (R3-008): the shell does not produce a `current` status without one, but a block opened on such a status would show a gate with nothing to type, a button that could never be enabled and no Cancel — a dead end, refused at the offer rather than at the confirmation. **Snapshots** are a table per kind that has any (`<table aria-label="Guidance note snapshots">` / `"Ignore rule snapshots"`, inside its own kind block, no table at all for an empty list): `file`, `id`, `taken at` (the wire instant as an ISO 8601 time), `sha256 short`, `size`, `existed`, `pinned` (both `yes`/`no`), `action`, every data cell inert, rows in the order listed (newest first). **Pin**/**Unpin** calls `guidance_pin` with exactly `{ id, pinned }` — the opposite of the row's flag — applies the returned snapshot to the row, and refetches that kind's status for its pinned count but not its listing, which would immediately overwrite the answer; one at a time, by a ref set synchronously and by the buttons' own `disabled`, so a double-click pins once and no slow answer lands on top of a newer one (R3-013). **Restore** opens the write block with the snapshot's facts (`snapshot: <id>, taken at <ISO>, sha256 short <short>, size <n>, existed <yes|no>`), then the two disclosures the facts alone do not carry (R1-002) — the fixed `the snapshot's bytes are not shown here: only what the manifest records about them`, and, for a snapshot that recorded no file, the fixed `this snapshot recorded no file: restoring it REMOVES the file rather than writing bytes back` — and the gate against the *current* file's short digest — the act stands in for having read what is about to be overwritten — or, for a file the status shows as absent, the snapshot's own; the click calls `guidance_restore` with exactly `{ id, fileSha256 }`, the status's full digest or `null`. Both disclosures stand before the gate. They exist because a restore is the one write here into user-owned, version-tracked content whose bytes the surface never shows — there is no command on the wire that returns a snapshot's content, and none was invented — and because a snapshot with `existed: false` *deletes*, which neither the `restore` action token nor the `Restore snapshot` button says. Restore is offered only on snapshots of the file the status line shows, and only on snapshots of that very kind (R1-006: the two file spaces cannot collide today, so this is defence in depth on a surface whose whole point is that the block's facts bind the write); a snapshot of another guidance file is listed, since it is that kind's history, with **Pin** alone, and a snapshot of a foreign kind is listed with no action at all.

**Banners.** The six new codes render through the unchanged banner as `<code>: <message>` — the shell's fixed catalogue messages through `PlainTextLine` — with no "untrusted" prefix and no detail, and `run-active` renders there too with its existing copy. `DENIAL_CODES` stays the four `LaunchGate` denials: none of `guidance-file-invalid`, `guidance-file-changed`, `guidance-block-modified`, `guidance-block-malformed`, `guidance-unmanaged` and `snapshot-unavailable` is a verdict on a producer — they are refusals of a write over a file whose state does not match what the surface showed, or of a store that could not be written, and `run-active` is a wait. A refusal keeps the block open with the typed gate intact and the final button enabled again, so the user can read the banner and try the same act — except `guidance-file-changed`, where the facts the block was bound to are stale by definition: the block closes and that kind's status **and** snapshots are fetched afresh. That behavior is exercised for all three writing commands, not for `guidance_apply` alone: `guidance_remove` and `guidance_restore` carry their own rejection cases, and a `guidance_pin` rejection reaches the same banner, leaves its row's flag exactly as it was, refetches nothing and offers Pin again. A `guidance_preview` rejection reaches it too, both as a `ShellError` — rendered `<code>: <message>`, no block opened — and as a plain `Error`, which shows `unexpected error` and nothing of its message.

**Guards.** While a run is active, Preview, Remove, Restore, the open block's input and its final button are all frozen — the shell refuses the three writing commands with `run-active` in the same window — while the file input and Pin stay live; everything is live again once the run ends, with the typed gate surviving the run. One preview or write at a time, and one pin at a time, each enforced by a ref set synchronously as well as by the buttons' own `disabled`. Every async path carries the `mountedRef` unmount guard, and every one of the seven commands has a test that proves it: each is driven until it is in flight, the panel is unmounted, and the promise is then settled both ways — no throw, no `console.error`, and **no IPC call after the unmount**, which is the assertion with teeth, since the success paths of `guidance_apply`, `guidance_remove`, `guidance_restore` and `guidance_pin` all refetch. (The earlier claim that every async path carried the guard was true of the code and untested for four of the seven; the test that stood named four commands and exercised three.)

Two stale-response guards sit beside it. `guidance_status` and `guidance_snapshots` are sequenced by request id **per kind**, so the two kinds never invalidate each other and a pre-pick or pre-commit response landing later is dropped in favour of the newer one — asserted across kinds now, with both tables rendered at once and a pin on one kind leaving the other kind's rows, status line and fetches untouched. And a **context generation** — the same shape as `spawnGenerationRef` — is bumped by a workspace pick and by a committed file name, so a `guidance_preview` or a `guidance_apply` still in flight when either happens is dropped whole (R1-001): without it a preview resolving after the documented reset re-opened the write block bound to the *previous* project's file, digest and gate inside the new project's section, and the shell's `fileSha256` binding cannot tell two projects apart whose `AGENTS.md` came byte-identical from one template — the realistic case for this very feature. The write's continuation carries the same guard for the same reason: its receipt names a file, and posting it under the section the user has moved to would read as a write into that project. A workspace pick resets the section — the open block, the typed gate and the result line go — before both kinds are fetched afresh; committing a different guidance file closes a block bound to the previous one and clears the result line with it.

The committed guidance file name follows the shell's own answer and never the typed draft (R1-004/R3-005). It moves only when a `guidance_status` resolves, so a name the shell refuses with `guidance-file-invalid` cannot become the name the automatic fetches reuse: before, clearing the field and leaving it poisoned the ref with the empty name, and the next automatic fetch — a Pin, or the next workspace pick — reused it with `surfaceError: false`, swallowed the same rejection, and took the status line, Preview, Remove and every Restore off the surface with no banner to explain it.

**Content security.** A managed file name carrying a literal `<b>`, a C0 byte and a bidi override renders in the status line, in the block's `file:` line and in the gate label as text, with the markup literal and the controls stripped; a proposed block carrying a `<script>` tag, a Markdown link, an HTML anchor and an HTML comment renders line by line as the text it is, with no `<script>`, no anchor, no `href` and no comment node anywhere in the Guidance region and no ESC or override in its `textContent`. **The proposed block is display-only throughout**: nothing here reads it as an instruction and nothing renders it as markup, so no content path — a file name, a note, proposal text, a guidance note — can approve or trigger anything ([HAP-001-R22](heavy-asset-publication.md), TM-001-R1). Nothing pre-fills the gate: a publish proposal and a candidate row naming the very short digest the gate wants, the digest shown in the label itself, and a programmatic `input.value` with no input event all leave the input empty and the final button disabled; only the user's own typing enables it. **TM-001-R7** is met the way the 5b approval meets it — the identity-bound facts (the file, the action, the block exactly as it would be written, the file's digest) and the act-as identity on the surface before the decision, no harness-authored summary anywhere, and a confirmation that cannot be completed without having read the digest — and the request then carries the shell's own digest, never the typed one, with the shell re-verifying the file against it and refusing `guidance-file-changed` if it moved.

**Totals and evidence.** `AgentPanel.test.tsx` grows by fifty-one (185 → 236: twelve for the whole-outbox listing, thirty-nine for the guidance section, its snapshots, its codes and guards, and its content security) and `harness.test.ts` by fifteen (52 → 67); `app.test.tsx` keeps its five, its mount mock now answering `guidance_status` and `guidance_snapshots` the way the shell does with no workspace (a `workspace-unavailable` rejection), as it already answers `outbox_status` and `publications_list`. `pnpm -r test` **325 → 391**, green on one plain run and on three consecutive `CI=true` runs; `pnpm --filter renderer exec vitest run src/AgentPanel.test.tsx` ten consecutive runs at 236/236; `pnpm -r lint` and `pnpm -r build` (`tsc -b && vite build`) clean; no `.only`; provenance sweep empty. **The renderer review-fix pass that followed took `AgentPanel.test.tsx` 236 → 270 and the suite 391 → 425** (`harness.test.ts` keeps its 67, one of them rewritten to assert the awaited rejection rather than the fixture that produced it — R3-018 — with the key set pinned so a wrapper that added a key of its own would be caught; `app.test.tsx` 5, `ApprovalSurface.test.tsx` 23, `HarnessPanel.test.tsx` 49, `PlainTextLine.test.tsx` 11), green on `pnpm -r lint`, `pnpm -r test`, `pnpm -r build`, three consecutive `CI=true pnpm -r test` runs at 425/425, and ten consecutive `AgentPanel.test.tsx` runs at 270/270; no `.only`; provenance sweep empty. Two reds this sitting owns rather than inherits, both recorded because they were regressions in already-green tests: three slice-3 and slice-5 tests failed with `expected [ '' ] to include 'claude-code'` beside an uncaught `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'length')` in `renderGuidanceBlock`, because their counted-invoke helper answers every command it does not handle with `null`, so `guidance_snapshots` resolved with `null` and the table's length check threw mid-render and took the whole panel down — fixed in the panel by treating a payload that is not the list the contract promises as no table, the fail-safe-to-nothing discipline `handleFrame` already applies to a frame it does not recognize, and not by editing the helper; and `tsc -b` reported two `Type 'string | null' is not assignable to type 'string | undefined'` on the gate, from narrowing the absent-file case through a boolean instead of through the digest itself.

**The renderer review-fix pass** is recorded one behavior at a time, the way slices 3 to 5b record theirs and the first sitting could not. Thirteen reds, all quoted from the run that produced them before any source moved: `expected <div …>…</div> to be null` twice for a `guidance_preview` resolving after a workspace pick and after a committed file name (R1-001), and `expected <div role="alert" …>…</div> to be null` for the same preview *rejecting* after a pick; `expected 'Guidance note AGENTS.md: written repl…' to be null` for a `guidance_apply` resolving after a pick (the same guard, extended to the write's own continuation — its receipt names a file); `expected { kind: 'guidance', file: '' } to deeply equal { kind: 'guidance', file: 'AGENTS.md' }` and `expected { kind: 'guidance', file: 'notes.txt' } to deeply equal { kind: 'guidance', file: 'CLAUDE.md' }` for the refused name poisoning the committed one (R1-004/R3-005); `expected false to be true` for Remove offered on a `current` status with no short digest (R3-008); `expected 'written replace, snapshot 0123456789a…' to be 'Guidance note AGENTS.md: written repl…'` for the receipt naming neither kind nor file (R1-003/R3-007); `expected [ 'file: AGENTS.md', …(4) ] to deeply equal [ 'file: AGENTS.md', …(5) ]` and `…(5) to deeply equal …(6)` for the missing removal and restore disclosures (R1-005, R1-002); `expected [ 'Pin Restore', 'Unpin Restore' ] to deeply equal [ '', 'Unpin Restore' ]` for a foreign-kind snapshot still offered both actions (R1-006); `TestingLibraryElementError: Unable to find role="region" and name "Candidates"` beside an uncaught `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'map')` in `AgentPanel.tsx:2490` for a `candidates_list` payload that is not a list (R1-007 — the same fail-safe `fetchGuidanceSnapshots` already had, now applied to the whole-outbox listing); and `expected 2 to be 1` for a double-clicked Pin firing twice (R3-013). Seven already-green tests then moved with the receipt copy and the two new disclosures, and are recorded as edits to expectations, not as reds.

The rest of that pass is coverage of behavior the code already had, and is recorded as a pin rather than as a red: every guidance continuation's unmount no-op, one test per command and both settle directions (R3-001 — verified to bite by temporarily dropping one `mountedRef` guard, which turned three of the seven red at `expected 13 to be 11`); the workspace pick clearing a *completed* write's receipt (R3-002 — the test that stood completed no write, so its claim was vacuously true; the open block and the receipt cannot in fact coexist, since every block-opening path clears the receipt, so the two halves are now two tests); rejections of `guidance_remove`, `guidance_restore` and `guidance_pin` (R3-003) and a `ShellError` from `guidance_preview` (R3-004); the snapshots refetch on `guidance-file-changed` that the `it.each` title claimed and the body did not check (R3-010); the proposed block's line splitting — trailing newline, interior blank line, no trailing newline, empty proposal, and a CRLF file's `\r` stripped (R3-006); per-kind independence with both snapshot tables rendered at once (R3-009); the positive half of the same-file Restore restriction (R3-012); and the gate's whitespace boundary and the absent-file gate's negative set (R3-016).

Deviations from the renderer brief, each with its reason: **the four automatic guidance fetches swallow their rejections** — the brief has the renderer show the banner for `guidance-file-invalid`, which it does for the fetch the user asks for by committing a file name and for every command a button invokes, but the mount-time and pick-time fetches clear their kind's line and raise nothing, because `workspace-unavailable` is the ordinary answer with no workspace (the outbox line's own precedent) and a listing the user did not ask for names no act to retry; **Pin stays live while a run is active**, the brief having frozen preview, apply, remove and restore and said nothing of pin, and the shell's own `guidance_pin` taking no supervisor, so freezing it would be stricter than the contract; **a payload that is not the list its DTO promises renders as nothing** rather than throwing (above) — for `guidance_snapshots` as no table, and, after the review pass, for `candidates_list` as no rows — the two places this renderer does not simply trust its DTO, because a render-time throw takes the panel down with it, freeze guards included; **the write block has no Cancel** (another Preview, a Remove, a Restore, a file change, or a workspace pick replaces it — the 5b approval block's precedent); and **the block's `file:` line for a restore names the status's file, not the snapshot's**, since Restore is offered only where the two are the same. Debt this half names without deciding: the guidance file name is validated only by the shell, the renderer sending what was typed and showing the rule's own message — never the name — when it is refused; the section offers no proposal of its own for a `modified` or `malformed` block beyond what `guidance_preview` answers; the result line survives until the next block opens, the next committed file name, or the next workspace pick — it names its own kind and file since the review pass, so a surviving receipt says what it is a receipt for; the snapshots table is unpaginated (the store is bounded at five unpinned per kind and file, so the table is small by construction, but nothing here enforces that); `taken at` renders the instant in ISO 8601 UTC with no time-zone or locale decision made; and the file input's draft survives a rejected commit, so the field can read a name the status line does not — the committed name behind it now stays on the last one the shell accepted, so the divergence is visible rather than load-bearing. Debt the review pass names rather than closes: **a snapshot's bytes are never shown before a restore**, and cannot be — no command on the wire returns a snapshot's content, and none was invented here, so the surface discloses the absence (and the deletion a snapshot of an absent file performs) instead of showing the evidence, which is weaker than every other gate in this panel and is stated as such rather than dressed up as a preview. Everything here stays **dev-mode-only** exactly as the Rust half states: every status, preview, write, snapshot and pin above came from a mocked `invoke` — no packaged build, no live shell exercised by any renderer test, and the shell's own re-verification of a file digest has run against no renderer request here — and no producer signing (the producer line's "(unknown)" is unchanged).

### Relationship to VP-001

Every result behind this slice — every passing test listed above; `cargo fmt --all --check` clean; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` (Linux) clean; `cargo clippy -p omnifrons-supervisor -p omnifrons-adapters -p omnifrons-app -p omnifrons-domain --all-targets --all-features --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu -- -D warnings` clean (cross-compiled, never executed; the shell stays outside that check for the `windres` reason slice 3 recorded); `cargo test --workspace --all-targets --all-features` all green at **632** tests, summed over every test binary's own `passed` count the way slices 3 to 5b were (domain 90, app 182, adapters 125, supervisor 69, shell 166; +86 over slice 5b's 546: domain +20, app +24, adapters +13, supervisor 0, shell +29 — eleven of them the risk and reliability review-fix pass above, and the last one the scoped re-review's bidi refusal, R1-004). **The shell's 166 is its Linux count, and the Windows total is 161, not 166** (R1-007, correcting an earlier claim here): six whole `#[test]`s in `src-tauri/src` are `#[cfg(unix)]` — `ipc/guidance.rs`'s link at the guidance name, `ipc/publication.rs`'s validation-refused approval, live supervised run, second-link refusal, and planted link, and `ipc/commands.rs`'s linked outbox — against the single `#[cfg(not(unix))]` counterpart, and the shell's four integration-test binaries carry no platform gate at all, so 166 − 6 + 1 = 161 there. `cargo test -p omnifrons-shell --test feature_gate` green (2 passed); `cargo tree -p omnifrons-adapters -e normal --depth 1` still exactly `nix`, `omnifrons-app`, `omnifrons-domain`, `serde`, `serde_json`, `sha2`, and the supervisor's still exactly `nix`, `omnifrons-app`, `omnifrons-domain`, `tokio`, `tracing`; no new `unsafe` (the two precedents, `demo.rs` and `pty.rs`, are the only sites); the provenance sweep empty — was produced against a `cargo test` development build, on one contributor's Linux machine, not a packaged release build on a pinned Windows/macOS/Linux baseline, and not against macOS or Windows at all: the Windows clippy pass is a cross-compilation type-check, and the unix-gated link tests have not run on macOS here (CI's `macos-latest` leg is where they do). Per [VP-001-R2](desktop-stack-verification-plan.md#product-requirements), this is `dev-mode-only` and therefore **not** admissible as VP-S28 evidence; it is an input to writing that scenario's procedure well (above), and, for the guidance note specifically, to fixing what "equals the template with only the path substituted" is checked against.

## Slice 5d — wrong-root detection, `misplaced`, and its three remedies (HAP-001 D16)

This slice's Rust half (`omnifrons-domain`, `omnifrons-app`, `omnifrons-adapters`, `src-tauri`) implements HAP-001 § Wrong-root detection and remedies at the post-run-scan half of D16's default: a scan of the active workspace root at every run end and on demand, the `misplaced` detection condition it produces, and the three remedies HAP-001-R32 fixes — quarantine, publish, ignore — each taken only on an explicit user choice bound to a name and a digest. It also carries the output-discipline report HAP-001-R33 and R34 fix, and one Windows-flake fix in `omnifrons-supervisor`'s own bookkeeping test. The handoff preflight and a file watcher (D16 names both), real providers, `catalog_repair`, the stale-`.part` sweep, recovery re-approval, MRP-001 tombstones, git awareness, Windows owner-only ACLs, and the vault and multi-project containment stay out (5e or later). **Dev-mode only**: nothing here is VP-001 evidence ([Relationship to VP-001](#relationship-to-vp-001-7)). The renderer half landed after the Rust half, against the wire shapes in [IPC shapes](#ipc-shapes-5), and is recorded in [Renderer](#renderer-7); it also closes the four carried renderer findings of 5c's ledger (R3-011, R3-014, R3-015, R3-017), which are renderer-only and needed no wire change here.

### Scope

In: a depth-first, no-follow walk of the active workspace root that decides each entry's type from the directory handle it holds, opens each file once, detects its type from the head of that handle, and digests the whole file only when the project's classification policy calls it `generated-heavy`; the outbox subtree and a *repository's own metadata directory* — recognized by what it holds, not by the name `.git` — excluded by the product's own rule and never by an ignore rule (HAP-001-R43, R44); a `MisplacedFinding` carrying a bounded project-relative name, size, digest, detected type, class and reason and never an absolute path (RCS-001-R14), with a heavy file found at a name no finding may carry counted and surfaced rather than dropped; the ignore ledger, append-only JSONL under the product work area, keyed to project identity, name and digest, failing closed on a corrupt line; the quarantine remedy, a handle-anchored rename on one volume and a copy-then-unlink otherwise, with both staging siblings created exclusively, the destination opened without following a link at its name, and the digest re-verified there before the original is unlinked; the publish remedy, copying from the one held handle into the outbox root as a new unattributed entry whose name is created exclusively, and leaving the original exactly where it was found (HAP-001-R28); HAP-001-R20's link-count refusal applied to the misplaced file at both remedies; a scan table keyed to the project it walked; four IPC commands, one new `AdapterEventDto` kind emitted at run end, and three new `ShellErrorCode`s beside the existing `outbox-linked`.

### Design decisions (spike defaults, each disclosed)

Every default below is the spike's; a feature that assumes it before the owner disposes must say so, and this section does.

- **D1 — no git awareness, and the classifier stands in for tracked-ness** (`crates/omnifrons-domain/src/wrong_root.rs`, `crates/omnifrons-app/src/wrong_root.rs`). Nothing in this repository reads `.gitignore` or the git index, and adding `gix` or `git2` would change the dependency table `crates/omnifrons-adapters/tests/deps.rs` asserts, which is an owner-visible surface. HAP-001-R32's operative arm — "any location outside the project's outbox" — needs no index, and HAP-001-R1 and R3 (a Git-managed file is never published and never flagged `misplaced` in its tracked location) are satisfied by the mechanism slice 5a already has: a finding the policy classifies `git-tracked` is not misplaced at all. **This was verified against the code before it was relied on, and it holds only in the approximate sense**: `OutboxPolicy`'s shipped `git-tracked` row claims plain text under 1 MiB with one of fourteen source and configuration extensions (`crates/omnifrons-app/src/outbox_policy.rs`, `GIT_TRACKED_TEXT_EXTENSIONS`, `GIT_TRACKED_TEXT_MAX_BYTES`), so "tracked" here means *the policy's class*, not the index. Two consequences, stated plainly: a `.rs` file the user never added to Git is still treated as tracked and never flagged, and **a heavy file the user deliberately committed — a PDF checked into the repository — classifies `generated-heavy` and is flagged `misplaced`**, which HAP-001-R3's acceptance item asks not to happen. Closing that needs the index, and the index needs a dependency this slice does not add. `WrongRootReason` therefore carries `InProjectOutsideOutbox` alone and no `TrackedPath` variant: a tracked path is not a reason, it is a filter applied before a reason exists.
- **D2 — only `generated-heavy` is misplaced** (`omnifrons_domain::wrong_root::is_misplaced_class`). `git-tracked` is D1's arm; `portable-text` is a Markdown note HAP-001-R2 refuses as an artifact at all; `executable` and `unclassified` are narrowed out on purpose. HAP-001-R4 routes an executable to quarantine as a *candidate entry*'s class, and HAP-001-R1 blocks an unclassified entry pending the Asset Policy Owner — neither has a destination this slice can offer, `artifact_approve` admits `generated-heavy` alone (slice 5b), and flagging either would report every build output in a worktree. D16's "or exceeding the size threshold" arm is not implemented separately: the policy's own rows carry the size bound, so a heavy *type* is the whole test here.
- **D3 — the root set is the active workspace root only, walked depth-first** (`crates/omnifrons-adapters/src/fs_wrong_root_scanner.rs`). Walked without following links, and every file opened once without following a link at its name, exactly as `FsCandidateProber` opens an outbox entry. **On unix every fact the walk uses comes from a directory handle and no path is resolved twice**: the descent is `openat` with `O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW` relative to the handle above it; what an entry *is* — and so whether it is examined at all — is `fstatat` with `AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW` on that handle; and **the listing is `fdopendir` over a duplicate of that same handle** (`nix::dir::Dir`, via the `dir` feature added for exactly this), never a `read_dir` of the directory's path. The listing was the last fact still taken by path, and calling it "a hint only" covered the names a listing *adds* and said nothing about the ones it *omits* (R1-013): a same-user process that swaps any ancestor for an empty directory in the window between the descent and the listing makes the whole subtree contribute nothing at all — the frame pops with its entries counted in none of `scanned`, `excluded` or `unreadable` while `truncated` still reads `false`, which is silent loss under a report that calls itself complete. **The same shape had been left standing in the other arm of that same function** (R1-024): the Windows listing skipped a `read_dir` entry it could not read (`let Ok(entry) = entry else { continue; }`), so that name was dropped from the walk while it was counted in none of `scanned`, `excluded` or `unreadable` and the listing still reported itself `complete`, leaving `truncated` false. It propagates now, exactly as the unix arm's `?` does, which makes the directory `unreadable` — a counted outcome — instead. A link is counted and skipped, never dereferenced. **On Windows none of that handle-relative half exists** (R1-014). `std` offers no relative directory open, stat or listing there and this repository adds no Windows API dependency, so the descent, the `entry_kind` stat and the listing all re-resolve the joined path, and `FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` refuses a reparse point at the final component **only**: one planted at an *ancestor* between two descents is followed and the walk leaves the project. The same shape is in `open_misplaced` and in `fs_candidate_prober::open_entry`, so a finding can name a file outside the project and the publish remedy, resolving that name the same way, would copy an outside file into the outbox as this project's own unattributed output. The quarantine remedy's deletion is not reachable there — `FsOutboxEntryOps` reports `Unverifiable` on Windows, which keeps the original — so the exposure is copy and disclosure, not loss. Nothing in this repository can execute Windows, which is precisely why this is narrowed here rather than asserted for both platforms. **The walk is depth-first, and that is a descriptor bound, not a traversal preference**: the open directory handles at any instant are the ones on the path the walk is on — at most `MAX_SCAN_DEPTH + 1` — never one per pending sibling, which is what a breadth-first walk holds and what exhausts a 256-descriptor process on a `node_modules`-scale worktree, losing every finding under the directories it can then no longer open. **The vault and other registered projects do not exist in this repository**, so HAP-001-R32's other two arms are left unimplemented rather than faked. The product work area is excluded by construction: HAP-001-R7 already guarantees it never resolves inside a registered workspace root, and `scan_project` re-checks that **before** it walks, so a work area configured inside the project refuses the scan rather than being walked and then complained about. Bounded in three directions. `MAX_SCANNED_ENTRIES = 20_000` **entries examined — a directory counts like a file**, or a tree of empty directories would walk without bound while the cap meant to stop it never fired; `MAX_SCAN_DEPTH = 32` levels; and `MAX_DIRECTORY_NAMES` names collected from any **one** directory. The third is a separate bound because the first was never one (R1-015): entries *examined* does not bound entries *listed*, so a single directory holding a few million entries — which the same-user producer this scan polices creates as easily as any other — was collected and sorted whole before the entry cap could fire once, and this scan runs automatically at every run end. `MAX_DIRECTORY_NAMES` is set **equal to** `MAX_SCANNED_ENTRIES` so the bound costs no fidelity: every name in a listing is an entry the walk would examine, so a directory wide enough to reach it already drives the entry cap past its own limit, and a listing cut short here can never hide a file that a complete listing would have reported without `truncated` being set anyway. *Which* names a cut-short listing holds is the platform's directory order and not a chosen subset; past the bound the report is partial and says so, and nothing about which part is claimed. Past any of the three, and whenever the walk runs out of file descriptors, the report says `truncated` rather than folding what it could not see into `unreadable` and calling itself complete.
- **D4 — when, and what it holds** (`src-tauri/src/ipc/commands.rs`, `RunEndTracker`). A scan runs at every adapter run end, emitting one event and nothing else, and on demand through `wrongroot_scan`; remedies are always on demand. **No handle survives the scan**, and during it the walk holds only the path it is on: each file's handle is dropped before the next is opened, and the directory handles are those of the current path and no more (D3). The 32-handle cap HAP-001 D22 sets is shared with the approval surface, and a scan whose descriptor use grew with the tree could starve it. A launch with no run record still scans, so `misplaced` is then the launch's *first* event, with no `candidates` frame before it and the terminal `state` frame after it on a contiguous `seq`. A scan that could not run emits the fixed `diagnostic` in the `misplaced` frame's place and records nothing, never a claim that nothing was misplaced. The scan takes no lock and is not frozen by a live run — a run-end scan is exactly a scan while a process is finishing — while **every remedy takes the publication surface lock and refuses with the existing `run-active`** while any supervised process runs, the same guard 5b's approval and publication commands and 5c's guidance writes hold.
- **D5 — `misplaced` is recomputed, never persisted, and every record names the project it walked** (`src-tauri/src/wrong_root_state.rs`). The contract calls it a detection condition, not a publication state (HAP-001 § Artifact states), so the findings live in shell state for the process's lifetime and are cleared when the active workspace changes — one project's findings never speak for the next, the same rule the run table follows. The only durable fact is the ignore decision. **Clearing on a workspace change is not on its own enough**, and this is not a hypothetical: one process holds one table, a run launched in project A finishes on its own thread, and its run-end scan repopulates that table *after* the user has switched to B. A reader that assumed the record was the active project's would then take its workspace from B and its finding from A, and with identical bytes at the same relative name — a shared fixture, a copied artifact — the quarantine remedy would delete a file in a project this session never scanned. So `ScanRecord` carries the `ProjectIdentity` it walked and every read is keyed to it: status, listing, remedy lookup and forget all answer for the active project alone, and for none when no workspace is active. Neither the status nor the listing *fails* without a workspace; they answer exactly as they would for a project no scan has run for.
- **D6 — the ignore ledger's key, and where it lives** (`crates/omnifrons-app/src/ignore_ledger.rs`, `crates/omnifrons-adapters/src/jsonl_ignore_ledger.rs`). "Recorded against the file's identity and digest" is implemented as **project identity + project-relative name + content digest**. A finding never carries a device path (RCS-001-R14) and a device/inode pair is neither portable nor stable across a restart on every platform this product targets, so the name stands in for identity. Two consequences, disclosed: the same bytes moved to another name are offered again, and a file rewritten in place is offered again — which is what the remedy promises ("until its content changes"). The ledger is `<work area>/ignore/decisions.jsonl`, `0o600` on unix, `sync_data`ed per line, one `schema` per line. **"Corrupt" is not only "not JSON"**: a line of another schema, a digest or project id that is not 64 hex characters, a detected type or class no enum knows, and a field this version never wrote (`deny_unknown_fields`) each fail the whole read closed — never "not ignored", which would silently re-offer every file the user already dismissed, and never a scan that quietly continues: a corrupt ledger is `work-area-invalid` and the scan does not complete. `WorkAreaRoot` therefore gains a fourth subdirectory, `ignore/`, beside `journal/`, `recovery/` and `snapshots/`, exactly as slice 5c added the third.
- **D7 — the quarantine lives at `<app-data>/quarantine/`, a sibling of the work area** (`crates/omnifrons-app/src/quarantine.rs`, `src-tauri/src/publication_state.rs`). Created and re-checked with the same discipline `WorkAreaRoot` runs: owner-only where the platform expresses it (unix `0o700`; Windows ACLs are outside `std` and stay the disclosed residual), canonicalized before use, outside every registered workspace root, and re-canonicalized and re-checked at every use so a workspace registered over it afterwards refuses the next move. **Not inside the work area**, whose contents HAP-001 fixes as the journal, the recovery entries and the snapshots. A spike default under RCS-001 D3, which decides only that the quarantine is "a dedicated directory outside any workspace"; the siting beside HAP-001 D1's work area is this slice's choice.
- **D8 — the quarantine remedy is the only one that removes the original** (`omnifrons_app::quarantine::quarantine`). A handle-anchored move when the platform offers one and both ends sit on one volume (`EXDEV` reports a fallback, never an error), else a copy from the held handle followed by an unlink. **That handle-anchored move is `linkat(source_dir, name, dest_dir, dest_name)` followed by `unlinkat(source_dir, name)`, and no longer `renameat`** (R1-021). `renameat` is atomic, which is why it was reached for — but POSIX `renameat` *silently replaces* an existing destination, and this is the branch that actually runs on unix in the normal case, both ends on one volume. The destination name is an 8-hex short digest (`SHORT_HEX_CHARS = 8`, `crates/omnifrons-domain/src/executable.rs`) and a sanitized display name, both halves chosen by whoever misplaced the file, so a 32-bit prefix collision against an already-quarantined entry is brute-forceable rather than hypothetical — and the file it would have destroyed is one the user deliberately preserved, whose original was already unlinked. That is exactly what the paragraph below said this remedy never does, asserted for the copy branch alone while the branch that runs did the opposite. `link(2)` is the one exclusive create, here as at both remedies' final names: it refuses with `EEXIST` and never follows a link at the name. What is traded is atomicity, in the direction that costs nothing — between the two calls the file answers to *both* names, so an interruption leaves a duplicate rather than a file with no name at all — and when the `unlinkat` fails the `linkat` is undone (the destination *this call* created, and only that, HAP-001-R28) before the copy path takes over from the state it found. **The `unlinkat` is guarded by the same by-handle identity check `remove_original` runs**, taken from the same `FsOutboxEntryOps` so the two cannot drift, because two calls can be raced where one atomic call cannot: unlinking a source name that had stopped holding this file would remove a file this product did not create, which is the same HAP-001-R28 rule the destination is protected by. **And which file the `linkat` actually linked is checked before that guard runs, because the guard alone had the two races exactly backwards** (R1-028). `linkat` resolves the source *name*, never the held handle, and the guard reads that same name afterwards — so a swap landing *before* the link and one landing *after* it end in the same state on the source side, and an arm that answered `Renamed` to both was honest about only one of them. Swapped after the link, the destination really is this file's new name and the source name is someone else's; not unlinking is right there, and that is the case the code argued. Swapped before it, the `linkat` linked the intruder: the destination is a second name this product created for a file it was never asked about, the approved file is left with no name at all, and the payload reported `outcome: quarantined`, `originalKept: false`, `renamed-unverified` — that the file had left the project — while nothing had been removed. The window is not tight, either: it runs from `quarantine()`'s own identity pre-check to the `linkat`, with the whole `digest_reader` pass over the held handle inside it, and the `Renamed` branch never cleans up, so the product-created link stayed in a quarantine directory that has no listing and no reveal affordance. Only the destination separates the two cases, so the adapter now compares the destination name against the held handle — the same by-handle comparison `remove_original` runs, from the same `FsOutboxEntryOps`, so the sites cannot drift — and when they differ it undoes the link *this call* created (HAP-001-R28 again) and reports `Fallback(Failed)`, handing the copy path a destination it verifies for itself. With that check in front, the guard's remaining arm is sound: a source name that no longer holds this file needs no unlink at all — the destination link has been confirmed to be this file's name and the move is done — and undoing it there would be worse than useless, leaving the file with no name at all. **The claim that this pair of arms could not be reached from a synchronous test is withdrawn for `DifferentFile`**: `rename_into` is a public trait method the adapter suite already calls directly, and arranging the window's *end state* rather than racing it pins the pre-link swap in `crates/omnifrons-adapters/tests/wrong_root_fs.rs` (`a_source_name_swapped_before_the_link_is_not_reported_as_a_move`), which is the test that produced the correction above. The **`Missing`** arm alone still needs real concurrency — a source name unlinked between the `linkat` and the guard with nothing put back at it — and it is that arm, and only that one, that is disclosed here rather than pinned; the `SameFile` arm is what every rename-branch test exercises. `EEXIST` reports a fallback like `EXDEV`, so a destination something already holds is verified and reused, or refused with the name left holding what it held, by exactly the copy branch's own code; the price of routing it there rather than adding a `RenameOutcome` the wire has no token for is one staging copy written and discarded in that collision case. **What is anchored to a handle on that copy path, and what is not** (R1-017): the source is the held handle, the identity re-check and the original's unlink run relative to the source's own directory handle, and the destination verification opens relative to the quarantine handle — but the copy's *writes inside the quarantine directory* (the staging create, the exclusive create of the final name, and the cleanup of a destination this copy created) are **by path**. `copy_into` is given `dest_path` and not the `dest_dir` handle `quarantine()` already holds, because `omnifrons-app` depends on `omnifrons-domain` and `thiserror` alone and `std` offers no `openat`, `linkat` or `unlinkat`; anchoring them would mean three more methods on `QuarantineStore`, which is a port change and not a fix. The precedent is `publication::write_recovery_entry` and the publish remedy's own copy-in, and the quarantine directory is owner-only and outside every registered workspace, which is why this is named as a residual rather than closed. Order: the quarantine's containment re-check, the handle's own `fstat` (a regular file), HAP-001-R18's check that the name still holds the handle's file, the digest taken from the handle and compared to the one the request bound to, then the move, then the destination re-opened and digested, and **only then** the identity re-checked and the original unlinked. **The copy path's staging sibling is created exclusively** (`create_new`), like the publish remedy's: its name, `.part-<short digest hex>-<sanitized display name>`, is fully predictable to whoever misplaced the file, and a `create` that followed a link planted there would write the moved bytes straight through into any file the user can write — after which the destination verification, opening the link that the rename then moved into place, would digest the victim and *pass*. **The destination is therefore opened without following a link at its name** (`QuarantineStore::open_dest`, the same `openat`/`O_NOFOLLOW` an outbox entry takes), so that check reads the file that arrived and nothing a link points at. **And the copy path's *final* name is created exclusively too, through `link(2)` from that staging sibling — never a `rename`** (R1-017), which is now true of *both* branches rather than of one (R1-021). The two remedies have to guarantee the same thing at their final name, and D9 had already rejected `rename` for the publish side for the reason that applies here unchanged: it replaces a file, a link, or a *dangling* link at the destination without a word, so a quarantine over a name something else already held would destroy that file on its way to reporting a verified move — the one thing HAP-001-R28 says this product never does. `AlreadyExists` is not itself a refusal: the destination is verified immediately afterwards through the no-follow open above, so an earlier attempt of this same remedy verifies and the move completes, and any other content fails there and the remedy refuses **with the name still holding exactly what it held**. **That verification reads three facts from the destination's own handle, and the link count is the third** (R1-022): digest and size alone cannot tell this remedy's earlier result apart from a hard link a same-user producer planted at the destination pointing at a file *outside* the quarantine directory that carries the very bytes the request bound to — accepting which unlinks the project's copy and leaves an alias of an inode the producer still holds another name for, and can rewrite once the surface has said the file is safely quarantined. That is HAP-001-R20's case at this remedy's own final name; the publish remedy's twin already refused it through `CandidateProber::probe`, and the `QuarantineStore::open_dest` path did not, because it never read the link count from the handle it opened. It does now, through the same `wrong_root::link_count` seam, so the two cannot drift — and on Windows that seam yields `None`, so the refusal cannot be made there any more than at an outbox entry or at a misplaced file: the same disclosed residual, widened to a third site rather than a new one. The cleanup on that failure path removes only what this copy created, for the same HAP-001-R28 reason. The price is the one D9 already names for publish: a file system with no hard links reports the remedy unavailable rather than replacing. **And a verification that fails after the handle-anchored move has already run is not a refusal.** By the time that move reports `Renamed` the destination name holds the handle's file and the original name no longer does — the `unlinkat` succeeded, or a fallback was reported instead and nothing moved at all; reporting a refusal there would tell the user the file is still where it was while it sits in the quarantine directory under a name only that payload knows, and would skip the `forget` that stops the list offering remedies for a file that has moved. The outcome is `renamed-unverified` instead: `originalKept: false`, the destination name in `name`, and a token that says which half failed. On the copy path nothing has moved yet, so a verification failure there *is* a refusal — and the unverified copy is removed rather than left in the quarantine directory to be mistaken for a moved file. On Windows neither the by-handle identity comparison nor the relative unlink exists in `std` (`FsOutboxEntryOps` reports `Unverifiable`), so the copy stands, the original is kept, and the payload says so through `originalKept: true` and a fixed `detail` token — the residual HAP-001-R19 requires disclosed, not closed.
- **D9 — the publish remedy never widens the outbox path, and publishes nothing itself** (`omnifrons_app::wrong_root::copy_in_from_misplaced`, `src-tauri/src/ipc/wrong_root.rs`). The bytes are copied from the single no-follow handle on the misplaced file into the **outbox root** as a new unattributed entry named `<short digest hex>-<sanitized display name>`, written to a `.part-` sibling created exclusively and given the entry's final name — **itself created exclusively** — only once the digest matches, then opened once through `FsCandidateProber` — HAP-001-R15 through R20 applied with the misplaced file as the candidate, the link-count refusal (R20) included at both the re-open and the copy-in, and `outbox-linked` reported for it exactly as the outbox path does. **"Created exclusively" is `link(2)` (`std::fs::hard_link`) from the finished `.part-` sibling, which is then unlinked, and not `rename`**: `rename` replaces whatever holds the destination name — a file, a link, a *dangling* link — without a word, so a claim of exclusivity built on it was false, and `Path::exists()` disagreed with it besides, reporting a dangling link as absent while `rename` would have replaced it. **The existence check is not a check at all any more: it is the prober's own no-follow open of that name, relative to the outbox handle** (R1-016). `symlink_metadata` followed by a separate `File::open` was a check-then-open in the one directory HAP-001 declares hostile, and the two halves could answer about different files: a same-user process arriving in the window between them made the guard report the regular file it saw while the open dereferenced whatever had replaced it, so the remedy read and hashed a file chosen by whoever won the race — an unbounded read, at a name it was never asked about. Asking `CandidateProber::probe` instead means one `openat(O_NOFOLLOW)` answers everything: the same bytes are the idempotent reuse; other bytes, a link, a directory, or a file that answers to a second name outside the outbox (HAP-001-R20) are `outbox-unavailable`, never followed, never replaced, never adopted; and nothing openable at the name goes to the write path, whose own create is exclusive and reports the same refusal for a name that is there but unreadable. **Three of those four are refused before a byte of them is read, and "other bytes" is not** (R1-023). A link, a directory and a second-named file are decided from the `openat` itself or from the `fstat` on its one handle, so `CandidateProbe::Escape` and `CandidateProbe::Linked` are reached without reading anything. `CandidateProbe::Regular` is only reachable *after* `hash_from_handle` has streamed the whole file, which is what makes the digest comparison possible at all — so a producer who plants a large regular file at the predictable entry name makes the remedy read it once before refusing it. That is the residual this half already records below, and the sentence here now says the same thing as that one instead of the opposite. `Path::exists()` was doubly wrong for the reason it always was — it follows a link and calls a dangling one absent. `acquire_source` is not widened and the publication transaction is not touched: **the remedy hands the new entry to the existing slice-5c whole-outbox path**, so `candidates_list { runId: null }` lists it, `artifact_approve` takes its own explicit per-artifact approval (HAP-001-R22, D3, which excludes `misplaced` and unattributed entries from any standing policy), and `artifact_publish` runs the transaction — over an entry the product itself created, which is what lets that transaction's cleanup unlink it (HAP-001-R28). The original is left exactly where it was found; removing it is a separate explicit user action this product does not offer. The copy's handle is released rather than held, so the remedy costs nothing against D22's cap. **This reading of "then run the existing slice-5c whole-outbox path over that outbox entry" is the coordinator's decision 6 resolved conservatively** — the remedy prepares, the user still approves — and is flagged as an interpretation rather than a re-opened decision.
- **D10 — a remedy names a finding by name and digest, and the digest binds it** (`src-tauri/src/ipc/wrong_root.rs`). Exactly as `artifact_approve` binds a candidate: a name the last scan never reported, or reported at other bytes, is `misplaced-unknown` and nothing moves. The remedy re-opens the file by its project-relative name through one no-follow open per component, so a link standing in for a directory component is refused rather than traversed.
- **D11 — the output-discipline report's two facts** (`omnifrons_domain::wrong_root::OutputDiscipline`, `reported_scope_mode`). HAP-001-R33 makes `enforced` conditional on the mode *and* the write set, so `DeclaredWriteSet` is a value rather than an assumption. The declared write set is reported as the project root because a launch plan sets the harness's `cwd` to the active workspace root and declares its output directory inside it (`validate_cwd_within_workspace`, `OUTPUT_DIR_ENV_KEY`), and nothing wider is ever declared. The scope mode is the **weakest** any registered adapter declares, `advisory` for an empty catalog: the product cannot claim `enforced` because one adapter is sandboxed while another is not, and every built-in adapter of this slice declares `advisory` anyway. HAP-001-R34's disclosure rides every report including a hypothetical `enforced` one; HAP-001-R33's second disclosure rides `advisory` only. Both are carried as text so the renderer states them verbatim rather than composing its own.

### IPC shapes

Four commands, one event kind, three error codes. Every payload is a logical value — a project-relative name, tokens, digests, counts — and never a device path (HAP-001-R5, RCS-001-R14).

`wrongroot_status` — the output-discipline report and whether a scan has run:

```json
{
  "outputDiscipline": "advisory",
  "scopeMode": "advisory",
  "disclosures": [
    "a write inside the project but outside the outbox is detected after the run, never prevented",
    "a write outside the project is possible and is detected after the run, not prevented"
  ],
  "scanned": false,
  "findings": 0
}
```

Under a hypothetical `sandbox-enforced` catalog whose write set is the project root, `outputDiscipline` reads `"enforced"`, `scopeMode` reads `"sandbox-enforced"`, and `disclosures` carries HAP-001-R34's line alone — one entry, never zero.

`wrongroot_scan` — what the walk saw. Counts only; the findings come from `misplaced_list`:

```json
{ "scanned": 12, "findings": 2, "ignored": 1, "excluded": 3, "unreadable": 0, "truncated": false }
```

`misplaced_list` — one row per finding still standing, in walk order:

```json
[
  {
    "name": "docs/report.pdf",
    "size": 4096,
    "sha256": "abababababababababababababababababababababababababababababababab",
    "sha256Short": "abababab",
    "detectedType": "pdf",
    "class": "generated-heavy",
    "reason": "in-project-outside-outbox",
    "remedies": ["quarantine", "publish", "ignore"]
  }
]
```

`misplaced_remedy { name, sha256, remedy }` — `remedy` is one of `"quarantine"`, `"publish"`, `"ignore"` and nothing else. One response shape, three outcomes:

```json
{
  "remedy": "quarantine",
  "outcome": "quarantined",
  "name": "abababab-report.pdf",
  "sha256": "abababababababababababababababababababababababababababababababab",
  "originalKept": false,
  "detail": "renamed"
}
```

```json
{
  "remedy": "publish",
  "outcome": "copied-to-outbox",
  "name": "abababab-report.pdf",
  "sha256": "abababababababababababababababababababababababababababababababab",
  "originalKept": true,
  "detail": null
}
```

```json
{
  "remedy": "ignore",
  "outcome": "ignored",
  "name": null,
  "sha256": "abababababababababababababababababababababababababababababababab",
  "originalKept": true,
  "detail": null
}
```

`detail` is a fixed token, never free text and never a path — **nine of them, each produced by a real run of the remedy and pinned both at its producer and at the mapping that puts it on the wire**: `"renamed"`; `"renamed-unverified"` when the rename ran and the destination could not then be re-read and verified (`originalKept: false`, because the rename removed the original name whatever the verification then found); one of `FallbackReason`'s `"different-volume"` / `"rename-unsupported"` / `"rename-failed"` when the copy path ran and the original was removed; or one of `"unlink-failed"` / `"original-already-gone"` / `"original-changed-during-the-move"` / `"identity-check-unavailable"` when the original was kept. `misplaced_remedy` always carries a `detail` for a quarantine and never for a publish or an ignore, and always carries the `sha256` it bound to.

The new `AdapterEventDto` kind, emitted once per adapter launch by `RunEndTracker::finish` **after `candidates` and before the terminal `state` frame**, with the launch's `seq` staying contiguous across all three:

```json
{
  "stream": "event",
  "body": {
    "id": 42,
    "seq": 10,
    "droppedBefore": 0,
    "kind": "misplaced",
    "payload": {
      "scanned": 12,
      "findings": 2,
      "ignored": 1,
      "excluded": 3,
      "unreadable": 0,
      "truncated": false
    }
  }
}
```

Counts only: no name and no path ride this frame. A scan that could not run emits the fixed `diagnostic` `"the project could not be scanned for output written outside the outbox"` in its place, never a claim that nothing was misplaced.

Three new `ShellErrorCode`s, each with a fixed, path-free message:

| Code | When | Message |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `misplaced-unknown` | `misplaced_remedy` named a finding the last scan did not report, or reported at a different digest; or the file is no longer at that name | `no scan reported that file at that digest; scan again and retry`, or `nothing sits at that name any more; scan again` |
| `quarantine-unavailable` | The quarantine directory resolves inside a registered workspace root, could not be created owner-only, could not be written, or holds a destination that answers to a second name | `the quarantine directory could not be used` / `… resolves inside a registered workspace root` / `… could not be made owner-only` / `the file could not be moved into quarantine` / `the quarantine destination has more than one name` |
| `scan-failed` | The walk could not open or list the active workspace root, so no verdict is claimed for it | `the project could not be scanned` |

Four existing codes carry this slice's other refusals rather than growing the closed set: `refused` when a fact bound at scan time stops holding (not a regular file, the name no longer holds the opened file, content changed since it was found), `outbox-unavailable` when the publish remedy's copy-in cannot create its entry or when something other than that same entry already holds its name, `work-area-invalid` when the ignore ledger cannot be opened, read, or written — the ledger being the work area's, exactly as the publication journal is, and a *corrupt* ledger failing the whole scan closed rather than re-offering a file the user already dismissed — and **`outbox-linked` when the misplaced file's own link count is above one** (HAP-001-R20, applied with the misplaced file as the candidate per HAP-001-R32): the same code the outbox path already uses for the same fact, so one refusal reads the same everywhere. A hard link inside the project to a file outside it would otherwise be quarantined — unlinking the project's name while the bytes live on elsewhere — or copied into the outbox as this project's own output. Not reproducible on Windows, where the by-handle link count is unavailable and this refusal cannot be made: a residual disclosed under HAP-001-R19, with its `#[cfg(not(unix))]` counterpart asserting exactly that the open proceeds there.

### Evidence procedures

**VP-S23** (wrong-root detection, publish remedy, and output-discipline label per scope mode) is the scenario this slice informs, and three of its steps need their procedure written differently than the catalog row reads today, because of what this half found:

- The row asks for an artifact written "into a tracked path outside the outbox, another registered project, and the vault under each scope mode the baseline supports". **Only the first arm is executable against this repository**: there is no vault and no second registered project (D3). The procedure should either fix those two arms as `uncertain` for the baseline that has neither, or state the registration the baseline must perform first; it must not record a pass for an arm nothing implements.
- The row asks that "the label reads `enforced` only under `sandbox-enforced` whose write set is the project root". **No adapter in this repository declares `sandbox-enforced`**, so the `enforced` half of that assertion has no executable path here at all; the procedure needs a baseline that supplies one, or must record the `enforced` arm as unexercised rather than passed by the `advisory` arm's success. The `advisory` arm and both disclosures are exercisable today.
- The row asks to "add a matching `.gitignore` rule" and expects it to change nothing. It does not, because nothing reads it — but the procedure should say *why* the evidence is weak: the verdict is unchanged because no ignore rule is consulted anywhere, not because a consulted rule was overridden. Under D1 the same caveat covers "a tracked path": what the scan actually tests is the classification policy's `git-tracked` class, so a procedure that stages a tracked heavy file and expects it not to be flagged **will fail**, correctly, and the scenario should say which of the two behaviors it is evidencing.

The path-swap step of that row — "choose the publish remedy for one and swap its path during the copy-in" — is directly exercised here on unix (`crates/omnifrons-adapters/tests/wrong_root_fs.rs`, `a_path_swap_during_the_remedy_is_refused`), and its Windows counterpart is the disclosed `Unverifiable` residual, not a pass.

### Deviations and debt

Deviations from the brief, each with its reason:

- **The scanner's contract runs against the real adapter only**, not against an in-memory fake as the ledger's and both remedies' do. A fake scanner satisfying `WrongRootScanner` would have to do real file IO for `open_misplaced` and would prove nothing about the walk; the rules the port must apply are pure instead — `is_excluded` and `finding_for` live in `omnifrons-app` and are tested there directly (`crates/omnifrons-app/tests/wrong_root.rs`), which is the fake side of that contract. Both remedies' contracts are parameterized by their ports and run twice: in `omnifrons-app` over a store that always declines the rename (proving the copy-then-unlink branch on every platform) and a `std`-based entry ops, and in `omnifrons-adapters` over `FsQuarantineStore` and `FsOutboxEntryOps` (taking the rename branch on one volume).
- **The publish remedy prepares rather than publishes** (D9 above), which is a reading of the coordinator's decision 6, taken because HAP-001 D3 excludes `misplaced` and unattributed entries from any standing approval and the remedy choice named the misplaced file, not the outbox entry that follows from it.
- **`copy_in_from_misplaced` returns more than the brief's sketch**: a `CopiedIn` carrying the new entry's name and digest alongside the `CandidateSource`, because the caller has no other way to name the entry it just created.
