- **The publish remedy's copy-in writes by path inside the outbox**, with the `.part-` sibling created through `create_new` and the final name created exclusively through `link(2)` from it — see D9 for why `rename` could not carry that claim. The directory components between the outbox's canonicalization and the create are not re-resolved by handle on that path — the precedent is `publication::write_recovery_entry`, which writes into the work area the same way; the outbox is inside the project rather than owner-only, so the residual is larger here and is named rather than hidden. **On Windows it is larger again**: `std` offers no way to open a directory relative to a held handle there either, so neither the outbox's components nor the quarantine's are re-resolved by handle on that platform, and the check-then-create window is a real one for a same-user process. Depending on `link(2)` also means a file system with no hard links (a FAT-formatted volume, say) reports `outbox-unavailable` for the publish remedy rather than creating the entry; on every file system this product is used on it exists, and an honest refusal was preferred to a `rename` that silently replaces. An entry of the target name already carrying **the same** bytes is reused (the remedy is idempotent); one carrying other bytes — or a link, or a directory — is `outbox-unavailable`, never followed and never overwritten.
- **Three units were written together with their module rather than after a failing test of their own**: `MisplacedFinding::new`'s name validation, `quarantine`, and `copy_in_from_misplaced`. Each one's *first* test was still a red — the contract and adapter tests that exercise them end to end — but the inline unit tests in `omnifrons-domain/src/wrong_root.rs` and the three quarantine refusal tests in `crates/omnifrons-adapters/tests/wrong_root_fs.rs` were green on arrival and are recorded as pins, under the same convention `tests/guidance_contracts.rs` already states. Every other behavior in this slice was driven from a quoted red.
- **The supervisor flake fix changes a test helper, not `observe`.** The 2026-09-10 windows-latest failure of `the_oldest_terminal_entry_is_evicted_past_the_retention_cap` is the red this item answers; it is not reproducible on demand here, so no new failing test stands in for it. Three things changed: the final assertion now prints the value it observed (it printed a fixed message and diagnosed nothing); `wait_for_terminal` now waits for a **confirmed** terminal state — `Exited` or `Killed` — instead of returning on any `Terminal(_)`; and **the assertion that actually flaked now uses that same `confirmed_terminal`**, where it had gone on accepting an unproven `Terminal(_)` that the wait immediately before it rejects — an assertion weaker than the wait that preceded it can only ever pass by accident. `confirmed_terminal` itself is exhaustive over `ProcessTerminalState` with no catch-all, so a variant added later has to have this judgment made for it rather than silently classified "not proof".
- **Descriptor exhaustion is classified by a unit test, not by an end-to-end one.** The walk reports running out of file descriptors as `truncated` and never as `unreadable`, and both sides of that branch are pinned (`only_descriptor_exhaustion_is_classified_as_exhaustion`) — but arranging real descriptor exhaustion *inside* the test process starves every other test sharing it, and lowering `RLIMIT_NOFILE` would mean adding `nix`'s `resource` feature to a dependency whose feature set this repository enumerates and justifies. What the end-to-end test does pin is the property the exhaustion was a symptom of: `a_wide_tree_is_walked_without_holding_the_whole_frontier_open` samples this process's own descriptors from inside the walk (through the classifier the walk calls per file) and fails if the walk holds the frontier — it read **405 descriptors against a 4 baseline over 400 sibling directories** before the depth-first rewrite. That sampling is Linux-only (`/proc/self/fd`); the property it pins is not.
- **R1-010's race has no test.** Deciding an entry's type by path where the *directory at that path* is no longer the one the walk descended into needs a second process swapping a directory between the walk's `read_dir` and its stat, which cannot be arranged deterministically from inside a synchronous test. The fix — take the type relative to the held handle — is applied and is what D3 now describes; it is recorded here as implemented without a red of its own.
- **R1-013's race does have one, because the state the two halves disagree about can be staged without racing anything.** The same class of finding as R1-010, and the reason it was testable where that one was not: the swap does not have to happen *during* the operation, only *before* it. `the_listing_comes_from_the_held_handle_and_not_from_the_path` (a unit test in `fs_wrong_root_scanner`) opens the directory handle, renames that directory aside, puts an empty one at the same path, and only then lists — which is exactly the window's end state — and the red it drove was `left: []` against `right: ["report.pdf"]`. The same trick pins R1-016 at `crates/omnifrons-adapters/tests/wrong_root_fs.rs` (`an_entry_already_at_the_name_is_refused_from_its_own_handle_and_never_read`): a hard link at the entry's name is a regular file to `symlink_metadata` and a second-named file to a no-follow open, so the two halves of the old check-then-open disagree about it standing still, and the test also counts the hasher's reads to pin that the refusal costs none. **Where this trick does not reach** is a window whose two states are the *same* file — R1-010's — which is why that one still has no red.

**The inconsistency the flake exposed, named rather than fixed.** `TokioProcessSupervisor::observe` (`crates/omnifrons-supervisor/src/lib.rs`) has a `try_wait` `Err(_)` arm that reports `ProcessStatus::Terminal(ProcessTerminalState::OrphanRiskUncertain)` while deliberately leaving the entry `Tracked::Running` and recording no terminal order — so what it *reports* and what the table *holds* disagree, and the very next `observe` answering `Ok(None)` reports `Running` again. Both halves are defensible on their own: the report is honest (descendants are unproven stopped), and not evicting is honest too (the pid cannot have been recycled, so the entry must stay). What is not defensible is a caller reading that report as an arrival, which is exactly what the helper did. **`observe`'s reporting semantics are left unchanged in this slice** — changing them is a contract decision about `ProcessSupervisor`, not a test fix — and the distinction is pinned instead (`an_unproven_orphan_risk_report_is_not_a_confirmed_terminal_state`). Debt: either `observe` should report `Running` with the uncertainty carried separately, or `ProcessStatus::Terminal` should stop being reachable without a recorded terminal order; a later slice decides which.

**Owner-visible threat-model item: the scan is now a security control, and both of its inputs come from inside the worktree it polices — this slice does not close that.** The verdict for every file rests on two things the product reads out of the project itself: the classification rows and the declared outbox, both from `.omnifrons/asset-policy.json`, a file inside the worktree and writable by exactly the same-user producer the scan exists to catch. A policy declaring `"outbox": "docs"` is accepted — it is a valid project-relative path — and from that moment `docs/report.pdf` is excluded from the verdict by the product's own rule (HAP-001-R44), silently and by design. The classifier rows are the same kind of input: a row that calls PDFs `git-tracked` takes every PDF in the worktree out of `misplaced` at once. Nothing in this slice signs, pins, or externalizes that file, and nothing warns when a declaration widens what the scan will not look at. Every other control here — the no-follow walk, the handle-anchored move, the link-count refusal, the digest binding — assumes an adversary at the file system; this one input assumes a cooperative one, and the two assumptions cannot both be right. **Named as an owner decision, not fixed here**: the plausible closures (the policy in the work area rather than the worktree; a signed or owner-approved policy; an exclusion diff surfaced for confirmation whenever the declaration changes) are all contract changes about where a project's policy lives and who may change it, which is HAP-001's question and not a spike's.

Six further residuals this half names and does not close, each reachable only by a same-user process and each an *existing* pattern rather than something this slice introduced:

- **On Windows the walk itself is path-based, and a reparse point at an ancestor redirects it out of the project** (R1-014). D3 carries the full statement; it is repeated here because it is the largest of these and because it is the one the module comments used to claim otherwise about — the sentence "so a link placed anywhere in the tree can never redirect the walk out of the project" was written for both platforms and was true of one. `std` on that platform offers no directory open, stat or listing relative to a held handle, so `FsWrongRootScanner`'s descent, its `entry_kind`, its listing, `open_misplaced`'s per-component walk and `fs_candidate_prober::open_entry` all re-resolve the joined path, and `FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` protects the final component alone. A finding can therefore name a file outside the project, and the publish remedy — resolving that name the same way — would copy it into the outbox as this project's own unattributed output. Quarantine's deletion is not reachable there (`Unverifiable` keeps the original), so the exposure is copy and disclosure, not loss. Closing it needs a relative open `std` does not have; the alternative is a Windows API dependency and hand-declared `NtCreateFile` bindings, which is a dependency-table change and `unsafe` besides. **Nothing in this repository can execute Windows**, which is why this is narrowed in the module comments, in `docs/repository-layout.md` and here rather than asserted away.
- **The copy-in's directory components are not re-resolved by handle.** Between canonicalizing the outbox and creating the entry, the path's directories are trusted; the precedent is `publication::write_recovery_entry`. The same is true of the quarantine copy path's own writes inside the quarantine directory (D8, R1-017): `copy_into` is handed `dest_path` and not the destination handle `quarantine()` already holds, so the staging create, the exclusive create of the final name and the cleanup are by path even on unix, where `openat`/`linkat`/`unlinkat` exist — anchoring them is three more `QuarantineStore` methods, a port change rather than a fix. The earlier note here said this without saying what it costs on Windows, which is more: `std` there offers no relative directory open at all, so *neither* the outbox's components nor the quarantine's are handle-anchored on that platform, and the check-then-create window is real.
- **The publish remedy's idempotent reuse still digests whatever regular file holds the entry's name** (R1-016's remainder). With the check-then-open gone (D9) the file it digests is no longer one a link can point anywhere: it is the file that opens at that exact name relative to the outbox handle, and a link, a directory or a second-named file is refused before a byte is read. What remains is that a same-user producer who plants a *large regular file* at the predictable entry name inside the outbox makes the remedy read it once before refusing it. The outbox is the product's own directory and the read is bounded by that one file; the same cost is already paid by the whole-outbox inventory and by the post-write probe, so it is named rather than separately closed.
- **The ignore ledger's own file is opened by path and follows a link at its name.** `JsonlIgnoreLedger::open` and `record` open `<work area>/ignore/decisions.jsonl` with plain `OpenOptions`, so a link planted at that name is followed. The work area is owner-only and outside every workspace, which is the whole reason this is a residual and not a finding — but it is not the no-follow discipline the outbox and the worktree get, and it is written down rather than assumed away.
- **`any_running()` is check-then-act, and the surface lock is held across a whole hash-and-copy.** A remedy takes the publication surface lock, asks whether any supervised process is running, and then hashes and copies with that answer standing; a process launched in the window between the two is not seen. The same shape the approval and publication surfaces already have, and the same fix — a launch that cannot start while the surface lock is held — is a supervisor contract change, not a remedy one. The cost of the second half is throughput, not correctness: a large file's copy blocks the approval surface for its duration.
- **`Unverifiable` counts as "proceed" in both pre-move checks**, so HAP-001-R18's path-change check is a no-op on Windows. `FsOutboxEntryOps::identity` reports `Unverifiable` there for every entry, and both the quarantine remedy and the copy-in treat it like `SameFile` rather than refusing. Refusing instead would make both remedies unavailable on Windows outright, which is why it stands; but "a path swap during the remedy is refused" is a unix sentence, and the Windows counterpart is this residual, not a pass.

Debt this half names without deciding: a heavy file deliberately committed to Git is flagged `misplaced` (D1); the vault and other registered projects are unimplemented arms of HAP-001-R32 (D3); the size-threshold arm of D16's default is folded into the policy's rows (D2); the handoff preflight — D16's second detector, and the third source HAP-001 names, a publish request naming a path outside the outbox — has no seam here; a file watcher is not implemented and D16 says it never should be the only detector; a truncated scan reports `truncated: true` and offers no continuation; the ignore ledger grows without bound and has no listing or revocation command; a quarantined file has no listing command and no reveal affordance, so RCS-001-R11's reveal-only rule is honored by omission rather than by an implementation; the scan re-digests every heavy file on every run end, so a project with many large artifacts pays for them repeatedly; **the `.part-` the publish remedy writes inside the outbox root is listed by the whole-outbox inventory as an ordinary unattributed candidate for as long as it exists** — which is not only after a crash (R1-019): it exists for the *whole duration of the copy*, and `candidates_list { runId: null }` takes no publication surface lock, so a listing that runs concurrently shows a partial entry carrying the digest of the bytes written so far. Approval stays fail-closed — an approval binds a name and a digest, and by the time a publish ran the `.part-` would be gone or its bytes different, so the transaction refuses — but the *listing* is wrong while it lasts, and after a crash the entry outlives the remedy entirely. The remedy removes it on every path it controls; nothing sweeps it, and slice 5d's scope explicitly leaves the stale-`.part` sweep out, so it is disclosed rather than fixed; a `.git` directory is excluded only when it holds `HEAD` and an `objects` directory, which is a heuristic and not the index — a repository whose metadata layout differs from that would be scanned, and a producer that fabricates both files gets its exclusion back; and Windows keeps four disclosed residuals here — no owner-only ACL on the quarantine directory, no by-handle identity comparison, no handle-anchored move (which together mean the quarantine remedy copies and keeps the original there), and no by-handle link count, so HAP-001-R20's `outbox-linked` refusal cannot be made on that platform for a misplaced file, for an outbox entry, or (since R1-022) at the quarantine destination.

Two more, recorded and not acted on, both from the third review pass:

- **An upstream descriptor leak in `nix::dir::Dir::from_fd`** (R1-026). `Dir::from_fd` takes the `OwnedFd` the listing hands it and calls `fdopendir`; when that call *fails* the crate returns the errno and drops nothing — the descriptor it was given is neither closed by `closedir` (there is no `DIR*`) nor still owned by the caller (it was moved in). The walk duplicates its own handle per listing (`try_clone`, R1-013), so a directory whose `fdopendir` failed leaks one descriptor per attempt. It is near-unreachable — `fdopendir` on a freshly duplicated, valid directory descriptor fails essentially only under `ENOMEM` — and the fix is upstream, in a pinned vendored crate this slice does not patch. Recorded so the shape is visible rather than assumed absent.
- **The instantaneous handle peak is depth + 3, not depth + 1** (R1-027). The bound this half states everywhere — the module comments, D3, D4 — counts the frame handles on the path the walk is on, `MAX_SCAN_DEPTH + 1` of them. At the instants the walk lists a directory it also holds the `try_clone` duplicate that `fdopendir` consumes, and at the instants it examines a file it holds that file's own handle, so the true peak is two higher. What matters for HAP-001 D22's shared 32-handle cap is unchanged and is why this is debt rather than a finding: the peak is still bounded by the *depth* of the tree and never by its width, which is the property a breadth-first walk loses. The number in the prose is nonetheless two short, and a later slice should either state depth + 3 or stop holding the duplicate across the whole listing.

### Renderer

The renderer half of this slice landed after the Rust half above, against exactly the wire shapes in [IPC shapes](#ipc-shapes-5), each one verified against `src-tauri/src/ipc/{wrong_root.rs, dto.rs, commands.rs}` before any of it was typed. `renderer/src/AgentPanel.tsx` carries all of the surface itself; the review pass below added one further component, `renderer/src/PanelErrorBoundary.tsx`, and nothing outside `renderer/` moved.

**Wrappers and types.** `harness.ts` gains the four wrong-root commands as typed wrappers, each with `# Errors` JSDoc naming every code it can reject with: `wrongRootStatus()`, `wrongRootScan()`, `misplacedList()`, `misplacedRemedy(name, sha256, remedy)` — the last invoking `misplaced_remedy` with exactly `{ name, sha256, remedy }` and the other three with no arguments at all. The types mirror `dto.rs` field-for-field: `OutputDiscipline` (`enforced` | `advisory`), `WrongRootStatus` (five fields), `ScanSummary` (six), `MisplacedRow` (eight), `Remedy` (three tokens), `RemedyOutcome` (three), `RemedyDetail` (**nine**, `renamed-unverified` included — see the review pass below), `WrongRootReason` (one). The closed `ShellErrorCode` union gains `misplaced-unknown`, `quarantine-unavailable` and `scan-failed`, and both compile-time exhaustiveness guards were kept in step by the type-level red `tsc -b` produced: `src/ipc/harness.test.ts(1476,17): error TS2322: Type '"misplaced-unknown" | "quarantine-unavailable" | "scan-failed"' is not assignable to type 'never'` for the code switch (38 → 41 codes) and `src/ipc/harness.test.ts(685,17): error TS2322: Type '"misplaced" is not assignable to type 'never'` for the `AgentEvent` kind switch (ten → eleven kinds), the latter after `src/ipc/harness.test.ts(2404,9): error TS2322: Type '"misplaced"' is not assignable to type '"candidates"'` gave the `AgentEvent` union its own red. A new `slice 5d closed sets` guard covers the five new unions the same way. `harness.test.ts` grows 67 → 82.

**Three differences from the renderer brief, each resolved in the Rust code's favour and reported.** (1) The brief's `misplaced_remedy` sketch shows `sha256` and `originalKept` always present; `MisplacedRemedyDto` types both as `Option`, and its own shape test constructs an `ignored` payload with `name`, `sha256`, `originalKept` and `detail` all `None`. The command bodies do populate `sha256` and `originalKept: Some(true)` on every one of the three paths, so the brief's *values* are right for the shipped code — but the *type* admits `null`, so `MisplacedRemedy` types all four of `name`, `sha256`, `originalKept` and `detail` as nullable and the surface states the absence (`whether the original was kept was not reported`) rather than assuming either. (2) The brief lists `refused`, `outbox-unavailable`, `work-area-invalid` and `run-active` as the existing codes also reachable here; `wrong_root.rs` reaches three more — `outbox-invalid` from `policy_store.load` in both the scan and the publish remedy, `invalid-request` from `Sha256Digest::from_hex` and from `OpenMisplacedError::InvalidName`, and `workspace-unavailable` from `active_workspace` on both `wrongroot_scan` and `misplaced_remedy`. All seven are in the wrappers' `# Errors` JSDoc and the banner test covers the reachable set. (3) `MisplacedDto::from_finding` fills `remedies` from `Remedy::ALL`, so every row today carries all three; the surface still reads the row's own array and offers exactly what it carries, which a test pins with rows offering one remedy and none.

**The Wrong roots section.** A `<section aria-label="Wrong roots">`, a sibling of the transcript, the Candidates region, the Guidance region and the Publications region and never inside any of them (RCS-001-R6), rendered whether or not a workspace is active — `wrongroot_status` and `misplaced_list` answer with no project, unlike the four project-scoped fetches, because the report is the adapter catalog's and the findings are shell state. It opens with a `<p role="status" aria-label="Output discipline">` reading `output discipline: advisory (scope advisory) — a write outside the project root is not prevented; not scanned yet` or, for the `enforced` report, `output discipline: enforced (scope sandbox-enforced) — the adapter declares that a write outside the project root is prevented; the product does not verify that claim; scanned, findings 2` — the discipline token verbatim, its explanation fixed copy per closed token through an exhaustive switch, the scope mode through the existing `formatScopeMode`, and the scan state as `not scanned yet` or `scanned, findings <n>`. When no report has been read the line is replaced by a `<p role="status" aria-label="Output discipline unavailable">` saying exactly that, never by a blank space. **Every disclosure the report carries is rendered, one line each, verbatim**, in a `<ul aria-label="Output discipline disclosures">`: they are the contract's own honesty about what is *not* prevented (HAP-001-R33, R34), so none is collapsed, truncated or hidden behind a toggle — a test renders a third, unknown disclosure and asserts all three stand, with no `<details>` and a `<b>` inside one left literal. **That list is rendered outside the status check and is never empty**, which is the R1-010 fix below: with no report, or with a report carrying no disclosures, the renderer's own advisory pair stands in.

**Scan** is a `Scan` button invoking `wrongroot_scan` with exactly `{}`, whose answer renders in a `<p role="status" aria-label="Wrong roots scan">` as `scan: 12 scanned, 2 findings, 1 ignored, 3 excluded, 0 unreadable` — every count always present in that order, a zero rendered as `0`, like the `candidates` line's eight — and, for `truncated: true`, the same line followed by ` — the scan stopped early; the list is incomplete`. **The scan is the one thing here that stays live while a run is active**, because the shell's own `wrongroot_scan` takes no lock, holds no handle and is exactly the body a run-end scan runs; a test clicks it during a run and asserts the call actually happens, rather than asserting an attribute. It is one at a time, by a ref set synchronously as well as by the button's own `disabled`. On success the section refetches `misplaced_list` and `wrongroot_status` rather than trusting the counts the response carried.

**The Misplaced table** (`<table aria-label="Misplaced files">`, no table at all while nothing is standing) has the columns `name`, `class`, `type`, `size`, `sha256 short`, `reason`, `remedies`, rows in walk order, every data cell through `PlainTextLine`. The reason is fixed copy per closed token (`in-project-outside-outbox` → `inside the project, outside the outbox`), never the raw token. **The name is display-only**: it is never resolved as a path, never used to build a link, and never sent anywhere but back to `misplaced_remedy` as the finding's own identity (RCS-001-R14) — a name carrying `<b>`, a C0 byte and a U+202E override renders as the text it is, with no `<b>`, no anchor, no `[href]` and no control or override in the region's `textContent`. The full 64-hex digest is not in the table at all; it is in the quarantine block, where the gate needs it in view. A name whose displayed form is not its whole form — anything the strip changed — carries the fixed mark `(this name contains hidden characters; compare by digest)` beside it, so two findings differing only by an invisible character are not left looking identical next to a delete button (R1-012 below).

**Remedies, and how HAP-001-R32's "never moved without the remedy choice" is met.** Nothing on this surface moves, copies or records anything until a remedy button is clicked, and the only call a click can make is `misplaced_remedy` with the row's own name and full digest. A row renders exactly the remedies its own `remedies` array carries, in its order, never a set the component holds — **intersected with the closed set this renderer knows**, so a token it cannot describe renders no button at all rather than a button whose behaviour would have to be guessed (R1-003 below). **Quarantine is the one remedy that removes the original, so it is the one that is gated**, and which remedies are destructive is read from an explicit list rather than inferred from what a token is *not*: its button opens a `<section aria-label="Quarantine confirmation">` — inside the Wrong roots region, beside the table, outside the transcript — and invokes nothing. The block states the row's identity facts one per line (`name:`, `class:`, `type:`, `size:`, `sha256:` in full, `sha256 short:`, `reason:`), then the fixed sentence `the file will be MOVED out of the project into the quarantine directory, outside any workspace; this product then offers no way to list, open or restore it` (RCS-001-R10 — neither the button name nor the `quarantine` token says where the file goes or what happens to it there; and see R1-006 below for why this line no longer promises reveal-only), then the fixed `act as: device-local-user`, then a gate labelled `Type the short digest (<sha256Short>) to quarantine`, an input that opens empty, and a disabled `Quarantine file`. The gate is an exact, case-sensitive match and nothing else: the fixed word `quarantine`, uppercase hex, a seven- or nine-character prefix, a leading or trailing space (the shape a paste produces), the empty string, the full 64-hex digest and another row's short digest all leave it disabled — as do a row whose `sha256Short` is empty and a row whose `sha256Short` is not a prefix of its `sha256` (R3-018, R1-015 below). The block is bound to the row's **name and full digest**, the pair `misplaced_remedy` itself binds to, so a refetch reporting that name at other bytes closes it rather than swapping the facts under a decision in progress (R3-028). The click then sends the row's own `sha256`, **never the typed value**. **Publish and Ignore do not remove the file** — publish leaves it exactly where it was found (HAP-001-R28) and ignore touches no file at all — so they act on a single click, and this paragraph is where that asymmetry is stated rather than letting the surface look inconsistent by accident. Ignore is nonetheless permanent, and its receipt says so (R1-013 below): it is not destructive to the file, but it is destructive to the detection, and the product has no listing or revocation for what it has suppressed.

**Outcomes.** One `<p role="status" aria-label="Remedy result">`, built from fixed copy per closed token: the remedy verbatim, then the outcome (`moved into quarantine, outside any workspace` / `copied into the outbox as a new unattributed entry` / `the decision was recorded, and the file is not offered again until its content changes`), then `name <name>` when the remedy produced something, then what `originalKept` actually says (`the original is gone from the project` / `this remedy did not remove the original` / `whether the original was kept was not reported`), then — for `copied-to-outbox` only — the fixed `the copy is not published: approve it in the outbox listing like any other entry`, for `ignored` only the fixed `this decision cannot be undone or reviewed from this product: nothing here lists what has been ignored`, and finally the `detail` token's own fixed copy. A completed quarantine therefore reads `quarantine: moved into quarantine, outside any workspace; name abababab-report.pdf; the original is gone from the project; moved by rename`, and each of HAP-001-R19's four kept-original details reads `this remedy did not remove the original` beside its own explanation — a quarantine that could not remove the original never reads as one that did. **The `true` arm deliberately claims no more than the flag carries** (R1-004 below): the shell sets `originalKept: true` for `original-already-gone`, `original-changed-during-the-move` and `identity-check-unavailable` too, so the old copy `the original is still in the project` contradicted the detail sentence on its own line in three of the four cases. After any remedy the block closes and both fetches run again.

**The run-end frame.** A `misplaced` event renders as one transcript line carrying **the same fixed copy the section's scan summary uses** — one shape, one wording — and triggers the same two refetches, so the table beside the transcript is not left showing the previous scan's rows while the line reports new counts. The fixed diagnostic a scan that could not run emits renders through the existing `diagnostic:` line, never as a `misplaced` line claiming nothing was found; a test asserts both.

**Banners.** The three new codes render through the unchanged banner as `<code>: <message>` — the shell's fixed catalogue messages through `PlainTextLine` — with no "untrusted" prefix and no detail rendered, and so do the existing codes reachable here; a `detail` sent alongside is not rendered. **`misplaced_remedy`'s reachable set is ten, and is now enumerated once and counted**: `misplaced-unknown`, `quarantine-unavailable`, `refused`, `outbox-unavailable`, `outbox-linked`, `outbox-invalid`, `work-area-invalid`, `invalid-request`, `run-active`, `workspace-unavailable` (R1-014 below; `scan-failed` is `wrongroot_scan`'s and has its own test). `DENIAL_CODES` stays the four `LaunchGate` denials: none of `misplaced-unknown`, `quarantine-unavailable` and `scan-failed` is a verdict on a producer. A rejection keeps the table and the block as they were, so the user can read the banner and retry — a claim that was untested until R3-027 below, since every banner case clicked Ignore from the table with no block open — except `misplaced-unknown`, where the block's facts name a finding the shell no longer reports: the block closes and the section is fetched afresh, the `guidance-file-changed` precedent. A later successful act clears the banner. A non-`ShellError` renders `unexpected error` and nothing of its message.

**Guards.** Every remedy, the open block's input and its final button are frozen while a run is active — the shell refuses each remedy with `run-active` in the same window — while Scan stays live; a click on a frozen remedy invokes nothing, belt and braces with the buttons' own `disabled`, and the typed gate survives the run. The run-active refusal is read from a **ref set synchronously in `handleStart`**, not from the render value: `disabled` and the handler's closure were one render behind a run that had just started, so a remedy click batched into the same tick as Start passed both (R3-024 below). One remedy at a time and one scan at a time, each by a ref set synchronously as well as by the buttons' `disabled`; **the workspace pick is held the same way while a remedy is in flight** (R1-008 below). All four commands are sequenced by request id and carry the `mountedRef` unmount guard. The unmount test settles each in-flight promise both ways and asserts no throw and no `console.error`; its **no IPC call after the unmount** assertion has teeth only for `wrongroot_scan` and `misplaced_remedy`, which refetch on success — `wrongroot_status` and `misplaced_list` issue no follow-up call, and a `setState` after unmount is a silent no-op in React 19, so there is nothing observable to assert for those two and the test says so rather than implying coverage it does not have (R3-022). Their sequencing, which *is* observable, is covered by two out-of-order tests that resolve an overtaken fetch last. A `misplaced_list` payload that is not the list its DTO promises renders as no table rather than throwing mid-render, the fail-safe-to-nothing discipline `handleFrame`, `fetchGuidanceSnapshots` and `handleListOutbox` already apply — and **each row is now checked as well as the list** (R1-001 below).

**The workspace generation, which is the R1-001 finding of slice 5c not repeated.** A `wrongRootContextRef` — the same shape as `guidanceContextRef` and `spawnGenerationRef` — is bumped by a workspace pick, which also clears the report, the findings, the scan line, the receipt, the open block and its typed gate before both fetches run again. A `wrongroot_scan`, a `wrongroot_status` or a `misplaced_list` still in flight when the user picks another project is then dropped whole, on both the resolve and the reject paths — `misplaced_remedy` can no longer be in flight across a pick at all, because the pick is held for its duration (R1-008 below), and its own generation guard therefore stands as belt and braces rather than as the reachable path it was. `wrongroot_status` had not been covered in either direction and now is, which matters because it is the fetch that carries `scanned, findings n`: a stale answer states the *previous* project's finding count on the new project's own status line (R3-023). The original five tests drove the reachable set at the time, and all five were red before the guard landed (`expected 'scan: 12 scanned, 2 findings, 1 ignor…' to be null` twice, `expected 'ignore: the decision was recorded, an…' to be null`, `expected <div role="alert" …(1)>…(2)</div> to be null`, and `expected <table …(1)>…(2)</table> to be null`). Without it a remedy's receipt — which names a file — would post under the project the user moved to and read as a move inside *that* project, and the shell's own name-and-digest binding cannot tell two projects apart whose `docs/report.pdf` came byte-identical from one template.

**Content security.** Every fact on this surface goes through `PlainTextLine`, disclosures included — and the disclosure test now uses the ESC and U+202E fixture the row-name test uses, since its previous markup-only assertion could not tell `PlainTextLine` from React's own escaping (R3-029). `stripControlCharacters` also strips **U+2028 and U+2029** as of this pass (R1-011). Nothing pre-fills the quarantine gate: the digest the label itself names, a finding whose own project-relative name *is* that short digest, and a programmatic `input.value` with no input event all leave the input empty and the button disabled; only the user's own typing enables it, and no `misplaced_remedy` is sent meanwhile. TM-001-R7 is met the way the 5b approval and the 5c guidance write meet it — the identity-bound facts and the act-as identity on the surface before the decision, no harness-authored summary anywhere, and a confirmation that cannot be completed without having read the digest — and the request then carries the shell's own digest, which the shell re-verifies against its last scan and refuses with `misplaced-unknown` if it moved.

**The four carried findings of slice 5c's renderer ledger, all closed here.** *R3-011*: `fetchGuidanceSnapshots`' `Array.isArray` fail-safe had only incidental coverage through a helper that answers `null`; it now has an `it.each` naming it, over `null`, `undefined`, an object and a string, asserting no table, the rest of the section standing and no `console.error` — verified to bite by removing the guard (`TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'length')` and `TypeError: snapshots.map is not a function`). *R3-014*: the synchronous busy refs were unexercised because `fireEvent.click` is `act`-wrapped, so React flushes between clicks and the second one lands on an already-disabled button. A `doubleClickWithoutRerender` helper dispatches both clicks inside one `act`, keeping the render batched so the second click reaches the handler with the button still enabled: what refuses it then is the ref alone. Four tests use it — the guidance pin, the guidance write, a wrong-root remedy and the scan — and all four go red (`expected 2 to be 1`) when the refs are swapped for their state flags. *R3-015*: "Pin stays live while a run is active" was an attribute assertion; a new test clicks Pin during a run and asserts `guidance_pin` is called with exactly `{ id, pinned }`, the row takes the store's answer and the kind's status is refetched (red `expected +0 to be 1` when `runActive` is added to the pin guard). *R3-017*: the two `data-testid` hooks were the suite's only non-semantic queries; the proposed block is now `<div aria-label="proposed block">` with one child per rendered line, both test ids are gone, and the queries assert the same things through the accessible name (red `Unable to find a label with the text of: proposed block` on eight tests before the label landed).

**Totals and evidence at the original landing** (the review pass below restates them). `AgentPanel.test.tsx` grows by fifty-two (270 → 322: forty-three for the Wrong roots section, its scan, its table, its remedies, its outcomes, its guards, its banners and its content security, and nine for the four carried findings) and `harness.test.ts` by fifteen (67 → 82); `app.test.tsx` keeps its five, its mount mock now answering `wrongroot_status` and `misplaced_list` — **not** with a `workspace-unavailable` rejection like the four project-scoped fetches, since neither needs a project, but with the advisory report and an empty list, which is the red that mock closed (`Unable to find an accessible element with the role "status" and name "Output discipline"`); every default mock in `AgentPanel.test.tsx` answers both for the same reason. `pnpm -r test` **425 → 492** (`AgentPanel.test.tsx` 322, `harness.test.ts` 82, `app.test.tsx` 5, `ApprovalSurface.test.tsx` 23, `HarnessPanel.test.tsx` 49, `PlainTextLine.test.tsx` 11), green on one plain run and on three consecutive `CI=true` runs at 492/492; `pnpm --filter renderer exec vitest run src/AgentPanel.test.tsx` ten consecutive runs at 322/322; `pnpm -r lint` and `pnpm -r build` (`tsc -b && vite build`) clean; no `.only`; provenance sweep empty.

**The TDD record, stated rather than dressed up.** Nine behaviors were driven from a quoted red the ordinary way: every `harness.ts` wrapper (`ReferenceError: wrongRootStatus is not defined` and its three siblings), both compile-time exhaustiveness guards, the section's opening (`AssertionError: expected null not to be null` for the output-discipline line, `expected [] to have a length of 3` for the disclosures), the run-end `misplaced` transcript line (`expected [ 'you: do the thing', '' ] to deeply equal [ 'you: do the thing', …(1) ]`), the five workspace-generation drops quoted above, the `app.test.tsx` mount mock, and all four carried findings. **The rest of the section — the scan handler and its button, the Misplaced table, the remedy buttons, the quarantine block and the remedy receipt — was written together with the section's scaffolding to satisfy that first red, so their tests were green on arrival.** Rather than record them as unverified pins, each was **verified to bite** by removing the implementation and quoting the failure the tests then produced (`Unable to find an accessible element with the role "button" and name "Scan"`; `Unable to find role="table" and name "Misplaced files"`; `Unable to find a label with the text of: Quarantine confirmation`; `expected null not to be null` for the receipt; `expected { quarantine: false, …(5) } to deeply equal { quarantine: true, …(5) }` and `expected 1 to be +0` for the run freeze; `expected <div …(1)>…(12)</div> to be null` for the stale-block close; `expected 14 to be 12` for the unmount guards; `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'length')` for the `misplaced_list` fail-safe) — the technique slice 5c used for the `mountedRef` guards, applied here to a whole surface. That is weaker than a red observed before the code existed, and is recorded as such. The banner tests are ordinary pins: the banner is code-agnostic, as slices 4 and 5 already found.

**Deviations from the renderer brief, each with its reason.** *The four wrong-root fetches swallow their rejections* — the brief has the codes reach the banner, which they do for Scan and for every remedy, but the mount-time, pick-time and post-remedy fetches clear their line rather than raising a banner the user cannot act on, the outbox line's and the guidance section's own precedent. *A run-end `misplaced` frame also refetches the list and the status*, which the brief did not ask for: without it the transcript would report new counts beside a table still showing the previous scan's rows, which reads as a surface disagreeing with itself. *The scan stays live during a run* while every remedy freezes, because the Rust half's D4 says the scan takes no lock and every remedy takes the publication surface lock — the brief's item 6 freezes remedies and says nothing of the scan, and freezing it would be stricter than the contract, the same reasoning slice 5c applied to Pin. *`class`, `detectedType` and `reason` are typed from their closed sets* (`ArtifactClass`, `string`, `WrongRootReason`) rather than as bare strings, since `dto.rs` fills each from a domain enum's `as_str`. *The block has no Cancel* — another row's Quarantine, a completed remedy, a `misplaced-unknown` refusal or a workspace pick replaces it, the 5b approval block's precedent.

**Guesses this half made, flagged as such.** The exact wording of every fixed line here is the renderer's own: HAP-001 fixes the disclosures and the remedy set, not the copy around them, so `output discipline: … — a write outside the project root is not prevented`, `the scan stopped early; the list is incomplete`, the quarantine sentence, the outcome and detail lines and `the copy is not published: approve it in the outbox listing like any other entry` are this implementer's phrasing of facts the contract states, and are the first thing a reviewer should disagree with. The column set and their order are likewise a choice, not a contract.

**Debt this half names without deciding.** A truncated scan says so and offers no continuation, because the wire offers none. There is no listing of ignored decisions and no way to revoke one from this surface, and none on the wire either. A quarantined file has no listing and no reveal affordance here, so RCS-001-R11's reveal-only rule is honored by omission — the same debt the Rust half names. The Misplaced table is unpaginated and a scan may report up to `MAX_SCANNED_ENTRIES` findings, so a project with many misplaced files renders a very long table. The receipt survives until the next remedy or the next workspace pick and names only the remedy and what it produced, not the finding it acted on, since the row is gone from the list by then. And the section refetches on mount even with no workspace active, which is one extra pair of IPC calls in that state — harmless, since both answer without a project, but it is a call the other sections do not make. Everything here stays **dev-mode-only** exactly as the Rust half states: every report, scan, finding and remedy above came from a mocked `invoke` — no packaged build, no live shell exercised by any renderer test, and the shell's own re-verification of a finding's digest has run against no renderer request here — and no producer signing (the producer line's "(unknown)" is unchanged).

#### Review pass: what two lenses found, and how it was answered

Both the risk and the reliability lens blocked the renderer half above. The reliability lens found its part by **mutation testing** — it copied the renderer out of the repository, made one behaviour-level change at a time, and re-ran the suite — and fourteen mutations survived all 492 tests. That method is the one this pass adopted, and it is the reason the paragraphs above have changed: a test that passes is not evidence, and "delete the feature and watch a test go red" is too coarse to be evidence either, because any `getByRole` query catches a deleted feature. **Every test written or repaired here was verified by mutating the specific behaviour it claims to cover** — flipping the boolean, dropping the one guard line, changing the one branch — in a copy outside the repository, and confirming the suite goes red. The mutation table is the evidence; where a mutation still survives, it is named below rather than left in the count.

**The blocking finding, and why it was blocking.** This is the one surface in the product that deletes a file inside the user's project, and it could be made to disappear. Three formatters (`formatWrongRootReason`, `formatRemedyOutcome`, `formatRemedyDetail`) were exhaustive switches over closed TypeScript unions with **no runtime default**, while the wire types every one of those fields as a plain string. An unrecognized token returned `undefined`, which reached `PlainTextLine` and threw `Array.from(undefined)`; a `misplaced_list` answering `[{}]` threw `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'map')` in the remedy cell. There was no error boundary in `renderer/src` and the three panels are bare siblings under one root, so either throw unmounted **the whole renderer** — the approval surface, the product's consent gate, and every freeze guard with it. Four things answer it. Every formatter over a wire-typed union now has a runtime default that renders the raw token as text through `PlainTextLine`, so an unrecognized value is visible and inert rather than fatal or blank (`unrecognizedWireToken`). `isMisplacedRow` checks each row field by field before it reaches render, the way the top-level `Array.isArray` guard already checked the list; a row that fails is dropped and the drop is announced, so a shortened table never reads as a shorter list of findings. `renderRemedies` offers a button only for a remedy in a known set. And `renderer/src/PanelErrorBoundary.tsx` — the one new component — wraps each of the three panels in `App.tsx` **individually**, so a render throw costs that panel and not the page; a shared boundary would keep the page alive and still lose all three surfaces at once. It is deliberately minimal: it names the panel that closed, says the others still stand, offers no retry (a panel whose render just threw has state the boundary knows nothing about), and puts no part of the error object in the DOM. It is a second line, not the first — the reachable failures are handled where they happen, with copy that says something useful. Formatters from earlier slices (`formatCandidateState`, `formatArtifactState`, `formatProviderState` and the rest) were **not** individually retrofitted with defaults and are contained by the boundary instead. **"Contained by the boundary" is a smaller claim than it sounds, and the second review pass below states its real size** (R3-043): the boundary's unit is the whole panel, and every one of those formatters lives in the *Agent* panel, so an unrecognised `candidates_list` state token — a slice 5b field, nothing to do with this slice — closes the Agent panel entire, taking the wrong-roots table, the three remedies, the freeze guards and Stop down with it. What the boundary buys is that the approval surface and the harness panel survive, which is exactly the coupling it was added to break, and no more than that. The blocking rationale — that a surface which can delete a file must not disappear because of a token it did not recognise — therefore holds *within this slice's own fields*, where the four wrong-root formatters now have runtime defaults; it is not met for tokens on the earlier slices' fields, which still close this surface by closing its panel. That is a named residual, not an oversight, and the second review pass below counts what is still exposed rather than leaving it as "and the rest".

**The fail-open discriminator (R1-003, R3-031).** `remedy === 'quarantine' ? openQuarantine(...) : runRemedy(...)` treated everything that was not literally that string as non-destructive and one-click — an unknown remedy assumed harmless purely because it was unknown, which is the wrong direction for this surface. The renderer now holds an explicit `KNOWN_REMEDIES` allow-list and an explicit `DESTRUCTIVE_REMEDIES` set; an unrecognized token renders no button, and the gate is required by membership of the destructive set rather than by exclusion, so a second removing remedy would arrive gated. The `row.remedies.includes` guard — dropping which had left the suite green — is now covered too.

**Copy that asserted more than the code delivers (R1-004, R1-005, R1-006).** Three lines were saying things this product does not do. `originalKept: true` rendered as `the original is still in the project`, but the shell sets it for `original-already-gone`, `original-changed-during-the-move` and `identity-check-unavailable` as well, so the receipt contradicted its own detail sentence in three of four cases — and a test pinned the contradiction as correct. It now reads `this remedy did not remove the original`, which is exactly what the flag records. The `enforced` discipline line claimed `a write outside the project root is prevented by the sandbox`, which HAP-001 records as unproven on every platform, which nothing in the product verifies, and which contradicted this panel's own `advisory scope — not a sandbox` badge two regions above; it now reports the adapter's declaration and says the product does not check it. The quarantine sentence promised the file was left `unexecuted and reveal-only`, while the Rust half's own debt list records reveal-only as honored by omission — no reveal affordance, no listing command, so nothing sets a flag and nothing enforces one. "Unexecuted" fails for a second reason worth recording, because it was found by reading the Rust rather than the brief: **the two quarantine paths do not agree with each other about the file's mode**. `quarantine.rs` creates the copy path's destination `0o600` on unix, while the rename path moves the file with whatever mode it already carried, and Windows has neither — so no single sentence about the quarantined file's own permissions is true of a quarantine in general. The line now says nothing about them rather than pick the flattering half, and states instead the negative that holds on every path: the product offers no way back to the file, which is also the more useful warning.

**Silence where a fact belonged (R1-009, R1-010).** A `misplaced_list` rejection set an empty list, and an empty list renders no table — pixel-for-pixel what a clean project looks like. On a detection surface "I found nothing" and "I could not look" are opposite facts, and the second silently rendering as the first is the whole failure mode this slice exists to prevent. A `misplacedFault` state now carries the reason and a fixed line states it. Separately, HAP-001-R34's disclosure was rendered **inside** a conditional on a status fetch that fails silently to `null`: measured with the status rejecting, the surface showed no discipline line, no disclosures and no banner, while Scan stayed enabled and Quarantine stayed offered — still doing the thing, no longer saying what it does not prevent. The disclosure list is now rendered unconditionally, falling back to the renderer's own advisory pair (the conservative choice: with no report in hand the product cannot claim an enforcement nobody told it about), and the missing report is itself stated.

**The receipt and the workspace pick (R1-008, R3-020).** The reset test never produced a receipt, so its `expect(remedyResultLine()).toBeNull()` was vacuous and deleting the reset left the suite green — meaning a previous project's DELETION receipt could survive under the next project with the suite reporting nothing. The test now runs a real quarantine, watches the receipt say the file is gone, opens a second block with a typed gate, and only then picks. The reachable window behind it was worse: the pick button was not disabled on `remedyBusy`, so "confirm quarantine, then pick another project" was ordinary clicking, and the generation guard then discarded the receipt — correctly, since posting it under the new project would read as a move inside *that* project, but silently, so the user was never told the file had left. **The choice made here is to hold the pick while a remedy is in flight**, rather than to surface the outcome anyway: surfacing it under the wrong project heading is the R1-001 failure the generation guard exists to prevent, so the only answer that is neither wrong nor silent is to keep the two from overlapping. A remedy is one file operation and the pick opens a native dialog, so the wait is short. The consequence is stated rather than hidden: `misplaced_remedy`'s own generation guard is now belt and braces rather than a reachable path, and the two tests that drove it were rewritten as the guarantee that replaced it — including one that reaches the handler's synchronous ref by dispatching both clicks inside a single `act`.

**Guards that could not fire, and tests that could not fail (R3-018, R3-019, R3-022, R3-023, R3-024).** `row.sha256Short !== ''` could be deleted with 322/322 still passing, and a row with an empty short digest made the delete button clickable on an empty input — a gate a blank row satisfies is not a gate. `formatOriginalKept(null)` had no test at all, so mutating it to claim a deletion the shell never made left the suite green. The generation guard was implemented in all four paths and proven in three of eight continuations, with `wrongroot_status` — the fetch that carries `scanned, findings n` — in neither of its two. And the handler-side `runActive` check could not be reached from any click *on the surfaces that read it*, because each of those entry points carries `disabled={runActive || …}` and React does not dispatch `onClick` on a disabled button: **deleting it left the whole suite green**, which is measured, not inferred. Rather than write a contrived test for a dead line, the guard was made live: `handleStart` sets the run active inside a click handler, so a remedy click batched into the same tick sees a stale `false` on the button *and* in the closure, and a synchronously-set `runActiveRef` closes that window. The test that now covers it dispatches Start and a remedy inside one `act` and goes red when the ref is removed. **The generalisation this paragraph originally made — "unreachable from any click, because *every* entry point carries `disabled={runActive || …}`" — was false, and false in the worst place** (R3-033, second review pass below): Start carries that attribute and read no guard at all, so two Start clicks in one tick both spawned. The claim is corrected here rather than left standing, and the paragraph now says only what was measured: the *remedy* entry points could not reach their check, and Start could, which is why Start now reads the ref like the rest.

**Where the honest answer was "this cannot be tested from here" (R3-022).** The four-way unmount `it.each` is vacuous for `wrongroot_status` and `misplaced_list`: those continuations issue no follow-up IPC, so "no IPC call after the unmount" cannot fail for them, and React 19 makes a `setState` on an unmounted component a silent no-op with no warning — measured by deleting `refreshMisplaced`'s `mountedRef` guard, which leaves the suite green. There is nothing observable to assert, so the test now says which parameters it actually proves something for instead of implying coverage it does not have, and the guard that *is* observable — the request-id sequencing — got two out-of-order tests of its own that resolve an overtaken fetch last.

**The smaller findings, applied.** *R1-011*: `stripControlCharacters` did not strip U+2028 and U+2029, both forced line breaks under CSS, so a producer-chosen file name could render as more than one visual line inside the delete confirmation, the extra line indistinguishable from the block's own fixed sentences — the same character class the Rust outbox path began refusing in slice 5c. *R1-012*: stripping is display-only, so two findings differing only by an invisible character rendered identically beside a delete button; a fixed mark now says the displayed name is not the whole name and sends the user to the digest column, without printing the removed characters back onto the screen. *R1-013*: one unconfirmed click suppresses a detection permanently with no listing and no revocation anywhere in the product, and the ignore receipt now says so — the remedy is not destructive to the file, but it is destructive to the detection. *R1-015 / R1-016*: the gate verified `sha256Short` while the request bound `sha256`, with nothing ever comparing them; a row whose short digest is not a prefix of its full digest is now refused rather than confirmed. *R1-014 / R3-025*: `outbox-linked` — HAP-001-R20's hard-link refusal, reachable from **both** remedies — was on none of the three lists that were supposed to enumerate this command's codes; the JSDoc named nine, the wrapper tests asserted seven and the banner tests a different seven, and no two disagreed loudly enough to be noticed. All three now name the same ten, and the banner table asserts its own length. *R3-027*: "a rejection keeps the table and the block" was untested, since every banner case clicked Ignore with no block open; it now opens a block, takes a `quarantine-unavailable` refusal, and asserts the block, its typed gate and the table all survive and the retry actually sends a second request. *R3-028*: per-row gate independence is covered, and the confirmation block is now bound to the row's **name and full digest** rather than its name alone — a run-end refetch arrives with the user doing nothing, and one reporting that name at other bytes used to swap the identity facts under an open confirmation; it now closes instead, since the facts the user read no longer describe anything. *R3-029*: the disclosure content-security test could not tell `PlainTextLine` from React's own escaping, and now uses the ESC and U+202E fixture the row-name test uses. *R3-030*: `setError(null)` on retry is covered. *R3-032*: the earlier R3-017 "fix" moved a `data-testid` onto an `aria-label` on a role-less `div`, which names nothing for a screen reader; the confirmation block is a `<section>` with an accessible name, which was the substance rather than the shape of that fix.

**Totals and evidence after the review pass.** `pnpm -r test` **492 → 537** across seven files: `AgentPanel.test.tsx` 322 → 361, `app.test.tsx` 5 → 6, `PlainTextLine.test.tsx` 11 → 13, the new `PanelErrorBoundary.test.tsx` 3, and `harness.test.ts` 82, `ApprovalSurface.test.tsx` 23 and `HarnessPanel.test.tsx` 49 unchanged in count. `harness.test.ts` holds at 82 because its two wrong-root findings were **reconciliations, not additions**: the detail-token test now walks all nine tokens beside the `originalKept` the shell pairs with each (it had called seven tokens "kept-original" while three of them are not), and the error-code test now walks the ten codes `misplaced_remedy` actually reaches, each asserting its own length so the next omission is a failure rather than a silence. The `renamed-unverified` token was driven from the compile-time red the closed-set guard produced (`src/ipc/harness.test.ts(2522,17): error TS2322: Type '"renamed-unverified"' is not assignable to type 'never'`), which is the same guard that caught the eleventh `AgentEvent` kind at the original landing. `pnpm -r lint`, `pnpm -r build` (`tsc -b && vite build`), three consecutive `CI=true pnpm -r test` runs at 537/537, and ten consecutive `pnpm --filter renderer exec vitest run src/AgentPanel.test.tsx` runs at 361/361 all pass; `rg -n '\.only\(' renderer/src` is empty.

**Residuals this pass names and does not close.** **The count in this paragraph was wrong, and the correction is recorded here rather than quietly applied** (R3-037, second review pass below). It read "of the thirty-seven mutations run, thirty-six are caught and one survives", and that number was offered as the pass's evidence, which makes its accuracy load-bearing. An independent forty-six-mutation set over the same tree found **six** survivors, four of them unnamed anywhere: `remedyButtonName`'s `default:` arm, `openQuarantine`'s synchronous ref read, `handlePickWorkspace`'s synchronous ref read, and `isMisplacedRow`'s `sha256Short` field check — the last of which mattered, since a row missing that field then closed the whole panel through `PlainTextLine text={undefined}`. `runRemedy`'s `setError(null)` was a fifth, recorded as covered when only `handleScan`'s was. The second review pass below closes four of the six, names the two that remain, and re-derives the table from scratch rather than carrying this number forward. What that paragraph got right, and what still holds: dropping `runRemedy`'s `isKnownRemedy` check leaves the suite green, because `renderRemedies` already filters the row's array down to the known set and the only other caller passes a literal, so no click can reach it. It is kept as the check that would still hold if a later caller reached `runRemedy` without going through the cell, and it is recorded here rather than given a test that reaches it by a route no user has. The `mountedRef` guards on `wrongroot_status` and `misplaced_list` remain unprovable from outside the component, for the reason above. `misplaced_remedy`'s generation guard is no longer reachable now that the pick is held, and is kept as defence against a future change to the button's `disabled` binding rather than as a live path. The wire-typed formatters of slices 3, 5, 5b and 5c still have no runtime defaults of their own and rely on the new boundary for containment — **counted and enumerated** in the second pass below rather than left as "and the rest" (R3-050). The same one-render-stale window the `runActiveRef` closes for the wrong-root remedies still exists for other `runActive` guards on this panel, which this pass did not touch; that set is **enumerated** below too, because "the approval and guidance surfaces" was not the whole of it (R3-051). And the boundary is a containment device, not a recovery one: a panel it closes stays closed until the page is reloaded — which the second pass makes the fallback copy say, since the user was previously left to work it out.

#### Second review pass: what a scoped re-review found, and how it was answered

The scoped re-review verified the pass above by mutation and found its own `runActiveRef` change applied to only half its surface. Everything below is verified the same way the pass above was — **each test written or repaired here was checked by mutating the specific behaviour it claims, in a copy of `renderer/` outside the repository, and confirming the suite goes red**. The table is at the end of this subsection; where a mutation still survives it is named there rather than counted as caught.

**The blocking pair, and why they are one finding (R3-033, R3-034).** `handleStart` set `runActiveRef.current = true` and **read no guard at all**. Start carries `disabled={runActive || …}` exactly like every remedy button, and that attribute is one render behind a run that has just started, so two Start clicks dispatched inside one `act` both reached the handler with the button still enabled and **both spawned**: measured, `expected 2 to be 1` on `harness_spawn`. The second spawn then won the generation check, so `setActiveId` never ran for the first — a child process with no active id, no Stop button, no transcript, and no way for this surface to reach it again. That is the window the ref was introduced for, on the entry point whose second click costs the most, and the paragraph above claiming the handler-side check was "unreachable from any click" has been corrected rather than left standing. Start now reads the ref first.

The second half is what the freeze was made of. `runActive` is `isSpawning || activeId !== null`, `isSpawning` was a boolean, and the mirroring effect follows it: a spawn whose generation a later Start had already replaced still cleared that boolean in its own `finally`, the ref followed it down, and every remedy came back live while a process was running and another spawn was still out — a probe sent `misplaced_remedy` in exactly that window. The flag needed a *count*, because "no spawn is pending" and "the last spawn to settle has settled" are the same fact only while at most one exists. `pendingSpawns` is now a counter and `isSpawning` is derived from it. The two are tested together and separately: two Start clicks in one `act` yield one spawn and `harness_stop` reaches that same id (red without the guard), and a remedy click after a settled spawn is refused while another is pending (red with **both** halves reverted, `expected 1 to be +0` on `misplaced_remedy`). The counter alone is belt and braces once the guard is in and is named as such in the table.

**A structural check that only checked two fields (R3-035).** `isMisplacedRow` exists to check a listing row field by field, and its fixture proved `name` and `remedies` only: each of the other seven checks could be deleted with the suite green. The `sha256Short` one mattered most — the cell renders it through `PlainTextLine`, which calls `Array.from(text)`, so a row without it threw mid-render and closed the panel. There is now one row per field, each carrying a value of the wrong type for that field alone, plus one for a `remedies` array whose elements are not tokens; all nine checks go red when dropped (`expected [ 'build/bundle.zip', …(1) ] to deeply equal [ 'docs/report.pdf' ]`, and for `sha256Short` the original failure itself, `TypeError: undefined is not iterable`).

**A banner that could stand over a receipt (R3-036).** R3-030 was recorded as covered and was covered for `handleScan` alone; `runRemedy`'s own `setError(null)` could be deleted with the suite green, leaving a `quarantine-unavailable` refusal above a receipt saying a copy had reached the outbox. A test now takes the refusal, then runs a Publish that succeeds, and asserts the banner is gone (`expected <div role="alert" …(1)>…(2)</div> to be null` without the line).

**A live frame read without a check (R3-049).** The listing rows got a structural check in the pass above; the transcript's `state` payload did not, and it is the one reachable from a **live frame** rather than from a fetch. `formatStateEventLine` called `payload.observations.map` at render, so `observations: null` threw `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'map')` inside the transcript — which closes the Agent panel entire. Observations are now checked as a list and element by element, unreadable ones are dropped, and the drop is stated (`some observations could not be read and are not shown`), the same discipline `isMisplacedRow` applies to a row. A partly readable frame keeps what it can read and says the rest could not be read; a fully readable one says nothing, so the notice never becomes furniture.

**The boundary says three things it did not say (R3-038, R3-039, R3-040).** It could close a panel that owns live work: a mid-run throw takes Stop with the panel, `harness_stop` is never called, the process keeps running, and "the other panels on this page are unaffected" is true of the page and false of the machine. It never resets — `failed` does not clear and no `key` above it changes — so a closed panel stays closed for the session even when good data arrives, which around the approval surface is the session's only revocation control gone, and the fallback gave the user no instruction at all. And it named `Harness` and `Approvals` while the panels are labelled **`Demo harness`** and **`Executable approval`**, so the one sentence whose whole job is to say which surface closed named a surface the user could not find, for two of the three. The copy now names the live work and the reload; `App` passes each panel's own name; and the fallback region keeps that name rather than becoming `<name> unavailable`, so the landmark a user navigates by does not move when the panel closes. One bad `approvals_list` closes all three panels at once, which is what makes the three names assertable together, and `app.test.tsx` asserts exactly that. A reset is still not offered, for the reason the pass above gave — a panel whose render just threw has state the boundary knows nothing about — so the fallback names the recovery that does work instead of implying one it cannot deliver.

**A wait with no deadline and no message (R3-042).** `misplacedRemedy` is a bare invoke, so a shell that never settles froze the pick and every remedy for the session with nothing said. `harnessStop(id, STOP_DEADLINE_MS)` is the in-repo precedent, and it is **not available here**: `harness_stop` takes its deadline as a parameter and the shell enforces it, while `misplaced_remedy` takes `{ name, sha256, remedy }` and nothing else. A renderer-side deadline could therefore only *abandon* the wait, which is the wrong answer on this command — the remedy holds the publication surface lock and may be part-way through moving a file out of the project, so releasing the freeze on a timer would put a second delete button in front of the user while the first delete is still running and report an outcome nobody observed. **The deadline added is a notice deadline, not a cancellation one**: after ten seconds a fixed line says the remedy has not answered, that it may still be running, that nothing here can say whether the file has moved, and that the findings stay frozen until it answers. The freeze is unchanged; the silence is not. The line is taken back the moment the remedy settles.

**The smaller findings, applied.** *R3-041*: `MisplacedRemedy`'s JSDoc still said `originalKept` was "`false` only for a quarantine that completed the move" — the claim R1-004 removed from the UI, contradicted by `RemedyDetail` twenty lines above (`renamed-unverified` carries `false`) and by `formatOriginalKept` below it. It now says what the flag records: whether *this remedy removed the original*, and that `true` is not a report that the file is still in the project. *R3-047*: the `dropped` fault rendered under `aria-label="Misplaced files unavailable"` while the table was on the screen with rows in it, telling a screen reader the findings were unavailable beside the findings; the two faults now carry two names, `Misplaced files unavailable` and `Misplaced files incomplete`. *R3-052*: `key={remedy}` over a wire array gave two children the same key for a row listing a token twice — reported on `console.error`, which this suite treats as a failure — and the cell now keys by position, the table's own rationale. *R3-045*: a test written in the pass above reached for `getByTestId('agent-scope-badge')`, the very thing R3-017 and R3-032 took off this surface; the hook is gone from the markup and both call sites query the badge's own fixed text. *R3-046*: every `vi.spyOn(console, 'error')` here is restored on the last line of its test body, which is exactly the line a failing assertion above it never reaches — so one red test silenced `console.error` for every test after it in the same file, disarming the console-error-as-failure signal. `restoreMocks: true` in `vite.config.ts` makes that the harness's job; measured out of repo with a deliberately failing spy-and-leak test, whose successor asserts `vi.isMockFunction(console.error)` is `false` (red, `expected true to be false`, without the setting). *R3-048*: the boundary test's `querySelector('b')` assertion was vacuous over an error message that contained no markup; the fixture now throws `<b>boom</b> at <i>docs/report.pdf</i>`, and two properties worth pinning were added — a throw from a descendant several levels below the boundary's own child, and that the boundary does **not** catch an event-handler error, which React never routes to one and which is the boundary's most likely misunderstanding on a surface whose every remedy acts from an `onClick`. *R3-044*: `remedyButtonName`'s `default:` arm is dead by construction, because `renderRemedies` filters through `isKnownRemedy` before a button exists to be named; it is kept and **named as dead** rather than counted as covered.

**The mutation table.** Thirty-six mutations, each applied one at a time to a copy of `renderer/` outside the repository, each followed by a full `vitest run src/`.

| # | Mutation | Result |
|---|---|---|
| M01 | `handleStart` drops its run-active guard (R3-033) | caught |
| M02 | `pendingSpawns` reverts to a boolean, so any settle clears it (R3-034) | **survives** — unreachable with M01's guard in place; see M03 |
| M03 | M01 **and** M02 together — the combination the two findings describe | caught (`expected 1 to be +0` on `misplaced_remedy`) |
| M04 | `handleStart` stops raising `runActiveRef` synchronously | caught |
| M05 | the `[runActive]` effect stops writing the ref | caught |
| M06–M12 | `isMisplacedRow` drops the `name` / `size` / `sha256` / `sha256Short` / `detectedType` / `class` / `reason` check (R3-035) | caught (seven) |
| M13 | `isMisplacedRow` drops the `remedies`-is-a-list check | caught |
| M14 | `isMisplacedRow` drops the `remedies`-element check | caught |
| M15 | a dropped row is never announced | caught |
| M16 | `runRemedy` drops its `setError(null)` (R3-036) | caught |
| M17 | the unanswered-remedy notice never fires (R3-042) | caught |
| M18 | the unanswered-remedy notice is never taken back | caught |
| M19 | both listing faults share one accessible name again (R3-047) | caught |
| M20 | `formatStateEventLine` reads `observations` unchecked again (R3-049) | caught |

| M22 | `isObservation` drops the `key` check | caught |
| M23 | `isObservation` drops the `value` check | caught |

| M25 | `App` names the harness boundary `Harness` again (R3-040) | caught |
| M26 | `App` names the approval boundary `Approvals` again | caught |
| M27 | the fallback region renames itself `<name> unavailable` | caught |
| M28 | the fallback stops naming the live work it left running (R3-038) | caught |
| M29 | the fallback stops telling the user what to do (R3-039) | caught |
| M30 | the remedy cell keys by token again (R3-052) | caught |
| M31 | `openQuarantine` drops its synchronous ref read (R3-037) | caught |
| M32 | `handlePickWorkspace` drops its synchronous ref read (R3-037) | caught |
| M33 | `runRemedy` drops its synchronous ref read | caught |
| M34 | `remedyButtonName` drops its unreachable `default:` arm (R3-044) | **survives** — dead by construction, named |
