`restoreMocks` (R3-046) is a harness setting rather than a source line, so it was measured the same way but with its own probe outside the repository: a test that spies on `console.error` and fails before its own restore line, followed by one asserting `vi.isMockFunction(console.error)` is `false` — red (`expected true to be false`) without the setting, green with it. R3-041 is a JSDoc correction with no behaviour of its own; what it was contradicting is already pinned by `harness.test.ts`'s detail-token walk.

**Residuals this pass names and does not close.** Of the **thirty-six** mutations run over the final tree, **thirty-three are caught and three survive**, and all three are structurally unreachable rather than untested: (1) reverting `pendingSpawns` to a boolean, which no click can reach now that `handleStart` refuses a second spawn — the combination of that revert *with* the guard removed **is** caught, which is the property the pair was fixed for; (2) `remedyButtonName`'s `default:` arm; and (3) `runRemedy`'s `isKnownRemedy` check, carried from the pass above. Four of the six survivors that re-review found are closed here: `isMisplacedRow`'s `sha256Short`, `runRemedy`'s `setError(null)`, and the synchronous ref reads in `openQuarantine` and `handlePickWorkspace`, the last two by dispatching Start beside each of them inside one `act`. **The wire-typed switches without a runtime default are thirteen, not "and the rest"** (R3-050), counted over this tree: `formatTerminalActionLabel`, `formatOutboxReason`, `formatOutboxStatusLine`, `formatWorkAreaLine`, `formatAttribution`, `formatCandidateState`, `formatArtifactState`, `formatProviderState`, `formatAvailability`, `formatAttributionFact`, `formatManagedBlockState`, `formatGuidanceAction` and `guidanceBlockLabel`; two more (`guidanceWriteButtonName`, `invokeGuidanceWrite`) switch on a value the renderer itself constructs. Each returns `undefined` for a token its union does not name, and each is contained by the boundary at the granularity of the whole Agent panel — see the blocking finding above for what that costs. **The one-render-stale `runActive` read is ten handlers, not two** (R3-051), also counted here: `handleListOutbox`; the four guidance handlers `handleGuidancePreview`, `openGuidanceRemove`, `openGuidanceRestore` and `handleGuidanceWrite`; the three approval and publication handlers `openApproval`, `handleConfirmApproval` and `handlePublish`; and the adapter and approval `<select>` `onChange` handlers. All ten read the render value with no synchronous ref beneath it, so all ten still have the same-tick window the wrong-root entry points no longer have. This pass did not touch them, for the same reason the first did not: they are a different slice's surfaces, and closing the window there is a change to those surfaces' own guards. The `mountedRef` guards on `wrongroot_status` and `misplaced_list` remain unprovable from outside the component, `misplaced_remedy`'s generation guard remains belt and braces now that the pick is held, and the boundary remains a containment device with no recovery — now stated in its own copy rather than only here.

**Totals and evidence after the second review pass.** `pnpm -r test` **537 → 565** across seven files: `AgentPanel.test.tsx` 361 → 384, `app.test.tsx` 6 → 7, `PanelErrorBoundary.test.tsx` 3 → 7, and `harness.test.ts` 82, `ApprovalSurface.test.tsx` 23, `HarnessPanel.test.tsx` 49 and `PlainTextLine.test.tsx` 13 unchanged. `harness.test.ts` holds at 82 because this pass's only change there was a JSDoc correction (R3-041), which the detail-token test already pinned the behaviour of. `pnpm -r lint`, `pnpm -r build` (`tsc -b && vite build`), three consecutive `CI=true pnpm -r test` runs at 565/565, and ten consecutive `pnpm --filter renderer exec vitest run src/AgentPanel.test.tsx` runs at 384/384 all pass; `rg -n '\.only\(' renderer/src` is empty; the provenance sweep over `renderer/` and `docs/` is empty. Everything here stays **dev-mode-only** exactly as before: every frame, finding and remedy above came from a mocked `invoke`, and no packaged build or live shell was exercised by any of it.

### Relationship to VP-001

Every result behind this slice — every passing test listed above; `cargo fmt --all --check` clean; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` (Linux) clean; `cargo clippy -p omnifrons-supervisor -p omnifrons-adapters -p omnifrons-app -p omnifrons-domain --all-targets --all-features --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu -- -D warnings` clean (cross-compiled, never executed; the shell stays outside that check for the `windres` reason slice 3 recorded, and its one `#[cfg(not(unix))]` body was lint-checked by temporarily flipping the gate to `#[cfg(unix)]` and restoring it, the technique slice 5c introduced); `cargo test --workspace --all-targets --all-features` all green at **723** tests, summed over every test binary's own `passed` count the way slices 3 to 5c were (domain 96, app 208, adapters 152, supervisor 70, shell 197; +91 over slice 5c's 632: domain +6, app +26, adapters +27, supervisor +1, shell +31 — of which +33 arrived with the first review-fix pass, below the first count of 681 (domain +1, app +12, adapters +8, supervisor 0, shell +12), +4 with the second, below 714 (app +1, adapters +3: the handle-relative listing R1-013, the per-directory listing bound R1-015, the outbox entry refused from its own handle R1-016, and the quarantine destination created exclusively R1-017), +3 with the third, below 718 (adapters +2, app +1: the handle-anchored branch refusing a pre-existing destination and reusing one that carries these bytes, R1-021, and the quarantine destination that answers to a second name, R1-022), and +2 with the fourth, below 721 (adapters +1, shell +1: the source name swapped *before* the `linkat`, which is not a move and is no longer reported as one, R1-028, and that same second-name refusal reached through `misplaced_remedy` rather than only through its mapping, R1-030)). **Those are Linux counts. The Windows totals are domain 96, app 194, adapters 125, supervisor 54, shell 189 — 658 in all**, recounted test by test against this tree rather than carried forward, because three of the five were wrong before. The gates each crate carries, and the arithmetic they give: `omnifrons-domain` carries none at all, so 96 either way. `omnifrons-app` has 16 `#[cfg(unix)]` whole tests against 2 `#[cfg(not(unix))]` counterparts (the link-count refusal a platform without a by-handle link count cannot make, now at the outbox entry and at the quarantine destination alike), so 208 − 16 + 2 = 194. `omnifrons-adapters` has 31 `#[cfg(unix)]`, one more that is `#[cfg(unix)]` *and* `#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]`, and one `#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]` alone — 33 that do not run on Windows — against 4 `#[cfg(not(unix))]` and 2 `#[cfg(windows)]`, the last two being `tests/fs_prober.rs`'s whole `#[cfg(windows)] mod`, which the earlier count of 130 missed along with ten of the unix gates: 152 − 33 + 6 = 125. `omnifrons-supervisor` has 16 unix-only tests and 3 `#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]` against 3 `#[cfg(windows)]`, so 70 − 19 + 3 = 54; the earlier 57 counted neither the two files carrying a file-level `#![cfg(unix)]` (`tests/demo_harness.rs`, `tests/pty_running_cap.rs`) nor the four tests inside `src/lib.rs`'s own `#[cfg(unix)] mod` nor the three Windows-only tests. And `src-tauri/src` has 9 `#[cfg(unix)]` against 1 `#[cfg(not(unix))]` — three more `#[cfg(unix)]` than slice 5c enumerated, all added here (the work-area-before-the-walk ordering and the unlistable project root, each of which needs a permission bit unix expresses and Windows does not, and the quarantine destination that answers to a second name, which needs the by-handle link count Windows does not offer) — so 197 − 9 + 1 = 189, with the shell's three integration-test binaries still carrying no platform gate at all. One test is in **neither** total: `tests/fs_prober.rs`'s handle-identity check sits in the `#[cfg(unix)] mod` under a further `#[cfg(not(target_os = "linux"))]`, so it runs on macOS alone. The five `#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]` tests do not run on macOS either; one of them is this slice's descriptor-count probe, which needs `/proc/self/fd`. **The review-fix pass's counts were taken after every finding it addressed**, not before. `cargo test -p omnifrons-shell --test feature_gate` green (2 passed); `cargo tree -p omnifrons-adapters -e normal --depth 1` still exactly `nix`, `omnifrons-app`, `omnifrons-domain`, `serde`, `serde_json`, `sha2`, and the supervisor's still exactly `nix`, `omnifrons-app`, `omnifrons-domain`, `tokio`, `tracing`. **The dependency graph is unchanged; one `nix` feature is not** (R1-013): `omnifrons-adapters` now enables `dir` beside `fs` and `user`, for `nix::dir::Dir`'s `fdopendir`. The whole feature situation, stated rather than implied — `omnifrons-adapters` `["dir", "fs", "user"]`, `omnifrons-supervisor` `["signal", "fs", "term"]` with `["resource"]` in its unix dev-dependencies, resolving workspace-wide to `dir`, `feature`, `fs`, `process`, `resource`, `signal`, `term`, `user` (`cargo tree -e features`; `feature` and `process` are transitive, pulled by `user` and `signal`). `dir = ["fs"]` in the vendored 0.31.3 `[features]` table, verified there rather than assumed, so it adds no crate and leaves both trees above byte-identical; `tests/deps.rs` asserts the dependency *tables*, which a feature does not touch, and it is unchanged. No new `unsafe` (the two precedents, `demo.rs` and `pty.rs`, are the only sites); the provenance sweep empty — was produced against a `cargo test` development build, on one contributor's Linux machine, not a packaged release build on a pinned Windows/macOS/Linux baseline, and not against macOS or Windows at all: the Windows clippy pass is a cross-compilation type-check — it does cover every `#[cfg(not(unix))]` body these passes added or rewrote, in `omnifrons-adapters` and in `omnifrons-app` alike (confirmed for each by planting a deliberate type error inside it, watching the Windows-target clippy fail on that line, and removing it: the rewritten path-based listing in `fs_wrong_root_scanner`, R1-024, and the new `not(unix)` counterpart for the quarantine destination that answers to a second name, R1-022) — and the unix-gated tests have not run on macOS here (CI's `macos-latest` leg is where they do). Per [VP-001-R2](desktop-stack-verification-plan.md#product-requirements), this is `dev-mode-only` and therefore **not** admissible as VP-S23 evidence; it is an input to writing that scenario's procedure well ([Evidence procedures](#evidence-procedures-5)), and, for the wrong-root scan specifically, to deciding what "a tracked path" is checked against before the scenario runs.
